# Decisions

## 2026-07-11 - Finished-Goods Photo Review Owns Its Todo Transaction

A finished-goods photo is production-task state, not an in-memory UI annotation. Upload persists the photo snapshot and audit log atomically. Review persists the task state, the customer-notification or retake todo, any automatically resolved prior retake todo, full todo events, and the operation log as one idempotent transaction. The workflow never creates inventory, reservations, or packing output.

Photo state is stored as JSONB with a production-task revision guard so attachment, review, and history remain one coherent snapshot. Both startup and production-task read repositories must expose it. Authenticated identity is the upload/review audit identity; body operator fields are deprecated compatibility input. Production must use the PostgreSQL photo transaction repository.

## 2026-07-11 - Inventory Correction Is One Auditable Transaction

Creating an inventory correction persists the review draft, related todo, full todo event, and operation log atomically without changing stock. Confirming it updates the inventory item and correction draft while inserting the inventory ledger, resolving the todo, appending its event, and recording the operation log in one idempotent transaction. A partial success must never be projected as a valid correction.

Correction writes use authenticated session identity, not body `operatorId`. Production serializes work by inventory item, correction draft, and todo and checks inventory/draft revisions before commit. The correction repository is mandatory in the production PostgreSQL profile; local memory remains demo/test only. Inventory reservation release follows the same authenticated-operator rule even though it uses its existing transaction repository.

## 2026-07-11 - Trusted Print Projection Is An Independent Service Boundary

The mapping from print-job status to print-record and fulfillment state belongs in a dedicated business service, not in the HTTP route composition entrypoint. Routes authenticate, authorize, validate, and invoke the service; the service owns the transition decision and persists through the existing fulfillment action transaction repository. Its fulfillment-record and operation-log builders remain explicit injected dependencies so the service is directly testable without booting the full API server.

This boundary does not change the physical-print trust rule: `queued` and `sent` may update a print record but never advance fulfillment, while only trusted `printed` may set physical print confirmation and move eligible express/LTL work to `待确认拉走`. Preview-only work remains non-advancing. Future dispatch, callback, polling, and retry extraction must call this service instead of duplicating transition rules.

Print-job status updates, dispatch, callback/polling, terminal-state validation, and retry use one lifecycle service with transport-neutral result objects. HTTP routes map those outcomes to status codes but do not own transition rules. The authenticated print-driver service account remains the audit operator; a driver payload's free-form operator value is retained only as reported metadata and cannot replace authenticated identity.

Fulfillment print commands own first-print/reprint validation, device resolution, print-record persistence, template generation, print-job creation, and voiding as one service boundary. In production, a print record without a supported persistent transaction target must fail closed; process-memory mutation is allowed only in demo/test compatibility paths and must never be reported as a production success.

## 2026-07-11 - V1 Status Separates Transport, Normalization, And Presentation

The V1 status feature keeps three explicit layers: HTTP actions issue authenticated/idempotent requests and fail closed, normalizers map backend artifacts into stable client DTOs, and presentation modules own fallback display models plus rendering. The existing public client import path remains compatible, but the normalization file must not issue HTTP requests directly and the page component must not redefine fallback completion data.

An HTTP 2xx response does not imply a V1 action succeeded: actions configured as readiness-gated remain `blocked` unless their normalized result has `ready === true`. Missing backend snapshots never display fixed release-gate, evidence, or signoff numbers. Management and technical-operations retain V1 access; office remains denied by the shared role catalog.

## 2026-07-11 - Filtered Workbenches Clear Out-Of-Scope Detail

When a search, status tab, delivery method, or other workbench filter has no matching record, both the list and detail pane must show an explicit empty state. The UI must not retain or fall back to a record from another filter, tab, or status because that can make an operator act on the wrong customer, material inbound, fulfillment, or driver task.

Derived summaries and optional device-integration helpers must accept a null selection. A no-result state is a normal product state, not an exceptional condition; it must not dereference task fields or blank the page. Direct regression checks should cover at least one empty filtered state for financial, inventory/material, fulfillment, and mobile-role workflows.

## 2026-07-11 - Fulfillment Advances Only After Trusted Printed Status

Creating a print request, rendering a browser preview, queueing a job, or receiving `sent` from a driver proves only that ERP or the print subsystem accepted work. These states must not mark a fulfillment as physically printed or move express/LTL fulfillment to `待确认拉走`. A fulfillment print record starts as `previewed`, `submitted`, or `reprint_submitted`; only a trusted driver callback or spool poll reporting `printed` may change it to `printed` / `reprinted` and advance the fulfillment projection. Express/LTL pickup confirmation must fail closed before that transition.

Manual batch-result confirmation records operator reconciliation of labels and todo/package results. It must not create print jobs after the fact, substitute for spool state, or advance fulfillment. Setting a device to `system_printer` means only that it is a real-print candidate; command bridge, spool readback, CUPS, physical output, barcode scanning, field QA, evidence, and signoff remain separate gates.

## 2026-07-11 - Browser Business Writes Require The Idempotency Header In CORS

Every browser business POST/PATCH already carries `Idempotency-Key`; API CORS policy must therefore allow `idempotency-key` in both prototype and strict modes or the browser will block the request before server authorization and transaction handling. Strict mode allows only `content-type`, `authorization`, and `idempotency-key`. Prototype mode may additionally allow `x-erp-user-id` and `x-erp-action-permissions`; these prototype identity headers remain forbidden by strict authentication.

Allowing the header does not weaken origin, identity, permission, or database idempotency enforcement. Strict mode still requires an approved origin and formal bearer session, while the server still validates the key and persists idempotent results through the configured repository. Direct CORS tests and a real cross-origin browser write must remain part of the regression evidence.

## 2026-07-11 - Evidence Attachments Use Content-Addressed Idempotent Storage

Payment proofs and other evidence uploads are deduplicated by `ownerType + ownerId + purpose + SHA-256 contentDigest`. Storage keys are content-addressed as `sha256/<prefix>/<digest>` under the configured namespace, so retries and same-content uploads do not create extra objects. Production uploads require database-backed idempotency; the same key and payload replay one result, while a changed payload returns `409`. Each first create or later digest reuse writes an operation log.

Legacy duplicate metadata is not deleted because it may be referenced by audit history. The migration records one canonical digest key and business attachment lists suppress noncanonical duplicates; direct ID reads and historical audit records remain available. PostgreSQL advisory locks serialize concurrent uploads of the same owner, purpose, and digest.

## 2026-07-11 - Statement Summaries Separate Current And Historical Debt

The statement workbench displays current-period receivable, current-period received, current-period unpaid, historical debt, and cumulative debt as separate values. Current-period unpaid is `max(receivable - received, 0)`. Historical debt comes from the customer debt snapshot. Cumulative debt is current-period unpaid plus historical debt. Overpayment does not make unpaid negative, and a statement variance field must not replace or merge these five display amounts.

Existing payment, variance, settlement, and write-off state machines keep their server-authoritative blocking rules. This decision changes financial presentation and removes an ambiguous combined debt display; it does not itself post payments, carry debt, or write off a statement.

## 2026-07-11 - Production Releases Use Immutable Commits And Atomic Symlink Switching

Production deployment must target a full 40-character Git commit in a new release directory. Recovery evidence must include env-file audit, clone, detached checkout, commit verification, locked dependency installation, production frontend build, migration dry-run, deployment-manifest verification, and production runtime smoke. Reports may include an abbreviated commit but must not include the repository URL, checkout path, env-file path, command output, or secret values. Failed recovery directories are retained for review and are never automatically deleted or overwritten.

The active version is `/opt/erp/current`, atomically switched to a verified `/opt/erp/releases/<commit>` directory. systemd sends SIGTERM; the API stops accepting work, drains HTTP requests, and closes shared PostgreSQL pools before exit. Application rollback switches to a previously verified release and does not automatically execute a database down migration. Real remote configuration, another-host recovery, backup/restore, monitoring alerts, and rollback rehearsal remain required evidence rather than inferred from local tests.

## 2026-07-11 - Order Confirmation Business-ID Conflicts Fail Closed

Formal order confirmation must never use `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` to overwrite an existing order, order line, price snapshot, fulfillment, reservation, inventory ledger, todo, or audit log. Idempotent replay is handled by the operation idempotency record; a new request that collides with an existing business ID must return a business-write conflict and roll back the draft transition plus every related insert and inventory change.

Readable IDs may still be allocated from the highest matching sequence, but database uniqueness and transaction write-count guards remain authoritative. Concurrent confirmations for different drafts may therefore require one operator to refresh and retry; they must never merge or overwrite the other order.

## 2026-07-11 - Production Core Workspace Starts From PostgreSQL Only

In production mode, the core workspace startup snapshot must replace the seed collections for customers, orders, order lines, pricing, inventory, reservations, ledgers, fulfillment, statements, todos, production, packing, and audit logs with PostgreSQL results. An empty production database means an empty business workspace; production startup must not recreate demo print jobs or retain seed customers, orders, inventory, fulfillment, or statements.

Specialized repositories may load additional domain collections after the core snapshot, but they must not restore demo data or overwrite PostgreSQL truth with local defaults. Business writes update the in-process projection only from committed repository results. This decision does not prove that real production PostgreSQL, object storage, backup/restore, printers, phones, field evidence, or owner signoff is complete.

## 2026-07-11 - Order Drafts Are Revisioned PostgreSQL Records

Production order recognition, draft save, and blocked confirmation must persist the draft, replacement lines, related todo records, and audit log before the in-process workspace projection changes. Draft save and confirmation require a positive client revision; missing drafts return 404 and stale revisions return 409. PostgreSQL is authoritative across API instances, so the route reads the current draft through the repository rather than trusting the process-local array. Recognition may derive a deterministic draft ID from the production idempotency key.

Formal order confirmation must ultimately update the source draft in the same database transaction as the formal order, lines, inventory reservations, inventory ledger entries, todos, and audit log. The current R1.1-R1.2 two-transaction confirmation bridge is temporary and remains an explicit B3 blocker until R1.3 removes it.

## 2026-07-11 - Production Business Writes Require Database-Backed Idempotency And Revisions

Every production business POST/PATCH request must carry a valid `Idempotency-Key`. The backend stores a canonical request hash and committed JSON response in PostgreSQL; the same key and payload replay the original response, while the same key with a different payload returns `409`. Internal server jobs without an HTTP request derive a deterministic key from their operation-log ID. High-risk resources use sorted PostgreSQL advisory transaction locks plus row locks or integer `revision` checks. Inventory shortages and stale writes must fail explicitly and roll back; arithmetic clamping must not hide over-deduction. Browser/workspace projections must use committed database records and revisions rather than the attempted input. Idempotency records may retain hashes, target metadata, operator IDs, responses, and expiry, but not raw sensitive request payloads.

## 2026-07-10 - Runtime Mode Must Be Explicit And Local Data Must Be Partitioned

The API supports exactly `demo`, `test`, and `production` runtime modes. Default local data belongs under a mode-specific partition; an explicitly supplied test storage root remains authoritative for isolated harnesses. The reset command may remove only the demo partition and must reject test or production targets. Runtime health summaries may expose mode and partition labels, but not local paths.

Production mode is not a label-only setting. It forces strict identity and production persistence checks and cannot be relaxed by constructor options. Production may not continue with local memory, local JSON, or local filesystem business persistence.

## 2026-07-10 - Production Startup Must Validate Actual Persistence Implementations

Production startup must force the V1 PostgreSQL and object-storage profiles and then inspect the repositories and storage adapters actually constructed. Missing database configuration, unconfigured object storage, or any local implementation causes startup to fail with `ERP_PRODUCTION_PERSISTENCE_REQUIRED`. Environment-variable presence alone is not sufficient proof.

This startup gate does not prove that real PostgreSQL, backup/restore, object-storage governance, printers, driver devices, or onsite evidence have passed. Those remain separate release gates.

## 2026-07-10 - Role Permissions Must Have One Shared Catalog

Frontend prototype presentation and server authorization must derive role defaults from `shared/auth/roleCatalog.js`. Office users do not receive any `system.v1_*` action. System V1 production configuration, persistence evidence, field-evidence mutation, and release-candidate actions belong only to management or technical-operations roles unless a later audited permission design explicitly changes them.

Production browser sessions must use formal runtime login. Prototype seed login, legacy identity headers, browser-local permission fallback, and local business-write fallback remain demo/test-only behavior.

## 2026-07-10 - Production Env Audit Artifacts Must Not Expose File Paths

Production env file audit runners may use the operator-supplied path internally to read the file and evaluate workspace, Git, template, and permission rules. Machine-readable reports, text output, findings, and errors must expose only deterministic ordered labels such as `env 文件 1`; they must include an explicit `envFilePathExposed=false` safeguard and must not retain an absolute path, workspace-relative path, basename, or storage-root fragment.

Subprocess tests that pass `--env-file` must invoke Node with the `--` option separator because Node 24 otherwise consumes the application flag before the script starts. Direct checks must cover successful, blocked, warning, multi-file, and missing-file outputs for both value and path redaction. Downstream release artifacts may relabel an already-redacted source, but they must not depend on a raw path being present.

## 2026-07-10 - Raw-Material Inbound List Rules Must Stay Pure

Raw-material list metrics, review/source labels, delivery-note fallback text, list filtering, visual tones, and the review / label-print / label-attachment eligibility rules belong in `src/domain/rawMaterialInboundListState.js` when they do not require React lifecycle, browser APIs, or network access. The office page remains responsible for rendering, user interaction, permissions, API calls, machine-side issue, and cost workflows.

Direct checks must cover the status transitions relevant to page actions. A displayed eligible state is never permission or inventory authorization: the server remains authoritative for review, label print, attachment confirmation, and the later available-inventory transition.

## 2026-07-10 - Frontend Master-Data Projections Must Stay Pure

Master-data import execution and review-draft updates, failed-row normalization and correction keys, visual status mapping, committed-plan detection, employee-account review projection, password labels, and revocation eligibility belong in `src/state/officeMasterDataState.js` when they do not require React lifecycle, browser APIs, or network access. The page and modal remain responsible for user interaction, authorization, and service orchestration.

Each new extracted projection must have a direct Node check in the core suite. The frontend may present a review state but must never treat it as authorization for a formal import, employee-account enablement, password issue, or rollback; those remain server-authoritative operations.

## 2026-07-10 - Frontend Print Projections Must Stay Pure

Printer-device QA initialization, print-job queue initialization and deterministic merge/sort, print-driver diagnostic initialization, printer display labels, and print-job status labels belong in `src/state/officePrintState.js` when they do not require React lifecycle, browser APIs, or network access. `App.jsx` remains responsible for state ownership, UI events, authorization, and service orchestration.

Each new extracted projection must have a direct Node check in the core suite. This boundary must not weaken server-authoritative printing: queue state and presentation labels never mean a print action, a spool readback, or physical printer QA succeeded.

## 2026-07-10 - Frontend Production And Packing Projections Must Stay Pure

Production and packing page-state calculations belong in `src/state/officeProductionPackingState.js` when they do not require React lifecycle, browser APIs, or network access. This includes local packing-task initialization, workshop task queries, API list mapping, deterministic task upserts, packing-to-fulfillment projection, print-record projection, and print-todo matching. `App.jsx` remains responsible for state ownership, rendering, event handlers, and service orchestration only.

Each new extracted projection must have a direct Node check and be added to the core test chain. Extracting a pure projection must preserve production, inventory, packing, and print business rules; it must not create a browser-local success fallback for server-authoritative writes.

## 2026-07-10 - Git Baseline Must Exclude Runtime Evidence And Real Configuration

The repository baseline was reviewed in four groups (runtime/data, frontend domain code, documentation, and CI) and committed locally as `72f25c4`. `.erp-local-storage/`, screenshots, build output, dependencies, and real environment files must remain ignored and must not enter the index through later blanket adds. A local commit is not a release, an off-machine backup, or a production-validation claim; remote recovery remains incomplete until a controlled Git remote is configured and the reviewed baseline is pushed.

New source, migration, test, documentation, and CI files belong in the baseline unless they contain real credentials or runtime evidence. Any exception must be explicit and documented rather than silently left untracked.

## 2026-07-10 - Strict Authentication Cannot Be Relaxed By Server Options

When strict authentication is enabled, seed users, legacy identity headers, action-permission overrides, and the default seed identity are always disabled. Constructor options may make local prototype behavior more restrictive, but they may not re-enable any prototype identity source under strict authentication. The only public strict routes are health and standard login; prototype login requires a session and cannot issue a seed session.

Runtime-user sessions remain valid only after signature and runtime-account checks. This rule does not replace production identity-provider integration, secret rotation, account provisioning, or deployment-specific CORS allowlisting.

## 2026-07-10 - V1 Dry-Run Proof Calculations Must Stay Pure And Redacted

Minimum-fill normalization, target signatures, age-window parsing, dry-run freshness, proof-status derivation, file-binding status mapping, user-facing status labels, next actions, and redaction safeguards belong in `server/v1ProductionEnvDryRunProofCore.mjs` as pure calculations. The core may accept injected report readers, already-redacted file-binding results, and execution-normalization results, but it must not read environment files itself, run commands, mutate workspace or business state, or return raw production values, paths, connection strings, object-storage endpoints, credentials, tokens, spool locations, command values, fingerprint digests, or raw reports.

Callers remain responsible for controlled file access, proof execution, authorization, redaction, and release-gate decisions. A passing calculation proves only the supplied summary is internally consistent; it never proves real production configuration, PostgreSQL, object storage, printing, driver devices, field evidence, or signoff.

## 2026-07-10 - V1 Go-Live Status Must Never Infer Current Gates From Static Data

The office V1 status page may display a backend go-live snapshot only after a successful API read. When no successful snapshot exists, it must present `状态未读取` and must not display static completion, release-gate, field-evidence, or signoff numbers as current facts. A retained successful snapshot must carry its successful-read timestamp; after 15 minutes it must be visibly marked expired. A transient API failure may show the labeled recent snapshot, but may not silently substitute constants.

This UI safeguard does not change V1 release gates or prove that production persistence, object storage, physical printing, driver devices, field evidence, or signoff is complete.

## 2026-07-10 - System Write Dispatch Must Use A Context-Restricted Allowlist

System V1 POST dispatch belongs in `server/routes/systemWriteRoutes.mjs` and must use an explicit route allowlist. Each entry declares its existing permission, handler, and one permitted input context: operator only, request body, HTTP request, or workspace. New system actions must not receive a generic raw context object or browser-supplied filesystem, command, environment, token, or secret values merely because another action needs them.

This is a routing-boundary rule only. It does not authorize production execution, relax an underlying handler's safeguards, or substitute for production environment validation, field evidence, or owner signoff.

## 2026-07-10 - API Security Policy Must Stay Outside Business Routes

Strict-auth policy construction, CORS origin decisions, public-route classification, and JSON request-size configuration belong in `server/apiSecurityPolicy.mjs`, not alongside business route handlers. The API server consumes this policy and the security regression must cover both policy behavior and request handling.

Business modules must not bypass the policy by reading untrusted identity headers, setting permissive CORS responses, classifying their own route as public, or choosing their own JSON body limit.

## 2026-07-10 - Browser Builds Must Not Contain Prototype Passwords

The browser must not embed seed-user passwords, even when the API's strict production profile already rejects seed login. Local prototype account switching uses `POST /api/auth/prototype-login` with only a selected user ID. The endpoint may create a seed session only when the API is in a non-strict local prototype profile; strict authentication must return `AUTH_SEED_LOGIN_DISABLED` regardless of supplied values.

Production and runtime employee accounts continue to use server-side password verification and sessions. New browser authentication flows must not reintroduce a static prototype credential map, and the production build must be scanned for known prototype passwords.

## 2026-07-10 - Shared API Client Core Must Stay Transport-Only

`src/services/officeApiClientCore.js` owns only API base-URL resolution, Bearer or prototype operator request headers, JSON response reading, the standard API error shape, and strict-mode write-unavailable error construction. All existing office API clients now adopt this transport boundary.

Domain clients retain endpoint paths, query construction, payload shaping, response normalization, attachment URL handling, local read fallback, and user-facing error wording. Attachment upload / download, driver mobile, and V1 status keep their domain-specific behavior and are covered by their own regression suites. New special transport protocols must extend the core with focused coverage rather than creating another local request / header helper. This avoids turning the core into another large business client while eliminating duplicated transport / authentication behavior.

## 2026-07-10 - Production Business Writes Must Fail Closed

When the browser runtime is configured with `VITE_ERP_RUNTIME_MODE=production`, `strict`, or `server_required`, every business write must require a successful backend response. Network errors must return a blocked API error and must not apply a local state projection, create a local audit record, generate a local business attachment, or present a local result as completed.

This rule now covers order save/confirmation, order-line quantity adjustment/void, fulfillment actions, todo handling, statement actions, attachments, print-batch records, master-data, production / packing, printer field-test, driver delivery, and V1 status actions. Read-only lists and explicit prototype-only previews may retain local fallback only when they do not mutate business state and clearly expose their fallback source.

## 2026-07-10 - PostgreSQL Repositories Must Migrate To The Shared Async Pool

New or migrated production PostgreSQL repository paths must use `server/postgresPoolClient.mjs`, not `spawnSync("psql", [connectionString, ...])`. A write transaction must lease one pool connection, explicitly begin, commit on success, roll back on failure, and release the connection. Connection strings must not be supplied as child-process arguments.

The migrated batch covers order confirmation, order-line quantity adjustment, order-line void, inventory-reservation release, fulfillment actions, statement payment, statement settlement, statement send, statement export, print batch, attachment access audit / metadata, print devices / jobs / field tests, driver dispatch / task reads, runtime identity, payment records, production schedules, driver-device field tests, order-pool reads, inventory-ledger reads, master-data import transactions / reviews, raw-material supplier-statement reviews, production / packing transactions / reads, and raw-material inbound actions. API startup now exposes a readiness promise and waits for persistent state before serving requests or binding the executable entrypoint. Startup-loaded workspace projections may be used for synchronous readiness aggregation, but database reads and writes must remain awaited. The shared client accepts SQL values separately so migrated SQL can become parameterized; string-built legacy SQL is technical debt and must not be expanded.

Order confirmation, fulfillment actions, and the full statement domain (payment, variance / write-off, send / receipt, customer confirmation) are the first transaction paths required to use `{ text, values }` binding through `postgresSqlParameters.mjs`. New high-risk business transactions must adopt this boundary rather than adding `sqlLiteral(...)` builders; existing repositories are migrated in reviewable batches with query-text and values assertions.

The shared `postgresTransactionExecutor.mjs` may own only pool creation and test-injected transaction execution. Domain SQL, result normalization, and workspace projections remain inside their repositories so transaction infrastructure reuse does not create a generic business repository.

## 2026-07-10 - Production Order Confirmation Is Server-Authoritative

When the web runtime is configured with `VITE_ERP_RUNTIME_MODE=production`, `strict`, or `server_required`, office order confirmation and draft saving must require a successful backend response. The browser must not generate a local order, inventory reservation, fulfillment task, or todo after a failed or unavailable confirmation request.

After a successful confirmation, the browser must refresh the server order, inventory, fulfillment, and todo projections. Local order confirmation remains only for the explicit non-strict prototype mode. This decision applies to order confirmation first; other business writes must be migrated to the same rule before production release.

## 2026-07-10 - Production API Must Use Strict Authentication Boundary

Production API startup must use `ERP_AUTH_MODE=strict` (or `NODE_ENV=production`) together with a non-empty `ERP_AUTH_SECRET`. In this profile, every business route requires a verified Bearer session; seed-user login is disabled unless an explicit test-only server option enables it; legacy `seed:<user>` bearer values, `x-erp-user-id`, `x-erp-action-permissions`, and the implicit seed-office identity are not accepted. CORS may allow only origins configured through `ERP_CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, and JSON request bodies default to a 24 MiB upper bound (`ERP_API_MAX_JSON_BODY_BYTES`).

The local seed profile remains an explicit non-production compatibility mode for prototype and regression checks. Strict authentication is a deployment boundary, not a substitute for production identity persistence, password rotation, auditable server-side revocation, PostgreSQL, object storage, field hardware validation, or owner signoff.

## 2026-07-10 - Real-Value Intake Apply Should Reuse Production Env Setup Target By Default

Standalone production env real-value intake apply and dry-run should use the target safe env file recorded by the production env setup report by default. Operators should not have to manually pass or copy a target env-file path for the normal path after setup has produced and audited the safe env draft.

`run-v1-production-env-intake-apply` may accept `--target-env-file <secure-env-file>` as an explicit bypass, but generated setup reports, runbooks, values-fragment templates, first-stage execution, handoff artifacts, and go-live suite artifacts should prefer `--use-production-env-setup-env-file` with the production env setup report. Reports may expose only redacted source labels, counts, statuses, and safeguard booleans. They must not expose target env paths, values-fragment paths, raw env lines, env values, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, access keys, secret keys, command values, spool paths, tokens, stdout / stderr, or browser-supplied fake values.

Reusing the setup target is only a path-consistency and safety guard. It does not prove real PostgreSQL, restore database, object storage, production API profile, printing, driver phones, real-order pilot evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, or V1/V2 boundary completion.

## 2026-07-10 - Real-Value Dry-Run Proofs Expire By Default

Formal production env real-value apply must treat first-stage real-values dry-run proof freshness as a separate gate. A proof that is ready and matches the current minimum blocking target signature is still not enough if it has no checked-at timestamp or is older than the configured freshness window.

The default freshness window is 24 hours and is controlled by `ERP_V1_PRODUCTION_ENV_VALUES_DRY_RUN_MAX_AGE_HOURS`. Setting the value to `0` disables only the freshness window; it does not disable the requirement that the proof match the current minimum blocking path. Status APIs, live-run responses, handoff artifacts, and the office status page may expose freshness booleans, labels, max-age hours, age hours, and checked-at coverage, but must not expose env values, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, secrets, command values, spool paths, local file paths, raw env lines, stdout / stderr, or browser-submitted fake values.

An expired or timestamp-missing proof must keep `targetEnvFileMayBeMutated=false` and require operators to rerun real-values dry-run before formal apply. This is only a stale-proof guard; it does not prove real PostgreSQL, restore database, object storage, production API profile, printing, driver phones, real-order pilot evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, or V1/V2 boundary completion.

## 2026-07-10 - Formal Real-Value Apply Requires A Recent Dry-Run Proof

Formal production env real-value apply must not rely only on the server apply flag, a configured values fragment, values-fragment safety audit, and production-env-setup target readiness. It must also require a recent first-stage real-values dry-run proof showing that the minimum blocking path is ready. If the latest first-stage execution artifact does not include a ready real-values dry-run proof, the status page and live-run API must keep `targetEnvFileMayBeMutated=false` and block before invoking the merge runner or writing the target secure env draft.

Status APIs, live-run responses, and the office status page may expose only redacted proof status, readiness, inclusion, minimum blocking coverage labels / counts, safeguard booleans, and next actions. They must not expose values-fragment paths, target env paths, raw env lines, env values, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, access keys, secret keys, command values, spool paths, tokens, local paths, stdout / stderr, or browser-supplied fake values. A passing dry-run proof is only a formal-apply precondition; it still does not prove real PostgreSQL, restore database, object storage, production API profile, printing, driver phones, real-order pilot evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, or V1/V2 boundary completion.

## 2026-07-10 - Real-Values Fragments Must Pass Safety Audit Before Dry-Run Or Apply

Any server-configured production env real-values fragment used by the office status page must pass the same safety audit expectations as secure production env files before it can drive first-stage dry-run or formal apply. A values fragment that is template-like, tracked by git, inside the workspace but not ignored, unreadable, unsafe, or still containing placeholder values must block with `audit_blocked` before invoking the dry-run executor or real-value apply runner.

Status APIs and live-run responses may expose only audit status, readiness, counts, source-variable names, and safeguard booleans. They must not expose the values-fragment path, raw env lines, env values, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, access keys, secret keys, command values, spool paths, tokens, local paths, stdout / stderr, or browser-supplied fake values. Passing the fragment audit is only a precondition for dry-run / apply; it does not prove real PostgreSQL, restore database, object storage, production API profile, printing, driver phones, real-order pilot evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, or V1/V2 boundary completion.

## 2026-07-09 - Status-Page First-Stage Execution Must Stay Non-Mutating By Default

The office status page may expose an `执行第一阶段` action for the production environment / persistence first-stage execution, but the browser must not control execution paths or sensitive values. The API may run the first-stage executor only with the server-resolved production-env-setup safe env file, and it must ignore request-body paths, env values, values fragments, command arguments, tokens, and caller-provided fake values.

The status-page execution entrypoint is allowed to produce a redacted first-stage execution result and local first-stage evidence artifacts, but it must not apply schema migrations by default, must not allow restore-database reset without explicit server-side authorization, must not formally merge real values, must not refresh release-candidate or go-live-suite artifacts, must not call physical printers, must not mutate driver delivery state, must not write business data, and must not declare full V1 completion. Reports may expose only counts, statuses, source categories, blocked stage labels, safeguard booleans, and next actions. They must not expose local env-file paths, raw command lines, stdout / stderr containing secrets, env values, raw env lines, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, access keys, secret keys, spool paths, tokens, or caller-provided fake values.

Passing or running this entrypoint remains only one controlled production-env / persistence step. Real PostgreSQL, restore validation, object-storage live probes, printer / CUPS physical QA, driver-phone QA, real-order pilot evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation remain separate V1 completion gates.

## 2026-07-09 - Production Env Real-Value Apply Requires A Server Flag

The office status page may expose a `正式合并真实值` action for the production environment / persistence first stage, but the action must be inert by default. Formal apply must require a server-side deployment flag, `ERP_V1_PRODUCTION_ENV_VALUES_APPLY_ENABLED=true`, in addition to the action permission `system.v1_production_first_stage_values_apply.run`.

The browser request body must not select the values fragment, target env file, command arguments, env values, or tokens. The API may read only the server-configured values-fragment variables and may mutate only the target secure production env draft resolved by production env setup. Reports may expose counts, variable names, statuses, source-variable names, and safeguards only. They must not expose real env values, raw env lines, local paths, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, access keys, secrets, command values, spool paths, tokens, stdout, stderr, raw command lines, or caller-provided fake values.

Formal apply is still only a controlled production-env real-value merge step. It must not execute schema migrations, refresh release-candidate or go-live-suite artifacts, call physical printers, mutate driver delivery state, write business data, declare full V1 completion, or substitute for real PostgreSQL / restore database / object-storage probes, print-device QA, driver-phone QA, real-order pilot evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-09 - First Production Env Fill Should Use Minimum Blocking Fragment

The first production-env real-value fill pass should start from `production-env-minimum-values-fragment.template.env.example`, not the full values fragment. The minimum fragment is the preferred operator artifact for unblocking the current production env intake gate because it contains only the active blocking path, prefers canonical keys such as `ERP_V1_DATABASE_URL`, excludes non-preferred aliases such as `DATABASE_URL` / `PGURL`, and excludes warning / optional fallback rows.

The first dry-run after filling that fragment must explicitly report minimum blocking coverage separately from full intake coverage. Operators should be able to see whether the current minimum path is complete, such as `11/11`, even when warning / optional rows remain open.

The full `production-env-values-fragment.template.env.example` remains available for later warning cleanup, independent fallback setup, or site-standard alias choices. Generated setup, handoff, suite, and dry-run artifacts must keep both files and reports redacted, must not expose real env values, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, secrets, command values, spool paths, tokens, raw env lines, or local paths, and must not treat the presence of either template or a passing minimum dry-run as proof that real PostgreSQL, restore database, object storage, printer / CUPS, driver phones, field evidence, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation is complete.

## 2026-07-09 - Target Dot-Matrix Formal Notes At EPSON LQ-615KII

The current formal-note dot-matrix printer model is `EPSON LQ-615KII`. V1 outbound / self-pickup note and delivery-note templates, printer device records, CUPS / system-printer setup, and field QA should record and test against this model and the current continuous two-copy paper. The model is not the CUPS queue name or driver name; field setup still must capture the real system printer / CUPS queue name, driver, connection method, paper size, alignment parameters, and sample print evidence.

Default ERP device records should use internal ID `PRN-DOT-A`, display name `EPSON LQ-615KII 针式单据打印机`, and recommended CUPS queue name `epson_lq_615kii_notes`. The default profile may include official model specs such as 24-pin / 82-column dot-matrix printing, USB2.0 / IEEE-1284 interfaces, ESC/P-K-compatible control codes, 101.6-254mm continuous paper width, 0.065-0.32mm paper thickness, and 1+3 copy capability. These values are device facts and template assumptions only; they must not mark the printer as `system_printer`, `printed`, or V1-ready without real queue preflight and onsite QA.

The field print host is Windows 11, and the current formal-note paper is two-part / two-up continuous dot-matrix paper. A current finished-goods customer delivery-note photo sample has been received; it is a formal outbound / customer delivery sample, not a raw-material supplier delivery/sales note. There is no known alignment issue yet, but the exact Windows printer name, driver name, port / connection method, measured paper size, tractor-hole pitch, top-of-form, left margin, and physical output QA still must be collected from the field machine.

## 2026-07-09 - Raw-Material Labels Must Be Waterproof

The raw-material label printer has not been purchased yet. Because raw materials may be stored in the yard, raw-material roll/piece labels must be selected and tested as waterproof, moisture-resistant, dirt-resistant, and readable after short outdoor storage. V1 must not assume ordinary non-waterproof thermal paper for these labels.

The exact label printer model, label size, label material, and attachment method remain open until physical samples are tested on real rolls/pieces. This decision sets the procurement and template constraint only; it does not mark label-printer QA, barcode scanning, material receiving, or raw-material inventory availability as complete.

## 2026-07-09 - Production Env Alias Groups Must Be Handed Off As Choose-One

Production env variable aliases such as `ERP_V1_DATABASE_URL`, `DATABASE_URL`, and `PGURL` are alternatives, not separate required production values. Handoff artifacts must make that explicit so operators do not fill multiple aliases with different real connection strings or assume every alias line is mandatory.

Generated production env setup and go-live handoff outputs must include a redacted real-value intake checklist with owner role, variable key, alternative group, source system, expected value type, fill status, verification status, and evidence reference columns. Fill templates may still show commented `KEY=<REPLACE_WITH_KEY>` lines for aliases, but they must add choose-one guidance and must prefer the canonical project-specific key when available. These artifacts may contain only variable names and safe literal defaults; real connection strings, buckets, endpoints, command paths, spool paths, tokens, secrets, raw env lines, and caller-provided values must stay out of reports and templates.

## 2026-07-09 - Statement Export Storage May Use Attachment Object-Storage Fallback

V1 production statement-export storage may use either an independent statement-export object-storage bucket or the complete attachment object-storage fallback. The shortest first-stage production env path should therefore prioritize the attachment object-storage variables first, and should not force operators to configure a second statement-export bucket unless finance / operations explicitly requires independent retention.

Production env intake and generated values-fragment templates must mark independent `ERP_STATEMENT_EXPORT_OBJECT_STORAGE_*` rows as optional fallback rows when no independent statement bucket has been started. Intake verification must treat those independent rows as not required when the attachment fallback is complete, but it must block a partially configured independent statement-export bucket until the remaining independent variables are completed or the partial independent configuration is cleared. Reports may expose only variable names, counts, fallback status, and next actions; they must not expose object-storage endpoints, bucket names, access keys, secret keys, tokens, object keys, signed URLs, raw env lines, or caller-provided values.

## 2026-07-09 - Production Env Fill Templates May Only Emit Real Variable Names

Production env fix checklists may include prose-heavy guidance such as "valid PostgreSQL connection string", "http/https URL", "valid bucket name", "positive integer", or "different host/port/database from production source". Those phrases are operator guidance, not env variable names.

Any generated env fill template, including production env setup output and go-live handoff output, may emit only real env variable names and safe literal defaults such as `=postgres`, `=object_storage`, `=false`, `=command_bridge`, or `=cups_lp`. It must extract names such as `ERP_*`, `VITE_*`, `DATABASE_URL`, and `PGURL` from guidance text when useful, but it must not create fake placeholders from validation prose. Reports and templates must remain redacted and must not print actual env values, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, commands, paths, tokens, secrets, or raw env lines.

## 2026-07-09 - Production Env Preflight Must Validate Value Shape Without Exposing Values

V1 production env preflight should reject obvious filled-value mistakes before later live probes run. Presence alone is not enough for production database, restore-validation, object-storage, and CUPS configuration.

The preflight may parse PostgreSQL URLs and `host/port/dbname` keyword connection strings only to derive safe booleans and compare source / restore-validation physical targets. It must block restore validation when the production source and restore-validation target resolve to the same host / port / database. It may validate object-storage endpoint URL shape, bucket-name shape, CUPS timeout shape, and API base URL shape, but reports must expose only variable names, counts, booleans, statuses, and next actions. It must not expose database URLs, hosts, ports, database names, endpoints, bucket values, command values, paths, tokens, secrets, raw env lines, or caller-provided values. Passing this preflight still proves configuration shape only; real PostgreSQL, object-storage live probes, printing, driver phones, real-order pilot, onsite evidence, and signoff remain separate completion gates.

## 2026-07-09 - Audit-Only Env File Sources Cannot Satisfy Production Go-Live

`ERP_V1_PRODUCTION_ENV_FILE_AUDIT_PATHS` is a read-only audit / preview source only. It may be used by diagnostics to inspect a secure env file without mutating the running API process, but it must not be treated as proof that the API has applied production env at startup.

Production go-live, production profile confirmation, and release-candidate refresh may use only API startup application sources: `ERP_V1_PRODUCTION_ENV_FILE` first, then `ERP_V1_ENV_FILE`. If only the audit-only source is configured, status APIs may show it as configured for troubleshooting, but it must be non-selectable / ignored for go-live and must keep the release gates blocked. Reports may expose variable names, source kind, counts, selected / ignored booleans and next actions only; they must not expose env-file paths, env values, connection strings, object-storage values, command values, tokens, secrets, raw file lines, or caller-provided paths.

## 2026-07-08 - First-Stage Evidence Suggestions Require Human Review

Automated production persistence and runtime-smoke reports may be used to prepare suggested V1 field-evidence intake rows for the `production_persistence` and `object_storage` groups, but those suggestions are not owner signoff and are not source-of-truth evidence by themselves. The suggestion runner must be non-mutating, redacted, review-required, and conservative when source reports are missing or incomplete.

The runner may suggest `accepted` only for evidence items fully supported by automated reports, and it must preserve existing onsite entries unless an explicit overwrite option is used. Partial or manual-only evidence, including backup strategy owner approval, bucket backup / control-plane proof, and access-audit query proof, must remain pending for humans to fill. Generated CSV / JSON / Markdown artifacts may support the existing intake apply / draft manifest flow, but they must not update the source CSV or source manifest directly, refresh release-candidate or go-live-suite artifacts, mutate production env files or business data, expose raw paths / secrets / evidence refs, or declare V1 complete.

## 2026-07-08 - Object Storage Governance Automation Requires Versioning, Lifecycle And Encryption

V1 production object-storage readiness must not stop at diagnostic object write / read / signed URL / delete probes. A separate first-stage governance check must use signed S3-compatible bucket subresource reads for versioning, lifecycle configuration, server-side encryption and bucket-policy readability, and the production persistence evidence packet must carry that governance stage separately from the live object write/read/delete stage.

The governance check is read-only. Versioning, lifecycle and default server-side encryption are blocking automation checks because they prove recovery, retention and at-rest protection safeguards. Bucket-policy readability is a non-blocking warning because some providers use IAM / RAM / CAM control-plane policies rather than a readable bucket policy. Reports may expose only booleans, HTTP status counts, readiness, safeguards and next actions; they must not expose endpoints, bucket names, access keys, secrets, session tokens, signed requests, object keys, signed URLs, bucket policy, response bodies, payloads or raw XML. Passing this check provides generic bucket-governance evidence only; onsite control-panel screenshots, backup-policy owner confirmation, access-audit query evidence, real attachment upload/readback, real statement-export download, owner signoff, and later print / driver / real-order validation remain separate V1 completion requirements.

## 2026-07-08 - PostgreSQL Restore Validation Requires A Dedicated Resettable Database

V1 production PostgreSQL backup / restore evidence may be generated by a runner, but restore validation must never run against the production source database. The runner must require a separate restore-validation database URL, must block when that URL is equal to the production database URL, and must require explicit reset authorization before dropping or recreating schemas in the validation database.

The production source database is read-only for this check. It may be accessed only through `pg_dump` for schema and safe metadata samples such as `schema_migrations`; business data dumps are not part of this first V1 validation path. The report may expose readiness, counts, byte sizes, safeguard booleans, and next actions, but must not expose database URLs, passwords, dump contents, local dump paths, raw SQL stderr, connection hosts, or secrets. Passing this runner provides backup / restore evidence tooling only; it does not create the real PostgreSQL instance, configure schedules, prove offsite backups, fill onsite evidence, obtain owner signoff, or complete print / driver / real-order validation.

## 2026-07-08 - Runtime Smoke May Probe A Long-Running Production API

The V1 production runtime smoke may run in two modes. The original mode starts a temporary API process from the secure env file, probes `/api/health` and `/api/system/v1-readiness`, then must stop the spawned process. The production-service mode is explicit through `--api-base-url <production-api-base-url>` and probes an already-running production API without spawning or stopping a process.

Both modes are read-only runtime evidence for the production env / persistence stage. Reports may expose only sanitized runtime mode and safeguard booleans such as `externalApiProbed`, `apiProcessSpawned`, and `apiProcessTerminated`; they must not expose env values, connection strings, object-storage values, command values, payloads, or secrets. First-stage closeout may accept either a stopped temporary API smoke or a read-only external API probe as the runtime process safeguard. This does not declare full V1 complete and does not replace real printer validation, driver-phone validation, real-order pilot evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-08 - Production First-Stage Execution Uses Secure Env Manifest Path

The production first-stage execution runner may resolve the filled V1 field-evidence manifest from secure deployment configuration. Explicit `--field-evidence-manifest` still takes precedence, then `ERP_V1_FIELD_EVIDENCE_MANIFEST` from the secure env files, then the current process env, and only then the checked-in pending template.

This path is deployment configuration, not evidence content. Generated reports and runbooks must redact the actual manifest path and must not expose env-file paths, raw evidence references, connection strings, object-storage values, command values, spool paths, tokens, or secrets. Reports may expose only the source category and configured / default-template booleans so operators can see whether the runner used CLI, secure env file, process env, or the checked-in template. Falling back to the pending template is acceptable only as a blocked default; plan-only and blocked next actions must say that closeout cannot become ready until a filled manifest path is configured.

## 2026-07-08 - Production First-Stage Closeout Requires Onsite Persistence Evidence

The production env / persistence first-stage closeout must not treat automated persistence reports or runtime probes alone as owner-signoff evidence. A first-stage closeout can be ready only when the automated persistence evidence and runtime smoke are ready, fresh, and guarded, and the filled field-evidence manifest also proves the required `production_persistence` and `object_storage` evidence items with `passed` / `accepted` status and an evidence reference.

The closeout remains non-mutating and redacted: it must not connect to external services, write business data, call printers, mutate driver state, expose raw evidence references, expose env-file paths, expose connection strings, expose object-storage values, or declare V1 complete. The first-stage execution runner may forward a filled manifest path to the closeout, but it must redact that path in generated output. Real backup / restore evidence, bucket policy review, attachment readback, statement-export evidence, onsite evidence, owner signoff, real printing, driver-phone validation, and real-order pilot evidence remain separate completion requirements.

## 2026-07-06 - Production Go-Live Precheck Must Reject Local Readiness Bypasses

V1 production go-live precheck must combine the secure env-file audit, production env-variable preflight, current API V1 readiness, and a separate current-runtime production-profile check. A lower-level readiness runner may be allowed to pass in laboratory automation by explicitly accepting local persistence or local file retention, but that acceptance must not be treated as proof that the running production API is ready for V1.

The production profile check must require the current API to run with production-ready repositories and live object storage. Local persistence acceptance, local attachment retention acceptance, local repositories, non-production repositories, or non-live attachment storage must keep the production go-live precheck blocked. The precheck is read-only: it must not apply env files to the current process, restart the API, refresh release-candidate artifacts, run the go-live suite, call physical printers, change driver task state, or expose env values, connection strings, object-storage values, command values, spool paths, local paths, tokens, or secrets.

## 2026-07-06 - Closeout Labels Must Show Visible Actions Over Total Remaining

Post-save closeout sections may intentionally render only the first few sanitized action rows to keep the ERP page dense and usable. Their count labels must therefore show visible actions over total remaining work, such as `5/33` for field evidence and `5/7` for signoff / boundary, rather than a total-only or all-visible-looking value.

The backend should expose both `actionShownCount` and `actionCount` for these closeout summaries. `actionShownCount` must match the actual returned `actions.length`; `actionCount` represents the total remaining action count from the draft-manifest validation or sanitized fallback. Signoff / boundary fallback readiness must treat validation rows with `blocking=false` as ready even when no explicit `ready=true` property exists. This is display accuracy only and must not broaden the save boundary, auto-complete evidence, auto-complete signoff, confirm V1/V2 boundary, refresh release-candidate / suite artifacts, or mutate production env, printer / driver state, onsite task state, or business data.

## 2026-07-06 - Post-Save Closeout Actions Use Draft Manifest Validation

After a field-evidence, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary draft row is saved, post-save closeout action rows should be derived from the freshly generated draft manifest validation whenever that validation is available. The mutable intake CSV may have only the row currently being staged or may not contain every remaining V1 manifest item, so CSV-only action generation can show closeout summaries without actionable remaining rows.

The returned actions must stay sanitized and limited to the identifiers needed for draft prefill, such as `groupKey:itemKey` for field evidence and `type:key` for signoff / boundary rows, plus labels, owner/group context, progress labels, and next action text. These actions may be rendered as `填到草稿` in the save result area. Clicking them may only preselect and clear the corresponding controlled draft form. It must not write CSVs, save another row, upload or reuse attachments, mark evidence or signoff complete, confirm the V1/V2 boundary, refresh release-candidate or go-live-suite artifacts, mutate production env, mutate printer / driver state, mutate onsite tasks, or mutate business data.

## 2026-07-06 - Stage Row Save May Return Sanitized Evidence Closeout

The field-evidence stage-row save boundary may return a compact `evidenceCloseout` summary after saving any evidence, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary draft row. This summary may include sanitized evidence progress, missing evidence counts, filled-reference counts, invalid row counts, next action text, and the first sanitized missing-evidence rows so onsite users can see what field evidence remains immediately after a save.

This closeout is status guidance only. It must not expose raw evidence references, notes, raw CSV rows, manifest contents, artifact paths, local paths, env values, command values, connection strings, tokens, secrets, or caller-provided fake values. Returning or rendering the closeout must not mark field evidence complete, refresh release-candidate state, refresh go-live-suite state, mutate production env, mutate printer / driver state, mutate onsite task state, or mutate business data. V1 remains incomplete until real field evidence, signoffs, boundary confirmation, production env, devices, field acceptance, and release gates all pass.

## 2026-07-06 - Stage Row Save May Return Sanitized Signoff Boundary Closeout

The field-evidence stage-row save boundary may return a compact `closeout` summary after saving any evidence, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary draft row. This summary may include sanitized signoff progress, missing signoff counts, invalid row counts, boundary status, next action text, and the first sanitized signoff / boundary action rows so onsite users can see what remains immediately after a save.

This closeout is status guidance only. It must not expose raw signer or confirmor values, timestamps, notes, raw CSV rows, manifest contents, artifact paths, local paths, env values, command values, connection strings, tokens, secrets, or caller-provided fake values. Returning or rendering the closeout must not mark signoff complete, confirm the V1/V2 boundary, refresh release-candidate state, refresh go-live-suite state, mutate production env, mutate printer / driver state, mutate onsite task state, or mutate business data. V1 remains incomplete until real field evidence, signoffs, boundary confirmation, production env, devices, field acceptance, and release gates all pass.

## 2026-07-06 - Signoff And Boundary Rows May Prefill Draft Selection Only

The office `上线状态` page may expose a `填到草稿` action on each visible V1 signoff or V1/V2 boundary action row. The action may select the matching `type:key` in the controlled `签字 / 边界草稿` form, set the default onsite status to `signed` for ordinary signoff rows or `confirmed` for the V1/V2 boundary row, clear stale person / time / notes / selected-file state, and focus the signer or confirmor input so onsite users can immediately fill the real signer / confirmor and time.

This action is not a signoff save, boundary confirmation, attachment upload, or release approval. It must not call the stage-row API, write intake CSVs, generate or validate manifests, upload or reuse attachments, mark signoff complete, confirm the V1/V2 boundary, refresh release-candidate state, refresh go-live-suite state, mutate production env, mutate printer / driver state, mutate onsite task state, or mutate business data. Clearing stale local draft fields is intentional: it prevents a previous signer, confirmation time, attachment selection, or note from being accidentally applied to another signoff or boundary item.

## 2026-07-06 - Missing Evidence Rows May Prefill Draft Selection Only

The office `上线状态` page may expose a `填到草稿` action on each visible missing field-evidence row. The action may select the matching `groupKey:itemKey` in the controlled `现场证据草稿` form, clear stale evidence-reference / notes / selected-file state, and focus the evidence-reference input so onsite users can immediately upload or paste the correct backend `ATT-*` / evidence reference.

This action is not a field-evidence save, upload, or release approval. It must not call the stage-row API, write intake CSVs, generate or validate manifests, upload or reuse attachments, mark evidence complete, confirm signoff or V1/V2 boundary rows, refresh release-candidate state, refresh go-live-suite state, mutate production env, mutate printer / driver state, mutate onsite task state, or mutate business data. Clearing stale local draft fields is intentional: it prevents a previous evidence reference or selected local file from being accidentally applied to another missing evidence item.

## 2026-07-06 - Missing Field Evidence Can Be Listed Fully But Remains Sanitized

The office `上线状态` page may expose the full V1 missing-field-evidence list from the go-live status API. The API may return all missing evidence items, while the UI should keep a compact default view and let users expand or collapse the list so the status page remains usable in dense ERP layouts.

The full list is still status and execution guidance only. It may include sanitized evidence item keys, labels, groups, owner roles, progress labels, and next actions. It must not expose raw evidence references, notes, CSV rows, manifest contents, artifact paths, local paths, env values, command values, connection strings, tokens, secrets, or caller-provided fake values. Expanding the list must not stage evidence, save signoff or boundary rows, upload attachments, confirm V1/V2 boundary, refresh release-candidate state, refresh go-live-suite state, or mutate production env, printer state, driver state, onsite task state, or business data. Seeing all missing evidence items is an execution aid, not V1 release approval.

## 2026-07-06 - V1 Closeout Full Review Is Read-Only Diagnostics

The office `上线状态` page may expose a `V1 收尾总复核` action to sequence existing V1 closeout checks from one place. This action may run field-evidence draft validation, V1/V2 boundary precheck, release-candidate refresh precheck, and runtime-readiness live precheck in order, using the already-approved read-only or precheck boundaries.

This action must remain diagnostic-only. It must not stage or save evidence rows, save signoff / boundary rows, upload or reuse attachments, confirm the V1/V2 boundary, refresh the release candidate, refresh the go-live suite, mutate production env, printer state, driver state, onsite task state, or business data. It may show only sanitized blocker counts, status labels, next actions, and safeguards from the existing checks. It must not expose raw evidence refs, signer / confirmor values, notes, CSV rows, manifest contents, artifact paths, local paths, env values, command values, tokens, secrets, or caller-provided fake values. A successful full-review click is an operation shortcut only; V1 remains incomplete until real production env, runtime readiness, field evidence, signoffs, V1/V2 boundary confirmation, and release gates all pass.

## 2026-07-06 - Boundary Save-And-Precheck Is Draft Save Plus Read-Only Boundary Precheck

The office `上线状态` page may expose a dedicated `保存并预检边界` action when the selected signoff / boundary draft row is the V1/V2 boundary confirmation row. This action may first call the existing controlled field-evidence stage-row boundary to save only the boundary draft row, and only after a successful stage result may it call the existing read-only V1/V2 boundary precheck.

This action must not introduce a broader write boundary. It must not confirm the V1/V2 boundary without the existing confirmor and confirmed-time validation, must not treat an attachment note as confirmation, must not refresh the release candidate, must not refresh the go-live suite, and must not mutate production env, printer state, driver state, field evidence, signoff rows, onsite task state, or business data. The result is an operation shortcut for onsite closeout only; V1 remains incomplete until the real boundary confirmation, production env, runtime readiness, field evidence, signoffs, and release gates all pass.

## 2026-07-06 - V1/V2 Scope Brief Refresh Updates Only The Generated Brief

The office `上线状态` page may expose a controlled `刷新差异` action for V1/V2 scope communication. This write boundary is `POST /api/system/v1-v2-scope-brief/refresh`, guarded by `system.v1_v2_scope_brief.refresh` for office / management users. The server may rerun the approved V1/V2 scope-brief generator with server-owned artifact roots and the current completion snapshot, then return only sanitized status, counts, V1 must-continue items, V2 categories / differences, module differences, owner review rules, and explicit safeguards.

This action must not confirm the V1/V2 boundary, must not refresh release-candidate artifacts, must not refresh the go-live suite, and must not mutate field evidence, signoff rows, production env, printer state, driver state, onsite task state, or business data. The browser request body must be ignored. Frontend-provided paths, output directories, tokens, command arguments, fake V2 differences, fake evidence refs, fake signers, or fake secrets must never influence which files are read, which command is run, which differences are reported, or whether V1 can be declared complete. Responses must not expose stdout / stderr, command lines, command arguments, artifact paths, local paths, env values, connection strings, object-storage values, access keys, tokens, secrets, raw evidence refs, signer / confirmor values, notes, or caller-provided fake values. A successful refresh means only that the owner-facing V1/V2 difference summary was regenerated; V1 remains incomplete until production env, runtime readiness, field evidence, signoffs, V1/V2 boundary confirmation, and release gates all pass.

## 2026-07-06 - Release-Candidate Refresh Requires Ready Precheck And Server-Owned Inputs

The office `上线状态` page may expose a controlled `刷新候选` action only after the separate refresh-precheck boundary exists. This write boundary is `POST /api/system/v1-release-candidate/refresh`, guarded by `system.v1_release_candidate.refresh`. The server must rerun the same release-candidate refresh precheck immediately before any refresh attempt. If evidence, signoff, production-env, runtime, or V1/V2 boundary gates are not ready, the route must return a blocked response such as `409 / blocked_by_precheck` and must not execute the go-live suite or refresh release-candidate artifacts.

When the precheck is ready, the route may run the approved go-live suite refresh command with server-owned inputs only: current API URL, server-configured env-file audit paths, configured operator identities, canonical artifact roots, and fixed script arguments. The browser request body must be ignored. Frontend-provided env-file paths, env values, bearer tokens, command arguments, fake evidence refs, fake signer values, or fake secrets must never influence which files are read, which API is called, which command is run, or whether the refresh proceeds. Redaction must remain explicit: no artifact paths, env values, command values, secrets, raw evidence refs, raw onsite-task-board payloads, raw field-evidence CSV payloads, raw signoff/boundary CSV payloads, raw signer names, raw confirmor names, raw signed / confirmed timestamps, raw notes, internal field names such as `evidenceRef` or `signer / signedAt`, connection strings, bucket values, access keys, secret keys, command paths, command args, spool paths, tokens, or local file paths should be exposed. If the API or artifacts are unavailable, the UI may fall back to static status text, but the completion standard stays the same: V1 is complete only after release candidate, field evidence, signoff, and V1/V2 boundary gates are truly satisfied.

The same route may expose sanitized field-evidence intake guidance when the intake CSVs and rules artifacts are available. It may show evidence / signoff counts, boundary status, draft-manifest status, rules availability, placeholder-only command templates, blocked reasons, and next action text because these help onsite owners turn filled CSVs into a draft manifest and rerun go-live checks. These commands must use placeholders such as `<field-evidence-intake-csv>` and `<filled-field-evidence-manifest-draft>`; they must not include `.erp-local-storage`, absolute paths, raw evidence refs, raw signer / confirmor values, raw timestamps, raw notes, raw rules text, raw draft manifest payloads, real env values, command values, or secrets. Showing intake guidance is execution guidance only; it must not mutate evidence CSVs, signoff CSVs, draft manifests, release-candidate state, go-live-suite state, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

The same route may expose sanitized field-evidence intake quality checks when the intake CSVs, signoff / boundary CSV, rules artifact, and draft-manifest status are available. It may show only counts, quality check labels, pass / block status, whether a draft can be generated, whether release candidate can be refreshed, and owner-facing next actions. This helps onsite owners distinguish present-but-empty CSVs, invalid partial rows, missing draft manifests, missing signoffs, and missing V1/V2 boundary confirmation. It must not expose raw CSV rows, raw evidence refs, signer / confirmor names, raw signed / confirmed timestamps, raw notes, draft manifest contents, artifact paths, local paths, environment values, command values, secrets, or concrete local draft filenames. Showing quality checks is read-only QA guidance; it must not mutate evidence, signoff, boundary, draft, release-candidate, or go-live-suite state and must not imply V1 completion while evidence, signoff, boundary, production environment, real devices, or release candidate remain incomplete.

The same route may expose sanitized runtime readiness blockers from release-candidate `blockingItems` when available. It may show the readiness count, blocker labels, blocker groups, owner roles, sanitized details, and next actions because those explain why `5/11` is still blocked. It must not expose raw runtime reports, artifact paths, command values, environment values, secrets, printer payloads, spool paths, driver payloads, raw evidence refs, signer fields, or local paths. Showing runtime blockers is execution guidance only; it must not mutate release-candidate state, field evidence, signoff, driver delivery state, printer state, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation, and it must not imply V1 completion while any release gate remains blocked.

The same route may expose a sanitized `fieldAcceptanceReport` when the generated field-acceptance package is available. It may show the report status, passed / total / blocking counts, module readiness rows, blocking criteria, required onsite evidence groups, remaining V1 risks, and next actions because those explain why the field-acceptance package is still blocked. It must not expose the raw field-acceptance report payload, source artifact paths, local paths, raw evidence refs, command values, printer payloads, spool paths, driver payloads, environment values, secrets, or physical-device payloads. Showing this report is go/no-go communication only; it must not mutate release-candidate state, onsite evidence, signoff, printer state, driver state, production configuration, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation, and it must not imply V1 completion while the report remains blocked or any release gate remains incomplete.

The same route may expose a sanitized `productionEnvGate` from release-candidate `envPreflight` and `envFileAudit` when available. It may show readiness counts, blocking / warning counts, audit status labels, check labels, owner roles, configured / total variable counts, missing variable names, and next actions because those explain the first production unblock stage. It must not expose raw preflight reports, raw env files, raw env-file audit payloads, raw line content, env-file paths, artifact paths, local paths, environment values, connection strings, object-storage endpoint / bucket values, access keys, secret keys, command paths, command args, spool paths, tokens, or payloads. Showing the production environment gate is execution guidance only; it must not mutate env files, production configuration, release-candidate state, field evidence, signoff, printer state, driver state, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation, and it must not imply V1 completion while real production env, PostgreSQL, object storage, CUPS, printer hardware, field evidence, signoff, or boundary confirmation remains incomplete.

The same route should expose a sanitized `v1V2BoundaryBrief` when the generated V1/V2 scope brief is available. This section may show V1 must-continue items, planned V2 categories, planned V2 differences, module-level V1/V2 differences, and owner review guidance because these explain what must stay in V1 and what can wait for V2. It must remain read-only and must not mutate boundary confirmation. It must also keep the owner-facing rule explicit: V2 differences do not block V1, but production configuration, real devices, field evidence, signoff, release candidate, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation cannot be moved to V2 to declare V1 complete.

## 2026-07-05 - V1 Status Page Is Communication Only

The office `上线状态` page may summarize V1 completion, module readiness, hard blockers, and planned V2 differences inside the ERP UI, but it must not be treated as a release approval mechanism. Its percentages and blocker rows are status communication for owners and operators.

V1 completion still requires release-candidate READY, real production configuration, real printer / CUPS / driver-phone field evidence, onsite task clearance, required signoffs, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation. If the status page displays `V1 仍未完成`, that wording is intentional until those gates are cleared. Future updates to the page must keep the completion boundary explicit and must not imply that code completion alone is enough to announce V1 complete.

The same page should surface the current minimum unblock path when available, so owners see the ordered next work instead of only percentages. That unblock path remains non-mutating status communication: selecting phases or reading first actions must not change evidence state, signoff state, release-candidate status, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

The unblock path may expose sanitized phase groups and first-task previews in the UI. Group labels, group counts, first-task titles, task types, owner roles, status labels, and next actions are acceptable execution metadata because they help onsite owners understand how to clear the current phase. The page must not expose raw task-board payloads, raw evidence refs, signer / confirmor names, local paths, artifact paths, env values, command values, or secrets, and task previews must remain read-only status guidance rather than release approval.

## 2026-07-05 - Raw-Material Reviewed Margin Report Is Internal Only In V1

Raw-material margin review in V1 creates an internal finance margin report, not a customer statement, collection record, or final financial settlement. `review-margin-snapshot` may only run after an `RMMG-*` margin snapshot exists, and the linked order revenue must be complete; missing snapshots, missing order revenue, and duplicate reviews must be blocked with stable business errors.
