A successful review creates an `RMMR-*` report, updates related inbounds, drafts, confirmations, loss calibrations, issue records, consumption records, and margin snapshots to `毛利已复核/报表可用` / `已财务复核/报表可用`, and records `marginEffect=reviewed_margin_report_snapshot`. The report is available for internal finance review and management analysis only. It must not write customer statements, must not register payment collection or final customer settlement, and must not treat machine counts / cycle counts as qualified finished-goods output. Office and warehouse users must not review this snapshot without `raw_material.margin.review`.

## 2026-07-05 - Raw-Material Margin Snapshot Is Review-Only In V1

Raw-material order margin in V1 is generated as a finance / management review snapshot, not as final financial settlement. `generate-margin-snapshot` may only run after `RMCL-*` loss calibrations exist; missing loss calibration and duplicate snapshot generation must be blocked with stable business errors. A successful action creates an `RMMG-*` record that links the inbound, loss calibrations, cost confirmations, cost drafts, issue records, consumption records, production tasks, and order lines.

The snapshot may calculate order-line sales amount, calibrated material cost, gross profit, and gross margin rate. Missing sales amount must remain a warning such as `需补订单收入`; the system must not fabricate revenue to make a margin rate look complete. The effect is `marginEffect=margin_snapshot_pending_review`: it may update raw-material costing records to `毛利快照待复核`, but it must not write customer statements, confirm final settlement, or treat machine cycle counts as qualified output. Office and warehouse users must not generate this snapshot without `raw_material.margin.snapshot`.

## 2026-07-05 - Raw-Material Loss Calibration Creates A Pending Margin Snapshot

Raw-material loss calibration is a finance / management boundary after cost confirmation, not a final order-margin write. `calibrate-loss` may only run after `RMCC-*` raw-material cost confirmations already exist; missing confirmations and duplicate calibration must be blocked with stable business errors. A successful calibration creates an `RMCL-*` record and updates the inbound, cost drafts, confirmations, issue records, and consumption records to `损耗已校准待毛利确认` / `已校准/待毛利确认`.

The calibrated effect is a loss-calibrated material-cost snapshot for later margin reporting: `costEffect=loss_calibrated_material_cost_snapshot` and `marginEffect=pending_margin_snapshot`. It may record expected output, actual qualified output, loss quantity, and loss rate, but it must not update final order gross margin, must not treat machine cycle counts as qualified output, and must remain unavailable to office / warehouse users without `raw_material.cost.calibrate`.

## 2026-07-05 - Raw-Material Cost Confirmation Creates A Material Cost Snapshot

Raw-material cost draft confirmation is a finance / management review boundary, not an order-margin write. `confirm-cost-draft` may only run after `RMCA-*` raw-material cost-allocation drafts already exist; missing drafts and duplicate confirmations must be blocked with stable business errors. A successful confirmation creates an `RMCC-*` confirmation record and updates the inbound, issue, consumption, and draft records to a reviewed state.

The confirmed effect is a material-cost snapshot for later loss calibration: `costEffect=confirmed_material_cost_snapshot`, `marginEffect=none`, and `lossCalibrationStatus=待损耗校准`. It must not calculate final loss, must not update order gross margin, must not treat machine cycle counts as qualified output, and must remain unavailable to office / warehouse users without `raw_material.cost.confirm`.

## 2026-07-04 - Raw-Material Cost Allocation Starts As Draft-Only

Raw-material cost allocation in V1 starts as a finance / management draft action, not as a final accounting write. `RMCA-*` drafts may be generated only from confirmed raw-material consumption records whose issue record is already matched to a production task; unlinked machine-side issue, missing unit price, missing consumption, and duplicate draft generation must be blocked.

The first draft amount uses the inbound unit-price snapshot and consumed weight / quantity, then links the draft back to the issue and consumption records. It must stay `costEffect=draft_only`, `marginEffect=none`, and `lossCalibrationStatus=待损耗校准`. Final order cost, loss calibration, margin reporting, and any irreversible financial view require a later review / approval workflow and field evidence. Ordinary office and warehouse users may see operational status but must not generate these cost drafts or see cost amounts without cost permission.

## 2026-07-04 - Raw-Material Issue Must Validate Production Task When Provided

Raw-material machine-side issue may still be recorded without a production task in V1, because helpers sometimes stage material by machine or near-term task group before office has finished precise costing. That path must be marked as `未关联生产任务` and cannot be used as proof for cost allocation or order margin until the task is filled.

When a `productionTaskId` is provided, it is no longer enough to save the string. The backend must validate that the production task exists, the issue machine matches the task machine, and bag-body raw-material color matches the order-line bag color. Unknown tasks, machine mismatches, and obvious fabric / nonwoven color mismatches are blocking business errors. Handle-material matching remains a manual-review boundary until real handle-color / handle-type rules and field evidence are collected. This matching decision creates a traceable issue point only; it still does not allocate raw-material cost, calculate loss, compute margin, create finished goods, or treat machine counters as qualified output.

## 2026-07-04 - Split-Roll Issue And Partial Consumption Are Trace-Only In V1

Raw-material V1 now supports a narrow split-roll / partial-consumption path for weighted rolls. When warehouse or packing issues only part of a labeled available roll, the system creates `RMI-SPLIT-*`, keeps the source roll available with the remaining weight, and creates a machine-side child roll tied to the source roll. A later `RMI-CONS-*` can record measured partial consumption of that machine-side child roll and leave the remaining weight at machine side.

This is still not a costing or production-output transaction. Split issue and partial consumption must not allocate raw-material cost to an order, must not calculate loss or margin, and must not treat machine count / cycle count as qualified finished-goods quantity. Real shop-floor issue confirmation, loss calibration, cost allocation, and order gross margin remain separate later workflows that need field evidence.

## 2026-07-04 - Leftover Review Can Restore Inventory But Not Costing In V1

Machine-side leftover return and leftover review are two separate V1 actions. Returning leftover material creates `RMI-RET-*`, moves the roll / piece to `余料待复核`, and keeps it out of available inventory until an authorized warehouse / packing / management operator reviews it. The review action creates `RMI-LREV-*`, records reviewed weight or quantity, review location, reviewer, and time, then moves the roll / piece back to `可用`.

This review is an inventory-state recovery, not a costing transaction. It does not allocate raw-material cost to orders, does not calculate loss, does not adjust margin, and does not treat machine counts or reported output as finished goods. Loss calibration and task / order cost allocation still require later workflows and field evidence.

## 2026-07-04 - Raw-Material Consumption And Leftover Return Stay Trace-Only In V1

Raw-material consumption confirmation and machine-side leftover return are follow-up states after a roll / piece has already been issued to machine side. The first V1 implementation records full-roll / full-piece consumption as `RMI-CONS-*` and moves the roll / piece to `已消耗`; it records machine-side leftover return as `RMI-RET-*` and moves the roll / piece to `余料待复核`.

These actions are intentionally not costing transactions. Confirming consumption does not create finished-goods output, does not treat machine counts as qualified quantity, and does not allocate material cost or margin. Returning leftover material does not automatically restore available inventory; it must be re-weighed / rechecked by a later workflow before it can become reusable stock or affect cost allocation. Precise partial consumption, split-roll leftovers, return-to-available inventory, loss calibration, and order gross-margin allocation require separate later transactions, permissions, and field evidence.

## 2026-07-04 - Raw-Material Issue Is Full Roll Or Full Piece In V1

Raw-material issue to machine side must follow label attachment confirmation. A roll / piece can only be issued after it has been physically labeled, phone-scanned / signed, and marked available. The first V1 implementation records `RMI-ISS-*` issue records through the raw-material inbound payload, moves the roll / piece to `机边领用`, and stores machine, optional production task, issue purpose, operator, time, and consumption status.

V1 issue is intentionally coarse: it supports full-roll / full-piece issue by warehouse, packing / helper, or management permissions, and blocks split-roll or partial-piece issue unless a later workflow records actual split weight, meter count, or remaining proportion. The issue action does not create finished-goods output, does not treat machine counters as qualified quantity, and does not allocate order cost or margin. Production reporting, leftover / return registration, loss calibration, and cost allocation require later separate transactions and field evidence.

## 2026-07-04 - Supplier Payment Confirmation Requires A Payable Draft

Supplier payment confirmation is a finance / management action that must follow a generated supplier payable draft. A raw-material supplier monthly statement must first be manually reviewed, confirmed with an `RMSRC-*` reconciliation id, and converted into an `RMSP-*` payable draft before an account with `raw_material.supplier_payment.confirm` may confirm payment.

The first V1 payment confirmation creates an `RMSPAY-*` confirmation id and records paid amount, payment method, optional bank reference / voucher, confirmer, and confirmation time. V1 requires the paid amount to equal the payable draft amount; partial payments, multiple payments, prepayments, payable aging, and complex variance carry-forward stay out of this first boundary. Confirming payment sets supplier payment status to paid and payable status to paid, but must not write raw-material inventory, customer statement payments, or customer receivables.

## 2026-07-04 - Structure Supplier Statement Adjustments Before Payable Drafts

Supplier monthly statement footer adjustments must be structured before they can safely feed supplier payable drafts. V1 now treats Baihou paper-tube deductions as a supplier-specific configurable rule with a default rate of `3.5 CNY / piece`, not as a generic hard-coded deduction for every supplier. The importer should preserve the supplier footer text and supplier-reported amount, calculate the rule amount from the piece count, and flag mismatches for manual split review when a footer appears to combine returns, tube deductions, or other adjustments.

Historical arrears, total balances, opening balances, and similar reference rows must be marked as non-current-period adjustments. Payable draft generation should use structured fields such as `isCurrentPeriod=false` and `adjustmentType=reference_balance` before falling back to text matching. This keeps supplier Excel import, reconciliation confirmation, and payable draft generation from silently mixing previous balances into current-period payable amounts.

## 2026-07-04 - Supplier Payable Draft Is Separate From Statement Confirmation And Payment

Supplier payable draft generation is a finance / management action, not an office reconciliation shortcut. A raw-material supplier monthly statement must first be manually reviewed as consistent and formally confirmed with an `RMSRC-*` reconciliation id. Only then may an account with `raw_material.supplier_payable.create` generate an `RMSP-*` payable draft.

The payable draft amount uses current-period statement line subtotal plus current-period adjustment items. Historical arrears, total balances, and opening balances remain reference adjustments and must not be silently folded into the current-period payable draft. Generating the draft sets `payableStatus=待财务复核`, keeps `paymentStatus=待财务付款确认`, and still does not write raw-material inventory or confirm payment. Actual supplier payment requires a later separate confirmation / payment transaction, permission, operation log, and field-evidence rule.

## 2026-07-04 - Supplier Statement Confirmation Is Separate From Payment

Supplier monthly statement review drafts may now become formal reconciliation confirmations, but this is still not a payment event. A supplier statement can only be confirmed after the draft has been manually reviewed as consistent. The confirmation writes a `RMSRC-*` statement confirmation id, sets the statement status to `已确认对账/待付款`, and marks `paymentStatus=待财务付款确认`.

This confirmation must remain a narrow audit / handoff boundary: it does not create raw-material inventory, does not create or settle a supplier payable ledger, and does not confirm payment. Supplier deductions, tube/core adjustments, payable generation, and payment confirmation require separate transactions, permissions, and later field-evidence rules. This keeps the office reconciliation workflow moving while preventing a supplier Excel upload or review click from silently becoming a financial write path.

## 2026-07-04 - Persist Supplier Statement Prechecks As Review-Only Drafts

Supplier monthly statement prechecks should be durable review records, not only temporary UI output. The first V1 boundary stores each precheck result as a `RMSR-*` raw-material supplier statement review draft through `rawMaterialSupplierStatementReviewRepository`; local development uses `local_json`, while production profile uses PostgreSQL `raw_material_supplier_statement_reviews.payload_json` and operation logs. These drafts may preserve adapter identity, file name, supplier, matched / candidate / unmatched rows, adjustments, issues, summary counts, reviewer decision, and audit fields.

The boundary remains review-only. Saving or marking a review draft does not create usable raw-material inventory, does not create supplier payables, does not confirm supplier reconciliation for accounting, and does not register payment. The draft may be marked reviewed as `已人工复核/一致` or `已人工复核/有差异`, but any future cost allocation, payable generation, supplier settlement, or payment confirmation must use a separate transaction, permission, and field-evidence rule. This prevents a supplier Excel upload from silently becoming a financial or inventory write path while still giving office staff a traceable handoff object for manual reconciliation.

## 2026-07-04 - Persist Raw-Material Inbound As API-First Payloads Before Full Costing

Raw-material inbound should now be treated as an API-owned workflow, not only a local UI prototype. V1 hardening stores each supplier delivery-note receiving record, roll / piece label state, signed-note status, and action history through `rawMaterialInboundRepository`; local development uses `local_json`, while production profile uses PostgreSQL `raw_material_inbounds.payload_json` plus operation logs. This keeps the first receiving workflow durable enough for office review, roll-label printing, and attach-confirm permissions without prematurely designing the final normalized costing ledger.

The business boundary remains strict: OCR review and label printing do not create usable inventory. A roll / piece becomes available raw material only after the physical label is attached to the matching material and an authorized phone scan / signed-note confirmation is recorded. The JSONB payload table is an intentional V1 bridge; later costing work may split supplier delivery notes, roll labels, issue / consumption records, paper-tube deductions, supplier reconciliation rows, and cost allocation into normalized tables after more real samples and field evidence are collected.

## 2026-07-04 - Include Runtime Identity In The V1 System Persistence Gate

Imported employee runtime identity is a production persistence boundary, not just an auth helper. `runtimeIdentityRepository` stores employee password state, lockout / expiry metadata, login enablement, `sessionVersion`, and seed-session revocation IDs, so the V1 system-persistence readiness gate must check it alongside master-data, audit, print, attachment, order, inventory, fulfillment, statement, production, and driver repositories. Production V1 remains expected to use PostgreSQL for this repository; local JSON is only acceptable when the explicit local-persistence V1 acceptance switch is set. The readiness output may expose repository key, label, kind, production-ready flags, and counts, but must not expose password hashes, token IDs, connection strings, local paths, or user payloads. This prevents a release candidate from looking production-ready while imported employee login state would still be outside the system persistence gate.

## 2026-07-04 - Raw-Material Inbound Requires Roll Labels And Phone Signoff

Raw-material inbound should follow the actual receiving sequence rather than treating OCR or printing as inventory completion. The V1 flow is: the raw-material supplier delivery/sales note arrives with the goods, staff upload a phone photo, OCR/recognition creates a raw-material inbound draft, customer-service / office receiving staff compare the raw-material note, recognition result, and physical supplier label, the system splits the inbound line into one roll/piece ID per roll/piece, office or print-authorized staff print one label per roll/piece, the label is physically attached to the matching material roll, and the label-attachment operator uses a phone scan / confirmation plus signed-note upload before the roll becomes available inventory. This note is the supplier document for incoming raw materials, not this factory's finished-goods outbound / customer delivery note. Numbering has two layers: supplier document number is optional and should be recorded when present but left blank when absent, while ERP inbound ID and roll/piece IDs are mandatory system-owned keys for internal search, labeling, inventory, issuing, and cost traceability. Label printing is only a pending-attachment state; OCR and printed labels do not by themselves create usable raw-material inventory. Confirmed inbound snapshots feed later supplier monthly reconciliation, but reconciliation confirmation remains separate from payment.

The first ERP UI implementation follows the same boundary: the `原材料` page may show OCR prefill, review, label print, attach confirmation, supplier statement matching summary, and roll / piece status in local UI state, but it must keep cost fields permission-gated and must not treat label printing as usable inventory. Roll / piece attachment confirmation is a separate raw-material permission because ordinary order-entry or finance work should not silently turn supplier material into available stock. This first UI loop is not a substitute for real OCR, persistent inbound APIs, physical label-printer QA, phone scan / signed-note field evidence, raw-material issuing, cost allocation, or margin reporting.

## 2026-07-04 - Normalize Supplier Statements Before Raw-Material Reconciliation

Supplier monthly statements should be imported through supplier-specific template adapters and normalized before matching ERP inbound records. Baihou-style statements use one `对账单` sheet with `重1` to `重5` per-roll weights and supplier-specific footer adjustments such as tube/core deduction; each weight column should become a roll-level reconciliation row while footer balances stay as adjustments/reference. Beichen-style statements use `每日发货明细` plus `每日发货统计`, with positive shipment rows carrying `批号` as a strong supplier roll/batch key and a separate shifted return section that must be parsed by detected headers. Automatic matching should prefer supplier roll/batch number, then supplier document number plus line/roll sequence when present. When the supplier did not provide a document number, matching should use ERP inbound ID / source photo plus supplier, received date/time, spec, color, piece/roll count, per-roll weight, unit price, and amount as candidates; time and quantity alone are not enough for automatic confirmation. Low-confidence matches, historical arrears, and total arrears must not auto-confirm current-period inbound costs or payments.

## 2026-07-04 - Keep Supplier Statement Imports As Review-Only Prechecks

The first supplier-statement import implementation is a review-only precheck, not an accounting write path. Uploading a Baihou, Beichen, or generic supplier monthly Excel file may normalize rows, split Baihou `重1`-`重5` into roll-level rows, identify Beichen `批号` matches, flag adjustments, and show matched / candidate / unmatched ERP raw-material inbounds, but it must not create usable inventory, create payables, confirm supplier reconciliation, or register payment. Baihou `重1` / `重2` style column labels are roll sequence / weight labels only and must not be treated as supplier roll numbers for strong matching; only real batch / roll fields such as Beichen `批号` can carry that confidence. Rows without supplier document numbers may become candidate matches from supplier, spec, color, weight, unit price, and amount evidence, but require human confirmation before any future payable or cost effect.

## 2026-07-04 - Audit Real Production Env Files Before V1 Preflight And Release Candidate

Real V1 production env files must be audited before preflight and whenever a release-candidate run is given `--env-file`. The audit is separate from variable completeness: it verifies that the env file is not a checked-in template or documentation file, is not tracked by git, is either inside an ignored path or outside the workspace, has no unresolved `<REPLACE_WITH_...>` / `<OPTIONAL_...>` placeholders, and reports duplicate variables or broad file permissions as warnings. The release-candidate gate count stays stable, but the production environment gate requires both variable preflight and env-file audit readiness; a template-like or unsafe env file must keep the release candidate blocked even if all variables are present. The audit may expose file paths, variable names, counts, statuses, and next actions, but must never print env values, comments, raw lines, connection strings, object-storage endpoints or buckets, access keys, secret keys, command paths, command args, spool paths, tokens, or payloads. This reduces the risk of leaking production secrets or falsely approving an unsafe env file during V1 handoff; it does not replace real PostgreSQL, object storage, CUPS, printer hardware, driver phones, field evidence, or signoff.

## 2026-07-04 - Apply V1 Lockout And Expiry To Imported Employee Runtime Accounts

Imported employee runtime accounts need a first account-security boundary before field use. V1 applies this to imported runtime accounts, not to built-in seed demo users: five consecutive failed login attempts lock the account for 15 minutes, and the lock state is persisted through `runtimeIdentityRepository`. Active employee passwords expire after 90 days; an expired-password login keeps identity visible but marks `passwordStatus=password_expired`, sets `mustChangePassword=true`, and returns no business permissions until the employee changes password. Admin temporary-password reissue clears failure / lockout / expiry state while still invalidating old sessions through `sessionVersion`; revoke clears password and lockout fields. PostgreSQL mode stores these extra fields in `users.metadata_json` rather than adding new V1 migration columns. This closes the first lockout / expiry gap for imported employees, while production identity provider integration, real account migration, operations runbooks, field acceptance, and signoff remain separate V1 work.

## 2026-07-04 - Normalize Master-Data Excel Date Cells Before Staging

Master-data import precheck should normalize workbook dates before they enter review drafts, confirmation plans, execution payloads, or failed-row correction loops. Excel / WPS may store `生效日期` and `盘点日期` as serial-number cells, while users may type common text forms such as `2026/7/1`, `2026.7.1`, or `2026年7月1日`; these should become stable `YYYY-MM-DD` strings in staged rows. This normalization is limited to explicit date fields so unit prices, quantities, inventory counts, machine counts, and other numeric business fields are never reinterpreted as dates. The rule reduces V1 real-data import mistakes but still requires real sample acceptance, formal permissions, rollback, field evidence, and signoff before V1 can be complete.

## 2026-07-04 - Keep Independent Master-Data Maintenance As Non-Writing Drafts

The independent `基础资料` page is the first backend-editing surface for customers, price snapshots, inventory/spec records, and employee / machine records, but it must not directly mutate formal master data in V1. Page edits create `MDM-*` maintenance drafts with the selected record, field, proposed value, reason, and operator context. The permission maps to the planning side of master-data maintenance (`master_data.import.plan.create`) and is intentionally separate from `master_data.import.execute`. Formal writes, especially prices, inventory, permissions, and employee account state, still require the existing confirmation-plan / execution chain. This closes the first independent maintenance-page gap while preserving the high-risk import / confirmation boundary.

## 2026-07-04 - Count Only Edited Failed Rows As Corrected

Master-data failed-row correction drafts may include inline field edits, but the system should count only touched rows as corrected. If a user opens the failed-row editor and does not modify a row, that row remains `unresolved` and the correction draft stays in manual-review status. This prevents the UI from turning downloaded failed-row content into an apparently clean re-import just because the row was displayed. The correction draft remains non-writing and must still re-enter the confirmation-plan / formal-import chain; broader Excel compatibility, independent maintenance pages, real data import acceptance, production deployment, and full lockout / expiry controls remain separate V1 work.

## 2026-07-04 - Keep Failed-Row Reimport As A Non-Writing Correction Draft Loop

Master-data import failed rows should re-enter the existing audit path as a new `MDI-*` review draft, not bypass confirmation or write directly. `POST /api/master-data/import-executions/{executionId}/failed-rows/correction-draft` uses the same `master_data.import.plan.create` permission as failed-row download, preserves the source execution / plan / failed-row reasons, and saves an operation log under `master_data_import_review_draft`. Drafts can then use the existing generate-plan and formal-import chain; actual writes still require `master_data.import.execute`. This closes the first V1 failed-row correction loop; broader external Excel compatibility, real data import acceptance, independent maintenance pages, and full lockout / expiry controls remain separate work.

## 2026-07-04 - Enforce V1 Imported-Employee Password Policy And Admin Revoke UI

Imported employee runtime accounts must use an explicit V1 password policy when changing the visible-once temporary password: at least 10 characters, at least one letter, at least one number, no whitespace, and no login name / user ID / employee ID substring. Policy failures should return `passwordPolicy` details so future employee-facing change-password UI can show the same rule instead of hard-coded text. The master-data employee review UI is also the V1 management surface for password reset and revoke: management can reissue a temporary password or revoke login from the same review row, and revoked rows must visibly show password state so an enabled employee profile is not mistaken for an enabled login. This closes the first V1 policy and admin-entry gap; production deployment, field acceptance, broader Excel compatibility, independent maintenance pages, and full lockout / expiry controls remain separate work.

## 2026-07-04 - Persist Runtime Imported Employee Identity And Seed-Session Revocations

Imported employee runtime accounts and seed-session revocations should not depend on the current Node process. `runtimeIdentityRepository` is the V1 boundary for this state: default `local_json` mode persists imported runtime users and revoked token `jti` records under `.erp-local-storage/metadata/runtime-identity.json`, while PostgreSQL mode stores imported runtime users in `users` and revoked seed sessions in `seed_session_revocations`. The unified V1 persistence profile should include `runtimeIdentityRepositoryOptions` so production profile runs move this state to PostgreSQL with the rest of the core repositories. The repository may persist password hashes and token IDs internally, but generated reports and API responses must not expose plaintext passwords or password hashes. This closes the first persistence gap for imported-employee auth; production deployment, field acceptance, broader Excel compatibility, independent maintenance pages, and full lockout / expiry controls remain separate work.

## 2026-07-04 - Revoke Imported Employee Sessions On Password Reset Or Revoke

Imported employee runtime sessions should become invalid when management reissues a temporary password or revokes the employee password. Runtime employee seed-session tokens therefore carry the account `sessionVersion`, and password issue / reset / revoke operations increment the stored version. A token whose version no longer matches the runtime user is rejected with `AUTH_TOKEN_REVOKED`; a revoked password also clears the runtime password hash, disables login, and marks `passwordStatus=password_revoked`. `/api/auth/logout` may also revoke the current token `jti`, with persistence handled by `runtimeIdentityRepository`. This is a V1 runtime boundary, not the final production identity system: production deployment, field acceptance, broader Excel compatibility, independent maintenance pages, and full lockout / expiry controls remain separate work.

## 2026-07-04 - Require Imported Employees To Change Temporary Passwords Before Business Access

Imported employee runtime accounts that receive a visible-once temporary password must not receive business permissions until the employee changes that password. While `mustChangePassword=true`, seed-session permission responses should keep the account identity visible but return no action or button permissions and expose `passwordChangeRequired=true`. The first-password-change API must verify the current temporary password, reject weak or repeated new passwords, clear the force-change state, sync the employee account review projection, write an operation log without password values, and make the old temporary password invalid. This is a V1 runtime-account boundary only; production deployment, field acceptance, broader Excel compatibility, independent maintenance pages, and full lockout / expiry controls remain separate work.

## 2026-07-04 - Make Go-Live Suite The Module Completion Entry Point

The V1 go-live suite should answer both "can V1 be declared complete" and "how complete is each ERP module" without requiring a reader to open a separate module-status document. `scripts/run-v1-completion-snapshot.mjs` should expose parsed `moduleCompletion` rows in its JSON command result, and `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs` should carry those rows into suite JSON / Markdown, including module count, low V1-readiness highlights, and the full module table. These numbers remain status communication, not release approval: V1 completion still requires release-candidate READY, field-evidence manifest READY, onsite task clearance, signoff, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Generate Standalone Production Env Fix Checklists In Handoff Packs

V1 go-live handoff packs should include standalone, redacted production-environment fix checklist files generated from the release-candidate `envPreflight.fixChecklist`. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs` should write both `production-env-fix-checklist.zh-CN.md` and `production-env-fix-checklist.csv`, list them in the handoff manifest / file index, and summarize the counts in `handoff-summary.zh-CN.md`. These files may expose owner role, required variable names, configured / total counts, missing variable names, placeholder variable names, severity / status, and next action, but must not copy real env values, connection strings, endpoints, buckets, access keys, secret keys, command paths, command args, spool paths, tokens, payloads, or raw deployment details. This is a handoff execution aid only; it does not replace production configuration, real hardware / device evidence, field-evidence READY, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Carry Production Env Fix Checklists Into Release Candidates

V1 production environment preflight should produce an actionable, redacted fix checklist, and the release-candidate report should preserve it. `scripts/run-v1-production-env-preflight.mjs` may expose `fixChecklist` items with owner role, required variable names, configured / total counts, missing variable names, placeholder variable names, severity, and next action. `scripts/run-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs` should copy a sanitized `envPreflight.fixChecklist` into release-candidate JSON and Markdown so handoff packs and owner reviews can assign environment work without rerunning preflight. The checklist must remain non-mutating and redacted: no real database URLs, object-storage endpoints, bucket names, access keys, secret keys, command paths, command args, spool paths, tokens, payloads, or raw env values. This improves V1 deployment execution clarity only; it does not replace real PostgreSQL / object storage / CUPS / printer / driver-phone evidence, field-evidence READY, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Ship Field-Evidence Intake Rules And Redacted Fix Hints

V1 onsite evidence collection should include a rules file and actionable, redacted error hints. `scripts/run-v1-field-evidence-intake-pack.mjs` must generate `intake-rules.zh-CN.md` with allowed CSV statuses, required fields, recommended evidence / timestamp formats, apply / validate / go-live suite commands, and safety boundaries. `scripts/apply-v1-field-evidence-intake.mjs` may expose `fixHint` for invalid rows, but those hints must not include raw evidence refs, signer names, connection strings, object-storage credentials, command paths, spool paths, tokens, or customer payloads. The go-live handoff pack should copy the rules file when a field-evidence intake pack is present. This reduces onsite data-entry mistakes; it does not replace real evidence, release-candidate READY, field-evidence READY, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Keep Go-Live Suite Latest Artifacts Time-Consistent

The V1 go-live suite should be able to refresh the top-level local `latest` artifact directories from the same suite run. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs --sync-canonical-latest` copies the same-run redacted onsite task board, completion snapshot, V1/V2 scope brief, owner decision brief, field-evidence intake pack, and handoff pack to their canonical `.erp-local-storage/v1-*` directories. A separate `--canonical-root` exists for tests so validation does not overwrite real project artifacts. When syncing the field-evidence intake pack, existing `evidence-items.csv`, `signoff-boundary.csv`, and `filled-manifest.draft.json` are preserved because they may contain onsite operator input. This sync is a reporting consistency rule only: it must not mutate source field-evidence manifests, change evidence statuses, promote pending evidence, reveal raw evidence refs or signers, or override release-candidate READY, field-evidence READY, onsite task clearance, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Reject Unresolved Production Env Template Placeholders

V1 production environment preflight must not treat unresolved template placeholders as configured values. Values such as `<REPLACE_WITH_...>`, `<OPTIONAL_...>`, or strings containing `REPLACE_WITH_` are placeholders and should behave like missing configuration in `scripts/run-v1-production-env-preflight.mjs`. The preflight may report placeholder counts and variable-name status, but it must not print placeholder text, real connection strings, object-storage endpoints, bucket names, access keys, secret keys, command paths, command args, spool paths, tokens, or payloads. This rule prevents a copied-and-uncommented env template from falsely passing release-candidate gates; it does not replace real production configuration or field acceptance.

## 2026-07-04 - Treat Signoff And V1/V2 Boundary Intake As Structured Gate Data

V1 signoffs and V1/V2 boundary confirmation should not stay as a Markdown-only checklist. The field-evidence intake pack must generate `signoff-boundary.csv` alongside `evidence-items.csv`, and the intake apply command must be able to merge that CSV into a new draft manifest without mutating the source manifest. Signed or accepted signoff rows require both signer and signed-at values; confirmed V1/V2 boundary rows require both confirmed-by and confirmed-at values. Console and generated reports may include fill-state booleans and counts, but must not print raw signer names, raw evidence refs, real env values, command paths, spool paths, payloads, or secrets. The go-live suite may consume both CSVs before generating downstream reports, but final V1 completion still requires refreshed release-candidate gates, full field evidence, all required signoffs, onsite task clearance, and confirmed V1/V2 boundary.

## 2026-07-04 - Include The Generated V1/V2 Brief In Handoff Packs

The V1 go-live handoff pack should include the generated owner-facing V1/V2 scope brief, not only the source V1/V2 scope document. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs` should accept explicit V1/V2 brief JSON / Markdown paths, auto-detect `.erp-local-storage/v1-v2-scope-brief/latest.*` when present, copy the files into the handoff directory, and summarize V1 must-continue work, V2 difference counts, V2 categories, and owner review rules in the handoff summary. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs` should pass the same-run V1/V2 brief outputs to the handoff pack, so suite artifacts remain time-consistent. This is a handoff clarity rule only; it does not override release-candidate READY, field-evidence manifest READY, onsite task clearance, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Generate A V1/V2 Scope Brief For Owner Review

V1/V2 boundary communication should have a generated, repeatable brief instead of relying on scattered wording across release reports. `scripts/run-v1-v2-scope-brief.mjs` should read the V1/V2 scope document and the current completion snapshot, then write `.erp-local-storage/v1-v2-scope-brief/latest.zh-CN.md` / `latest.json` with V1 must-continue work, planned V2 differences, V2 categories, and the owner review rule. The go-live suite should include this brief as a first-class step. The brief is non-mutating and redacted; it must never imply that production persistence, real printers, driver phones, field evidence, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation can be postponed to V2.

## 2026-07-04 - Let The Go-Live Suite Apply Field Evidence CSVs Into Drafts

When onsite owners fill the V1 field-evidence intake CSV, the go-live suite may apply that CSV into a draft manifest before generating handoff materials. The suite must preserve the source manifest, use the draft only as the active manifest for downstream reports, and warn when the release-candidate JSON has not been refreshed after applying the draft. Final go/no-go runs must pass `--refresh-release-candidate` so release gates are recalculated from the draft manifest. A CSV-applied draft can improve the evidence count in task boards, handoff packs, and refreshed release-candidate summaries, but it must not bypass the release-candidate gate, field-evidence validation, required signoffs, onsite task clearance, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Use One Go-Live Suite Runner For V1 Completion Handoff

V1 completion handoff should be reproducible from one orchestration command after the release-candidate report exists. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-suite.mjs` should read the release-candidate JSON and field-evidence manifest, then generate the onsite task board, completion snapshot, owner decision brief, field-evidence intake pack, and go-live handoff pack under `.erp-local-storage/v1-go-live-suite/`. The suite is a redacted, non-mutating command-chain guard: it may summarize current blockers and V2 differences, but it must not change field-evidence statuses, print raw evidence refs, reveal signer names, expose env values, command paths, spool paths, payloads, or secrets, and it cannot override the real approval gates of release-candidate READY, field-evidence manifest READY, onsite task clearance, signoff, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Use A Generated Owner Decision Brief For V1 Completion Claims

V1 completion claims should be made from a generated owner-facing brief derived from the current completion snapshot, not from manual status wording. `scripts/run-v1-owner-decision-brief.mjs` should read the completion snapshot and write `.erp-local-storage/v1-owner-decision-brief/latest.zh-CN.md` / `latest.json` with a direct completion decision, completed highlights, unfinished items, release gates, next actions, and V2 differences. The go-live handoff pack should copy this brief when present. The brief is communication evidence only: it must remain redacted and non-mutating, and it cannot override release-candidate READY, field-evidence manifest READY, onsite task clearance, signoff, or V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Apply Field-Evidence Intake CSVs Only Into Draft Manifests

Filled V1 field-evidence intake CSV files should be merged into a new draft manifest, never into the source manifest in place. `scripts/apply-v1-field-evidence-intake.mjs` should require an explicit `onsiteStatus` for every row it applies, require `onsiteEvidenceRef` for `passed` / `accepted`, reject sensitive-looking values, and keep stdout redacted. Missing status, blank rows, or sensitive refs must not auto-pass evidence. The output draft still has to pass `scripts/validate-v1-field-evidence-manifest.mjs`, receive required signoffs, and pass the release-candidate gate before V1 can be called complete.

## 2026-07-04 - Split The Production Frontend Bundle By Stable Vite Chunks

The production frontend build should avoid one oversized application chunk as the ERP prototype grows. `vite.config.mjs` should use Vite / Rollup `manualChunks` to split React, Ant Design icons, ERP pages, ERP runtime services/state, domain rules, seed data, and remaining vendor modules into stable chunks. `state` and `services` should stay in the same `erp-runtime` chunk because splitting them separately creates a circular chunk warning. This is a build-shape optimization only; it must not change routing, UI behavior, permissions, data parsing, or V1 readiness gates.

## 2026-07-04 - Generate Redacted Field-Evidence Intake Packs Before Signoff

V1 field evidence should be collected through a redacted intake pack before teams edit the filled manifest or claim readiness. `scripts/run-v1-field-evidence-intake-pack.mjs` should read the current field-evidence manifest and optionally include the release-candidate report, onsite task board, and completion snapshot, then write `.erp-local-storage/v1-field-evidence-intake/` with a summary, machine-readable index, CSV, signoff / V1-V2 boundary sheet, and one Markdown sheet per evidence group. The go-live handoff pack should copy this intake pack when present. This pack is non-mutating and must never change `pending` evidence to `passed`, print raw `evidenceRef` values, print signer names, print notes, or expose env values, command paths, spool paths, payloads, or secrets. It is a field execution aid only; release-candidate READY, complete field evidence, signoff, and boundary confirmation remain the approval gates.

## 2026-07-04 - Generate A Redacted V1 Completion Snapshot For Status Communication

V1 status should be communicated through a generated snapshot rather than repeated manual estimates. `scripts/run-v1-completion-snapshot.mjs` should read the current release-candidate report, onsite task board, module completion document, and V1/V2 scope document, then write a redacted Markdown / JSON snapshot with requirement confirmation, P0 prototype/code completion, V1 go-live readiness, release gates, onsite task counts, blocker groups, and planned V2 differences. The go-live handoff pack should copy this snapshot when present. This snapshot is for communication and field handoff only: it must not print secrets, raw evidence refs, signer names, command paths, spool paths, or payloads, and it must not replace the actual approval gates of release-candidate READY, field evidence manifest ready, onsite task clearance, signoff, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation.

## 2026-07-04 - Distribute V1 Onsite Tasks As Per-Role Markdown Files

The combined V1 onsite task board should also produce one Markdown file per active role, so each field group can work from a focused list instead of scanning a long shared report. Files should be written under `.erp-local-storage/v1-onsite-task-board/roles/*.latest.md` and copied into the go-live handoff pack under `onsite-task-board-roles/` when present. These files must keep the same redaction rules as the combined board: no raw evidence refs, signer names, real env values, command paths, spool paths, payloads, or secrets. Per-role files are execution aids only; release-candidate READY, complete field evidence, signoff, and V1/V2 boundary confirmation remain the approval gates.

## 2026-07-04 - Include Role Task Boards In V1 Handoff Packs

V1 handoff packs should carry the role-based onsite task board snapshot when it exists, so field teams do not need to chase separate files after a blocked release-candidate run. `scripts/run-v1-go-live-handoff-pack.mjs` should auto-detect `.erp-local-storage/v1-onsite-task-board/latest.md` and `latest.json`, allow explicit task-board paths, copy the snapshot into the handoff directory, and summarize task totals and role buckets in the handoff summary / manifest. This must remain a redacted execution aid: task-board inclusion does not change READY criteria, and the handoff pack must still avoid raw evidence refs, signer names, real env values, command paths, spool paths, and secrets.

## 2026-07-04 - Split V1 Remaining Work Into Redacted Role-Based Onsite Tasks

V1 field execution should not depend on one long release report or one long evidence manifest. `scripts/run-v1-onsite-task-board.mjs` should read the current release-candidate report and field-evidence manifest, then produce a redacted task board grouped by technical / management, office, warehouse / fulfillment, workshop, driver, and finance roles. The task board may include blocker labels, evidence item labels, status, and next action, but it must not print raw evidence refs, signer names, real env values, command paths, spool paths, or secrets. It is an execution aid after a blocked release-candidate run, not a readiness bypass: V1 is still complete only when release-candidate gates, field evidence, signoff, and boundary confirmation are ready.

## 2026-07-04 - Generate V1 Handoff Packs From Redacted Release Evidence

V1 go-live handoff should be generated from the current release-candidate report, field-evidence manifest validation, the production env template, runbook, checklist, and V1/V2 scope document. The default handoff pack must not copy real env files and must not print raw `evidenceRef` values; it writes a redacted field-evidence manifest snapshot and requires an explicit `--include-raw-field-evidence` flag before copying the raw manifest for internal retention. This keeps the site handoff concrete enough for office / warehouse / workshop / driver / finance / technical review while reducing the chance that connection strings, object-storage credentials, command paths, spool paths, ticket links, or private evidence paths are shared in ordinary status reports.

## 2026-07-04 - Make Field Evidence A Release-Candidate Gate

The V1 release-candidate report must not become READY unless a filled field-evidence manifest is provided and validates as ready. `scripts/run-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs` therefore treats the field-evidence manifest as a release gate alongside production environment preflight, runtime readiness, and the field-acceptance report. If no filled manifest is passed, the runner validates the checked-in template and remains blocked by default. The gate may include only manifest path, status, summaries, blocker labels, and safeguard counts in the release report; it must not print raw evidence refs because they may contain sensitive paths, ticket links, or deployment details. This makes the final V1 go/no-go stricter and prevents a lab-ready or report-generated state from being mistaken for onsite acceptance.

## 2026-07-04 - Validate V1 Field Evidence Through A Manifest Before Signoff

V1 field acceptance should be checked through a structured manifest before anyone describes the system as complete. `docs/development/v1-field-evidence-manifest.template.json` is the template for onsite evidence, and `scripts/validate-v1-field-evidence-manifest.mjs` is the gate for filled manifests. Required evidence includes production PostgreSQL migration / backup / restore proof, object-storage upload and retention proof, real CUPS and printer hardware proof, driver phone / native bridge proof, real workflow pilot proof, security / operations controls, six role signoffs, and explicit V1/V2 boundary confirmation. The template must keep every required item `pending` by default, and the validator must return blocked until evidence refs, statuses, signoffs, and boundary confirmation are complete. Evidence refs are identifiers only; validator output must not print raw refs because they may contain paths, ticket links, or sensitive deployment details. Passing this manifest check is still not a bypass for the release-candidate gate; it is the field-evidence input needed before V1 approval.

## 2026-07-04 - Keep V1 Production Env Templates Redacted And Generated

V1 production configuration should have a maintained template and runbook, but those files must not contain real secrets or local command paths. `scripts/generate-v1-production-env-template.mjs` is the source for `docs/development/v1-production.env.example` and `docs/development/v1-go-live-runbook.zh-CN.md`. The env example keeps all values commented and uses placeholders only; operators must copy it to a secure untracked file before entering real database URLs, object-storage credentials, command paths, spool paths, tokens, or hostnames. `scripts/check-v1-production-env-template.mjs` should stay in CI-style validation so changes to preflight requirements do not leave the template stale. Passing the template check means the deployment checklist is complete and redacted, not that any real environment is configured.

## 2026-07-04 - Use One V1 Release-Candidate Gate For Go/No-Go Communication

V1 status should be explainable through one release-candidate report, not only through separate environment, runtime, and field-acceptance checks. `scripts/run-v1-release-candidate-check.mjs` is the combined gate: it runs the production environment preflight, runs the top-level API readiness check, generates the field-acceptance Markdown / JSON package, and then writes a release-candidate Markdown / JSON report that states READY or BLOCKED, lists current blockers, records the V1 scope, and lists planned V2 differences. The report is a communication and audit artifact, not a bypass. A READY result still requires responsible people to review real production configuration and onsite evidence; a BLOCKED result must not be described as V1 complete just because a report was generated.

## 2026-07-04 - Run A Redacted Production Environment Preflight Before V1 Readiness

V1 deployment should fail early when required production environment variables are absent or malformed. `scripts/run-v1-production-env-preflight.mjs` is the pre-start configuration gate for the current prototype backend: it checks PostgreSQL V1 persistence profile selection, object-storage profile and credentials, system-printer command bridge settings, CUPS non-printing preflight settings, readiness identities, field-acceptance report output, and local-persistence bypass flags. The preflight must stay non-mutating and redacted: it may report variable names, counts, booleans, statuses, and next actions, but it must not expose database URLs, endpoints, bucket names, access keys, secret keys, command paths, command args, spool paths, or payloads. Passing this preflight only means the environment is syntactically prepared for V1 checks; the API must still be started and pass runtime readiness, object-storage live diagnostics, print hardware QA, driver-phone QA, and field acceptance.

## 2026-07-04 - Persist V1 Field-Acceptance Reports Without Treating Them As Go-Live Approval

V1 readiness should produce a durable handoff artifact for field work, not only console output. `scripts/run-v1-field-acceptance-report.mjs` writes a redacted Markdown / JSON report from the top-level V1 readiness runner, including module status, blockers, required onsite evidence, safeguards, and next actions. The report may be generated while blocked for audit and planning, but a blocked report must still exit non-zero by default and must state that V1 is not complete. Even a ready report is a candidate acceptance package, not automatic go-live approval; real production credentials, object-storage policy, CUPS queues, printer sample output, driver phones, business signoff, and onsite evidence still decide final release.

## 2026-07-04 - Treat Automated 11/11 V1 Readiness As Lab Evidence, Not Field Acceptance

The combined V1 production-profile live check may prove that all 11 top-level readiness criteria can pass under PostgreSQL, object storage, command-bridge print diagnostics, CUPS queue preflight, printer QA evidence, and driver native-device QA evidence. This positive path is useful as a code and deployment-shape compatibility gate, but it is still automated lab evidence. It must not be used to claim real V1 go-live completion until the same gates are run with real production PostgreSQL / object-storage credentials, real CUPS queues, real label and dot-matrix printers, physical paper output, barcode scans, void/reprint evidence, real driver phones, native shell permissions, map opening, watermarked photos, and operator field acceptance.

## 2026-07-04 - Persist Master-Data Import Review State In PostgreSQL For V1

Master-data import review state is part of V1 production persistence, not disposable prototype state. Review drafts, confirmation plans, import executions, and their audit logs should support PostgreSQL in addition to the local JSON prototype store. The first PostgreSQL boundary stores common lookup fields such as IDs, status, file name, operator, timestamps, and last execution status in dedicated columns, while preserving the full workbook-derived plan/execution payload in JSONB. This keeps V1 persistence reliable without prematurely over-normalizing every imported workbook row. Future V2 maintenance and analytics can split more fields into relational tables after the V1 import and rollback workflow is stable.

## 2026-07-04 - Verify The Combined V1 Production Profile Before Field Gates

V1 production persistence should be tested as a combined runtime shape, not only as separate PostgreSQL and object-storage unit checks. `scripts/check-v1-production-profile-live.mjs` is the automated compatibility gate for that shape: it starts temporary PostgreSQL, applies migrations, starts a local S3-compatible endpoint, starts the ERP API with the unified V1 persistence profile, and verifies system persistence readiness, attachment retention readiness, baseline top-level readiness, and a positive automated field-gate path. The baseline phase must still report print and driver gates as blocked when field evidence is absent; the positive phase may reach `11/11 passed` only after explicit automated printer and driver QA evidence is inserted. A passing result proves the ERP can leave local memory / local JSON / local filesystem modes and that the code gates can close under a production-profile shape, but it must not be treated as full go-live approval.

## 2026-07-04 - Keep Object-Storage Live Checks Separate From Production Bucket Acceptance

Attachment object-storage checks should include an HTTP-level S3-compatible live path, not only unit-level signing assertions. `scripts/check-attachment-object-storage-live.mjs` may start a local S3-compatible endpoint and the ERP API in `object_storage` mode to prove upload, readback, signed URL generation, storage diagnostics, V1 attachment readiness, cleanup, audit logging, SigV4 header presence, and redaction. This is a V1 code and deployment-shape compatibility check, not proof that the real production OSS/S3/COS bucket is ready. Production readiness still requires real credentials, bucket policy, network access, lifecycle / retention rules, backup monitoring, virus scanning, resumable upload decisions, and field acceptance evidence.

## 2026-07-04 - Use A Unified V1 Persistence Profile For Production Startup

Production V1 persistence should not depend on manually remembering every individual `*_STORE=postgres` repository switch. The API server should support one explicit, redacted profile for supported production persistence defaults: `v1PersistenceProfile` / `persistenceProfile` in code, or `ERP_V1_PERSISTENCE_PROFILE=postgres`, `ERP_V1_FILE_STORAGE_PROFILE=object_storage`, and `ERP_V1_DATABASE_URL` in deployment. The profile may set supported repository factories to PostgreSQL and file-retention stores to object storage only when the caller has not already supplied a concrete repository object or explicit per-repository `mode`; explicit overrides remain authoritative for tests, local development, and staged cutovers. Health and readiness responses may expose only the profile name, applied/skipped counts, and whether a database URL is configured. They must not expose connection strings, local paths, object-storage endpoints, bucket secrets, access keys, or session tokens. This profile lowers V1 deployment risk, but it is not a readiness bypass: a repository counts as production-persistence ready only when its PostgreSQL/object-storage implementation and live verification exist, and field gates such as print and driver QA still require their own evidence.

## 2026-07-04 - Gate System V1 Persistence Separately From Prototype Health

Prototype health and V1 production persistence are separate decisions. `/api/health` may expose repository kinds for diagnostics, but it is not a go-live gate. `GET /api/system/v1-readiness` must be the blocking V1 persistence gate for core repositories across order, inventory, fulfillment, statements, production, driver, attachments, print, master data, and file-retention stores. `local_memory`, `local_json`, and `local_fs` must be treated as blocked for production V1 by default; `local_memory` is especially risky because process restart can lose runtime business state. The only two ways to pass this gate are to run production-grade persistence such as PostgreSQL / object storage, or to explicitly accept local persistence through server configuration with a documented risk reference. The readiness response should stay read-only and redacted: repository kinds and counts are allowed, but business payloads, connection strings, local paths, and secrets are not.

## 2026-07-04 - Gate Attachment V1 Retention Separately From Storage Diagnostics

Attachment storage diagnostics and V1 retention readiness are separate decisions. `GET /api/attachments/storage-diagnostics`, guarded by `attachment.view`, proves only that the current adapter can write, read, verify, and clean up a diagnostic object without exposing secrets. `GET /api/attachments/v1-readiness` is the go-live gate: a local filesystem adapter may pass diagnostics, but it must remain blocked for V1 production retention unless local filesystem retention is explicitly accepted through server configuration or `ERP_ATTACHMENT_LOCAL_FS_V1_ACCEPTED`. Real object storage remains the default production path. The top-level `scripts/run-v1-readiness-check.mjs` must include this as a blocking criterion so a normal development folder is not mistaken for OSS/S3/COS readiness. This gate still does not complete credential rotation, network policy, lifecycle rules, virus scanning, resumable upload, backup monitoring, or field acceptance.

## 2026-07-04 - Check Real CUPS Queues Through A Non-Printing CLI First

Field machines should have a direct CUPS queue preflight command before any physical sample print or ERP go-live claim. The command is `scripts/run-cups-queue-preflight.mjs`, wrapping `scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs --action cups-preflight`. It may run a real queue status command such as `lpstat -p {cupsPrinterName}` on the host, but it must not read order payload, create a print file, submit a print job, expose command paths, expose stdout/stderr contents, expose spool paths, or claim paper output. A passing direct CUPS queue preflight means only that the queue status command is reachable and the queue is allowed; V1 still requires physical sample output, paper alignment, barcode scan, void / reprint, driver or spool callback evidence, and operator acceptance.

## 2026-07-04 - Use A CLI Runner For V1 Print Readiness Checks

Before field go-live, print readiness should be checkable from a local or on-site terminal against the running ERP API, not only by manually opening UI panels or calling individual endpoints. The runner is `scripts/run-print-v1-readiness-check.mjs`; it reads `GET /api/print-driver/cups-diagnostics` and `GET /api/print-driver/v1-readiness`, supports text or JSON output, and uses explicit exit codes: `0` means the gate is ready, `1` means the API or read failed, and `2` means the gate was reachable but still blocked. The runner may report readiness summaries, blockers, remaining risks, and next actions, but must not expose command paths, command args, stdout/stderr contents, spool paths, payload details, or claim physical output. It is an operations readiness check over the current API configuration and recorded QA evidence; real label-printer / dot-matrix output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, void / reprint, vendor SDK status, and operator acceptance remain field QA.

## 2026-07-04 - Preflight CUPS Queues Without Printing

CUPS queue readiness must be checked through a non-printing preflight before operators treat a printer queue as available. The first boundary is `scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs --action cups-preflight` plus `GET /api/print-driver/cups-diagnostics`, guarded by `fulfillment.print`; the office print diagnostics UI should surface the same result, and `GET /api/print-driver/v1-readiness` must include it as a blocking go-live criterion. The preflight may run a status command such as `lpstat -p {cupsPrinterName}`, but it must not read order payload from stdin, create a print file, submit a print job, expose command paths, expose command args, expose stdout/stderr content, expose spool paths, or claim physical output. It still requires `cups_lp` mode, a CUPS printer name, and a CUPS printer allowlist. A passing queue preflight proves only that the queue status command is reachable; paper output, alignment, barcode scan, void / reprint, vendor SDK status, and operator acceptance remain separate V1 field QA. V2 can add deeper vendor status, automatic recovery, and printer load balancing later.

## 2026-07-04 - Keep CUPS Submission Explicit And Still Gate Printed Status

The local print command bridge may support a real CUPS `lp` submission path, but it must remain opt-in and separately guarded from the default `spool_only` mode. `cups_lp` requires an explicit bridge mode, a configured CUPS command, a CUPS printer allowlist, and a printer name; it must invoke commands with `shell:false`, keep command paths and args out of API/UI responses, and generate a controlled print file rather than exposing raw payloads. A CUPS acceptance proves only that the OS print queue accepted the job, so the bridge record and ERP print job should move to `sent`, not `printed`. Printed status still requires command-bridge completed status, a driver callback, or field QA / operator evidence. This is V1 hardening toward real label-printer and dot-matrix output; V2 can add deeper vendor SDK status, automatic recovery, and multi-printer load balancing later.

## 2026-07-04 - Require Structured Evidence For Print Device Field QA

Printer device field QA must not be treated as complete from pass/fail checkboxes alone. A `printer_device_field_tests` record should carry structured evidence for sample / paper output, barcode scanning, driver or spool callback, void / reprint, and on-site operator acceptance. The first implementation stores this evidence in the existing field-test read/write contract and PostgreSQL `summary_json` boundary so no extra migration is required. The V1 print go-live gate should require both all six field-QA checks to pass and the evidence summary to be complete before marking a required printer device ready. This still does not prove the physical event by itself; it records the office/operator evidence that must be collected during real CUPS / label-printer / dot-matrix field acceptance.

## 2026-07-04 - Treat Print Device Driver Mode As A Real Backend Configuration

Office users may maintain a printer device's `settings.driverMode` from the `打包/标签` print-device QA panel, but this is a backend configuration change, not a local UI preference. Saving the mode must call the dedicated `POST /api/print-devices/{printDeviceId}/driver-mode` route, require `fulfillment.print`, write an operation log through the print-device repository boundary, and fail closed when the API is unavailable or the user lacks permission. The route is intentionally narrower than generic device upsert: it may update `settings.driverMode`, `updatedBy`, and `updatedAt`, but must not overwrite paper size, DPI, supported document types, or other device profile fields. The UI keeps driver-mode saving separate from field QA saving: setting a device to `system_printer` only means ERP may dispatch to the configured system-printer path; it does not prove sample printing, paper alignment, barcode scanning, driver callback, legibility, void / reprint, CUPS/vendor SDK integration, or physical printer status acceptance. The V1 go-live gate remains the authority that combines mode, configuration, spool diagnostics, and field QA evidence.

## 2026-07-04 - Gate V1 Print Go-Live With Configuration Plus Field QA Evidence

V1 print readiness should be decided by an explicit go-live gate, not by the existence of print pages or isolated adapter checks. The route is `GET /api/print-driver/v1-readiness`, guarded by `fulfillment.print`. It combines system-printer configuration, command-bridge spool diagnostics, non-printing CUPS queue preflight, active V1 print devices, device driver mode, and latest field QA records for the label printer and dot-matrix printer. The gate is ready only when command_bridge real dispatch is configured, spool readback diagnostics pass, CUPS queue preflight passes, required devices cover the V1 document types, devices are set to `system_printer`, and the latest field QA records pass all required checks: sample print, paper alignment, barcode scan, driver callback, legibility, and void / reprint. The gate must not call the real print command or expose command paths, args, spool paths, stdout/stderr contents, or print payloads. It is still evidence over ERP configuration and recorded QA; physical output, paper alignment, barcode scanning, vendor SDKs, status protocols, and operator acceptance remain the actual field QA work.

## 2026-07-04 - Diagnose Print Command-Bridge Spool Readback Without Printing

V1 needs a runtime check that proves the ERP print adapter can use the configured command-bridge spool directory before operators rely on driver-status polling. The route is `GET /api/print-driver/spool-diagnostics`, guarded by `fulfillment.print`. It may write diagnostic-only spool records, confirm `queued` maps to pending / sent, confirm `completed` maps to printed, and clean up the diagnostic files. It must not call the configured system print command, create a business print job, expose command paths, expose command args, expose spool paths, expose print payloads, or claim physical printer success. Physical printer acceptance remains a separate field QA process covering CUPS / label printers / dot-matrix printers, paper alignment, barcode scanning, void / reprint, vendor status protocols, and on-site operator validation.

## 2026-07-03 - Preflight Attachment Storage Without Exposing Secrets

V1 needs a runtime check that proves the configured attachment storage adapter can write, read, verify, and clean up a small diagnostic object before operators rely on evidence uploads. The route is `GET /api/attachments/storage-diagnostics`, guarded by existing `attachment.view` permission. It may report storage kind, configured status, missing configuration field names, diagnostic storage key, digest checks, cleanup status, and a redacted error summary, but it must not expose access-key values, secret keys, authorization headers, session tokens, raw signed headers, or bucket credentials. The diagnostic object is not a business attachment and should be removed after the readback check where the adapter supports cleanup. This is a V1 readiness preflight; real OSS/S3/COS bucket credentials, network policy, lifecycle rules, virus scanning, resumable uploads, and field acceptance remain separate hardening work.

## 2026-07-03 - Validate Attachment Uploads By Business Purpose

Attachment uploads should be validated by business purpose instead of relying only on generic `contentDataUrl` parsing. Payment screenshots must be image files up to 8 MB. Delivery watermark photos, signature photos, and custom printed finished-goods photos must be image files up to 12 MB. Statement customer-confirmation attachments may be image or PDF files up to 12 MB. Other generic attachments are limited to common evidence file types, such as images, PDFs, spreadsheets, or documents, up to 15 MB. The frontend may pre-block invalid uploads for operator feedback, but the backend route remains the authority and re-infers file type from MIME / filename before saving. V1 uses this as a basic evidence-quality and safety gate; virus scanning, automatic image-quality checks, resumable uploads, and real object-storage live acceptance remain separate hardening work.

## 2026-07-03 - Use A Dedicated Permission For Workshop Finished-Goods Photo Attachments

Workshop users need to upload custom printed finished-goods photos from the mobile production task, but they should not receive general attachment upload authority. The first V1 permission is `attachment.finished_goods_photo.create`, accepted only when an attachment request is for `ownerType=production_task` and `purpose=finished_goods_photo`. Workshop roles can combine this with `production.report.complete` to upload or retake finished-goods photos; office or management still reviews the photo before any customer notification todo is created. General `attachment.create` remains reserved for office / finance / management flows such as payment screenshots, statement confirmation files, and other back-office evidence.

## 2026-07-03 - Keep V1 Customer Notification Todos Manual And Auditable

Customer-notification todos created after accepted finished-goods photos should support office copy / manual send / confirmation, not automatic customer messaging in V1. The `customer_notification_copied` action records prepared copy and keeps the todo open; the `customer_notification_sent` action is the manual office confirmation that closes the todo. The todo detail may show notification text, channel, photo prompt, copied metadata, and notified metadata, but it must not call WeCom, customer-group, SMS, or other external send channels until a separate integration adds explicit permissions, audit, callback handling, and manual takeover.

## 2026-07-03 - Keep Finished-Goods Photos As A Manual Quality Gate Before Customer Notification

Custom printed finished-goods photos are a quality gate, not an inventory or fulfillment trigger. The first V1 boundary lets production upload a `finished_goods_photo` attachment against a production task, then requires office / management review before a customer-notification todo is created. Accepting the photo creates or deduplicates a `待通知客户` todo; rejecting it creates or deduplicates a `成品图需重拍` todo. Upload, acceptance, and rejection must not create finished-goods inventory, inventory reservations, packing tasks, delivery records, billing quantities, or automatic customer messages. Customer contact remains manual in V1; later automatic sending can only be added as a separate integration with explicit permissions, audit, and manual takeover.

## 2026-07-03 - Keep V1 Queue Moves Manual But Reasoned And Auditable

V1 machine-queue moves and insertions should remain human-controlled office actions, but every move should capture why it was done and what it affects. The office UI should require or default an adjustment reason, show a compact impact preview before `移动/插队`, include the reason in the success toast, and persist a remark such as source machine, target machine, target queue position, source resequence count, and target push-down count. This improves V1 auditability and operator confidence without pretending to solve automatic scheduling. V2 can add insertion recommendations, capacity optimization, mold-change optimization, and deeper workshop-display synchronization on top of the same audited queue records.

## 2026-07-03 - Treat Machine Reassignment As An Explicit Schedule Queue Move

Moving a production task to another machine should be an explicit schedule queue action, not an implicit edit inside production reporting or inventory flow. The first route is `POST /api/production-schedules/machine-queue/move`, guarded by `production.schedule.sequence.update`. It updates the production task's `machine_id`, marks the old machine schedule record as `moved`, writes the new machine schedule record as `active`, records `sourceKind=machine_reassignment`, and writes an operation log. The action must not create finished-goods inventory, reservations, packing tasks, workshop reports, fulfillment quantities, or billing quantities. A moved source record may have `queueSeq=0` because it is historical audit context, not an executable queue position. This gives V1 an auditable machine-reassignment boundary while full insertion strategy, mold-change work orders, shift / substitute scheduling, capacity optimization, workshop display sync, and field board acceptance remain later scheduling-engine work.

## 2026-07-03 - Persist Manual Queue Sequences In Formal Schedule Records

Manual same-machine queue resequencing should be persisted in `production_schedule_records` rather than remaining only in local skeleton state. The first boundary stores production task, order line, published schedule id, machine id, queue sequence, source kind, operator, timestamp, and remark, with a unique key on `machine_id + production_task_id`. `POST /api/production-schedules/machine-queue/resequence` writes through a local or PostgreSQL repository and returns the persisted schedule-record summaries. The transaction must still not create inventory, reservations, packing tasks, workshop reports, or billing quantities. This gives V1 an auditable record boundary for sequence changes while leaving insertion planning, machine reassignment, mold-change work orders, shift / substitute scheduling, capacity optimization, workshop display sync, and field board acceptance as later scheduling-engine work.

## 2026-07-03 - Require Full Same-Machine Order For Manual Queue Resequencing

The first manual scheduling adjustment should resequence only the current machine queue and require the client to submit the complete ordered production-task id list for that machine. `POST /api/production-schedules/machine-queue/resequence`, guarded by `production.schedule.sequence.update`, rejects missing, duplicate, unknown, or cross-queue task ids. The route records manual queue sequence, operator, timestamp, remark, and an operation log, then returns the updated queue. It must not create inventory, reservations, packing tasks, workshop reports, or a formal shift record. This avoids ambiguous partial moves while keeping the V1 skeleton auditable; PostgreSQL schedule tables, insertion, machine reassignment, mold-change planning, shift / substitute rules, workshop display sync, and capacity optimization remain later scheduling-engine work.

## 2026-07-03 - Derive First Machine Queue From Published Production Tasks

The first scheduling board should derive a read-only machine queue from existing production tasks instead of introducing a formal schedule table prematurely. `GET /api/production-schedules/machine-queue` includes non-completed tasks that have a `publishedScheduleId` or carry-over daily progress, assigns per-machine `queueSeq`, and exposes customer / product / planned / remaining summaries for office visibility. It must not create inventory, reservations, packing tasks, workshop reports, or official shift records. Manual resequencing, insertions, machine reassignment, mold-change planning, substitute / shift rules, workshop display sync, and capacity optimization remain later scheduling-engine work.

## 2026-07-03 - Publish Production Schedules Before Workshop Visibility

Workshop production tasks should become visible through an explicit office / management publish action, not by implicit report or packing actions. The publish route is `POST /api/production-tasks/{productionTaskId}/publish-schedule`, guarded by `production.schedule.publish`. Publishing writes `publishedScheduleId`, machine id, `制袋已排产 / 丝印已排产` status, order-line status, and an operation log, then the workshop mobile task pool may show the task only when the current machine matches. Publishing must not create finished-goods inventory, inventory reservations, packing tasks, inventory ledgers, or workshop reports. Full shift scheduling, substitutions, insertions, mold-change planning, and capacity optimization remain later scheduling-engine work.

## 2026-07-03 - Separate Cross-Day Daily Progress From Final Production Completion
