Cross-day workshop daily progress is a separate production action from final production completion. `POST /api/production-tasks/{productionTaskId}/daily-progress` records the day's qualified quantity, exception quantity, machine action / cycle count evidence, cumulative quantity, remaining quantity, carry-over status, and next work date in `workshop_reports.evidence_json` with `reportKind=daily_progress`. This action may update a production task to `跨日继续` or `待完工确认`, but it must not create finished-goods inventory, inventory reservations, packing tasks, inventory ledgers, billing quantities, fulfillment quantities, or machine-capacity calibration samples. Only final `report-complete` may create finished-goods inventory and downstream reservation / packing effects.

## 2026-07-03 - Calibrate Machine Capacity From Qualified Production Reports

Machine capacity calibration samples should be derived from confirmed qualified production quantity, not from machine action / cycle counters. The first V1 loop records a `machine_capacity_baselines` sample from `POST /api/production-tasks/{productionTaskId}/report-complete` using machine + size + date, `source_kind=production_report`, and `confidence=medium`. Multiple reports on the same machine, size, and date accumulate `daily_capacity_qty`. `machineCount` remains evidence for operator / machine context only and must not affect inventory, fulfillment, statements, billing, or capacity quantity. If a production report references a machine that has not yet been imported into machine master data, PostgreSQL skips the calibration sample without blocking the report; machine master-data cleanup and real production-summary reconciliation remain separate V1 acceptance work.

## 2026-07-03 - Issue Imported Employee Passwords Only After Review

Imported employee accounts may receive a login password only after management has enabled the employee profile. The password issuing route is `POST /api/master-data/employee-account-reviews/{employeeId}/password`, guarded by `master_data.employee_account.password.issue`. It rejects employees that are still `pending_admin_review`, returns the temporary password only in the issuance response, stores only a runtime password hash on the internal user projection, and writes a `master_data_employee_account_password` operation log. Dynamic login through `/api/auth/login` can use the issued employee login name and temporary password, and session / effective-permission reads resolve the employee's reviewed role. Production hardening remains separate: forced password change, reset / revoke, password policy, admin UI, production deployment, and field acceptance are still required before treating this as a complete production identity system.

## 2026-07-03 - Enable Imported Employee Accounts Only After Management Review

Employee rows imported through master data remain disabled until a management user reviews them. The first ERP-side review flow is `GET /api/master-data/employee-account-reviews` plus `POST /api/master-data/employee-account-reviews/{employeeId}/enable`, guarded by `master_data.employee_account.review`. Enabling a reviewed employee marks the employee profile as `account_enabled`, creates or updates an internal user projection, and writes a `master_data_employee_account_review` operation log. This is not automatic login provisioning by itself: password issuance must still happen through the separate management-only password route, and a production user store remains separate hardening work.

## 2026-07-03 - Treat Master Data Failed-Row Downloads As Non-Writing Review Work

Master-data failed-row CSV downloads are review / correction work, not official import execution. The failed-row endpoint `GET /api/master-data/import-executions/{executionId}/failed-rows` should require `master_data.import.plan.create`, so office and management users can download failed rows and fix Excel data without receiving write authority. Official import execution remains management-only through `master_data.import.execute`, an explicit `officialImportEnabled=true` request, and a configured transaction writer. The UI should keep these actions visually separate: `下载失败行` is a correction aid, while `正式导入` is a high-risk write action.

## 2026-07-03 - Require Explicit Transaction Writers For Master Data Official Imports

Master-data execution records are non-writing by default. Official customer, price, product-spec, inventory, employee, and machine imports require a management operator, `master_data.import.execute`, an explicit request flag `officialImportEnabled=true`, and a configured writer kind such as `local_transaction` or `postgres`. The writer must apply rows in dependency order, write an operation log, and roll back the whole batch when any referenced master row is missing or invalid. Imported employees remain disabled and `pending_admin_review` even after a committed import. This advances V1 from planning-only import records to a local / PostgreSQL transaction boundary with Docker live smoke coverage; after the UI and failed-row download pass, the remaining gates are broader Excel compatibility, admin account enablement, independent maintenance pages, and real-data field acceptance.

## 2026-07-03 - Use The Updated Bag Price Image For New Pricing Only

The current P0 bag price version is `P0-PRICE-20260703`, sourced from `docs/product/assets/price-size-table-2026-07-03.png`. New order recognition, formal-order confirmation price snapshots, local order-pool read-model price splits, and master-data price-template examples should use this version. Existing fixture order amounts and historical order price snapshots must not be retroactively rewritten by the new table. The 2026-07-03 image does not include a separate extended-handle table, so P0 keeps the current default for plain blank bags: extended handle = base price + 0.03 CNY until a dedicated extended-handle price table is provided. The image changes notable style data too: bear/dog uses `40*35*12`, laminated first size is `30*27*10`, gift bags have a 1000+ piece discount of 0.05 CNY, and silk-screen pricing uses manual single-sided 0.09, manual double-sided 0.13, and machine tiers 0.09 / 0.08 / 0.06 CNY at 3000 / 5000 / 10000 pieces.

## 2026-07-03 - Import Employee And Machine Master Rows As Reviewed Profiles

The `员工机台` master-data sheet should map into employee profiles, machines, default employee-machine assignments, and machine capacity baselines once those tables exist. Imported employee rows must not automatically create or enable login accounts; they enter `pending_admin_review` with `accountEnabled: false`, while the requested enabled state is retained for admin review. Machine capacity imported from the template is a low-confidence `manual_estimate` baseline until calibrated from production summary data. This removes `员工机台` from the default failed-row path; official writes now go through the explicit transaction writer, while account enablement still requires a later admin review flow.

## 2026-07-03 - Preserve Master Data Staged Rows And Failed-Row Reports Before Official Writes

Master-data import precheck results should preserve parsed row data as `stagedRows` and carry it through review drafts, confirmation plans, and execution records before any official writer is requested. The first execution payload version is `p0-master-data-import-execution-payload-v1`, which maps supported customer, product-spec, price-table, initial-inventory, employee, and machine rows into target-record payloads for the transaction writer. Unsupported future rows must be returned as failed rows with a downloadable CSV report. Execution records should distinguish missing staged rows, generated failed rows, missing official writer configuration, and committed writer results instead of reporting every case as a generic writer block.

## 2026-07-03 - Gate Master Data Import Execution Behind Management And Non-Writing Records

Master-data import execution requests should be recorded before any official writer is invoked, and they must be gated separately from confirmation-plan creation. Office and management roles may generate `MDP-*` confirmation plans, but only management has `master_data.import.execute` for creating `MDE-*` execution records. The execution API must re-read the saved plan by `planId` from the server-side repository instead of trusting a frontend-submitted plan object. Default execution requests must keep `officialWriteAttempted: false` and `officialWriteScope: none`, usually with status `blocked_official_writer_not_configured`; explicit management requests with `officialImportEnabled=true` and a configured writer kind may move to a committed transaction result. Failed-row download and formal UI remain separate gates.

## 2026-07-03 - Separate V1 Core Readiness From V2 Automation Scope

V1 should focus on a single-factory, human-controlled ERP core: order entry, inventory, outbound, printing, packing, driver delivery, statements, payments, master-data import safety, permissions, audit logs, real devices, real storage, and field QA. V2 should carry automation and optimization work such as customer-group archive reading, AI/OCR order recognition expansion, auto-reply assistance, route and scheduling optimization, deeper material/cost/margin, after-sales responsibility analytics, payroll depth, and broader BI. V2 must not weaken V1's manual confirmation, permission, audit, and rollback boundaries.

## 2026-07-03 - Persist Master Data Confirmation Plans Before Enabling Official Import

Master-data confirmation plans should be persisted and auditable before any official master-data write path is invoked. The first API boundary is `POST /api/master-data/import-confirmation-plans` plus `GET /api/master-data/import-confirmation-plans`, guarded by `master_data.import.plan.create` for office and management roles. The API saves review drafts, confirmation plans, and related operation logs through a local JSON repository first, and the frontend may fall back to a local-only plan only when the API is unavailable. This does not enable formal import by itself: confirmation-plan responses must still keep `officialImportEnabled: false` and `officialWriteScope: none`; only a later management execution request with explicit writer configuration may call the transaction writer.

## 2026-07-03 - Keep Master Data Confirmation Plans Non-Writing Until Transaction Boundary Exists

Master-data review drafts may generate a versioned confirmation plan before any official import. The first plan version is `p0-master-data-import-confirmation-plan-v1`, implemented in `src/domain/masterDataImportConfirmationPlan.js`. A plan records target write batches, target tables, operation-log draft, transaction policy, rollback / failed-row requirements, and safeguards for customers, price tables, product specs, initial inventory, employees, and machines. It explicitly keeps `officialImportEnabled: false` and `officialWriteScope: none`; it is a contract for the later API / PostgreSQL import path, not an import action. Real official import still requires permission checks, persisted operation logs, transaction-safe writes, rollback verification, failed-row download, and broader Excel compatibility.

## 2026-07-03 - Keep Master Data Review Drafts Separate From Official Writes

Master-data uploads that pass precheck may enter a versioned review draft queue before any official import. The first review version is `p0-master-data-import-review-v1`, implemented in `src/domain/masterDataImportReviewQueue.js`. A blocked precheck cannot enter the queue; a warning-only precheck becomes `待人工确认`; a clean precheck becomes `待确认导入`. Review drafts keep file name, precheck/template versions, sheet summaries, issue counts, operator, and a stable `MDI-*` draft id, but explicitly set `officialImportEnabled: false` and `officialWriteScope: none`. Later official import still requires permission checks, operation logs, transaction-safe PostgreSQL writes, rollback behavior, and failed-row download.

## 2026-07-03 - Precheck Master Data Imports Before Any Write Transaction

Master-data Excel uploads must produce a reviewable precheck result before any customer, price, product-spec, inventory, employee, or machine records are written. The first precheck version is `p0-master-data-import-precheck-v1`, implemented in `src/domain/masterDataImportPrecheck.js`. It checks workbook structure, required fields, duplicates, price risk, inventory quantity consistency, allowed statuses, employee / machine numeric fields, and cross-sheet product-spec matching. A failed precheck blocks import, a warning-only precheck requires human confirmation, and even a passing precheck only means the upload may enter a later confirmation/import queue. No upload should silently overwrite official master data, price snapshots, historical orders, inventory ledgers, or production records.

## 2026-07-03 - Generate Master Data Templates Before Enabling Imports

Customer, price, product-spec, initial-inventory, and employee / machine master data should first be collected through versioned `.xlsx` templates before any Excel upload writes to official data. The first template version is `p0-master-data-import-template-v1`, generated by `src/domain/masterDataImportTemplate.js` on top of the shared `src/domain/xlsxWorkbook.js` builder. It includes a readme sheet plus business sheets for `客户档案`, `价格表`, `尺寸颜色款式`, `初始库存`, and `员工机台`. Later imports must run whole-workbook prechecks first and create a reviewable result for duplicates, missing fields, high-risk prices, invalid specs, and inventory anomalies. Price imports should default to review-required, inventory imports must create ledger / initial-count evidence, and historical orders, price snapshots, and inventory ledger records must not be silently overwritten by master-data changes.

## 2026-07-03 - Generate Print Driver Integration Kits From Sanitized Runtime Config

Office print-driver handoff payloads should be generated from sanitized runtime configuration instead of hand-written examples. The first kit is `p0-print-driver-integration-kit-v1`, created by `buildOfficePrintDriverIntegrationKit(...)`, and exposed in the office print-driver diagnostics panel as `打印联调包`. It contains the command-bridge dispatch contract, placeholder argument template, stdin payload shape, expected dispatch result, spool status readback API, service permission, lifecycle actions, and bridge-to-ERP status mappings such as pending-to-`sent` and completed-to-`printed`. The kit must not expose command paths, raw arguments, spool paths, stdout/stderr, configured printer names, secrets, or full print payload contents. It is a developer / deployment integration contract only; physical acceptance still requires real CUPS / label-printer / dot-matrix driver setup, paper alignment, barcode scans, driver readback, void / reprint tests, and printer-device field QA records.

## 2026-07-03 - Generate Driver Native Integration Kits From Runtime Bridge Contracts

Native-shell handoff payloads for driver scanning and navigation should be generated from the same runtime request builders that the web page uses to call the bridge. The first kit is `p0-driver-native-integration-kit-v1`, containing the scanner contract `driver.packageLabel.scan`, the navigation contract `driver.navigation.open`, direct JS bridge method names, Android JSON method names, WebKit handler name, browser event callback names, global callback names, expected result shapes, and current task payload examples. This avoids maintaining stale hand-written sample payloads in documents or screenshots. The kit is a developer integration contract only; real acceptance still requires Android/iOS shell implementation, permission handling, scanner / map SDK behavior, physical-label scans, installed map-app tests, and driver-phone field QA evidence.

## 2026-07-03 - Store Driver Native Bridge Snapshots With Field QA Records

Driver field QA records should persist the native bridge diagnostic snapshot that was visible at save time. Store the snapshot as `nativeBridgeDiagnostics` inside `driver_device_field_tests.summary_json`, alongside the existing `packageLabelScanSample`, so P0 can retain browser/native-shell readiness evidence without adding a migration-only column. The snapshot may record native scanner and native navigation support status, bridge type, protocol version, and a compact label such as `原生 0/2`. It is evidence of bridge availability at the time of field QA, not evidence that scanner hardware, camera permissions, physical labels, map SDKs, installed map apps, or route opening passed on a real driver phone.

## 2026-07-02 - Surface Driver Native Bridge Diagnostics Separately From Field Proof

The driver mobile UI should expose native shell readiness as a visible diagnostic, but it must stay separate from real field acceptance. The diagnostic may combine the native scanner bridge (`p0-driver-native-bridge-v1`) and native navigation bridge (`p0-driver-native-navigation-bridge-v1`) into a compact `原生桥接` panel showing support status, detected bridge type, protocol version, and a summary such as `原生 0/2`. This helps office / field QA see whether the page is running inside an Android/iOS shell with bridge methods available. It is not evidence that scanner hardware, camera permissions, physical package labels, map SDKs, installed map apps, or route opening work on a real driver phone; those still require explicit native-shell integration and field QA records.

## 2026-07-02 - Use A Versioned Driver Native Navigation Bridge Contract

Driver native map navigation should use a small versioned bridge contract that stays separate from office dispatch planning and route optimization. The P0 bridge version is `p0-driver-native-navigation-bridge-v1`, with message type `driver.navigation.open`, a generated `requestId`, fulfillment/order/operator context, route context, delivery address snapshot, optional GPS point, and the external map URL fallback. Native shells may expose `window.erpDriverNative.openNavigation(...)` / `DriverNativeBridge`, Android-style `ErpDriverNative.openNavigation(JSON.stringify(request))`, or `window.webkit.messageHandlers.erpDriver.postMessage(...)`; navigation results return through the direct function result, the `erp-driver-native-navigation` browser event, or `window.__erpDriverNativeBridge.receiveNavigationResult(...)`. The Web page may mark the driver field-test `外部导航` item as passed only after the native shell returns an opened result. This is an integration boundary only; real driver-phone acceptance still requires the native shell, installed map SDK / app, permission handling, field QA evidence, and later route optimization.

## 2026-07-02 - Use A Versioned Driver Native Scanner Bridge Contract

Driver native package-label scanning should use a small versioned bridge contract instead of embedding Android/iOS SDK details in the React page. The P0 bridge version is `p0-driver-native-bridge-v1`, with message type `driver.packageLabel.scan`, a generated `requestId`, fulfillment/order/operator context, and expected package ids. Native shells may expose `window.erpDriverNative.scanPackageLabel(...)` / `DriverNativeBridge`, Android-style `ErpDriverNative.scanPackageLabel(JSON.stringify(request))`, or `window.webkit.messageHandlers.erpDriver.postMessage(...)`; scan results return through the direct function result, the `erp-driver-native-package-scan` browser event, or `window.__erpDriverNativeBridge.receivePackageScanResult(...)`. The Web page must normalize all successful native results into the same package-check and `packageLabelScanSample` path using method `native_sdk`. This is an integration boundary only; real driver-phone acceptance still requires the native shell, camera / scanner permissions, physical paper labels, and field QA evidence.

## 2026-07-02 - Store Driver Package-Label QA Samples With Device Field Tests

Driver physical package-label QA should be recorded as a structured sample on the driver device field-test record, not as free text only. The P0 sample fields are expected package id, scanned text, matched package id, scan method (`scanner_wedge`, `camera`, `manual`, `native_sdk`), result, message, and checked time. The UI may generate this sample from scanner-wedge / manual entry, camera scan results, and camera-start failures, then show it as `标签样本` in the driver `现场验收` section. For P0 persistence, store the sample inside `driver_device_field_tests.summary_json.packageLabelScanSample` and lift it back as top-level `deviceFieldTestRecord.packageLabelScanSample` on readback, avoiding a migration-only metadata column until field QA expands beyond one latest sample per record. This evidence still does not replace real physical-label scans on driver phones, native scanner SDK validation, or physical device acceptance.

## 2026-07-02 - Treat Non-Image Statement Evidence As Downloadable Attachments First

Statement customer-confirmation and payment evidence may include images, PDFs, spreadsheets, documents, CSV, TXT, and other common files. The attachment client should infer file type from MIME type and filename instead of forcing all statement evidence to `image`. The UI should preview images inline, but non-image evidence should show a file card with metadata and a download action. P0 should not pretend to parse, OCR, render inline PDFs, or provide online Office preview until those capabilities are explicitly implemented and verified.

## 2026-07-02 - Use Existing Attachment Boundary For Statement Customer Confirmation Evidence

Statement customer confirmation screenshots, chat-record images, PDFs, spreadsheets, documents, and other evidence files should reuse the shared attachment boundary instead of creating a separate customer-confirmation file table. The attachment owner is `statement`, the purpose is `statement_customer_confirmation`, and the business confirmation record stores the resulting attachment IDs through `statement_confirmation_records.attachment_ids_json`. The confirmation record owns the business meaning, timing, channel, confirmer, and operator; the attachment API owns file metadata, storage, preview/download behavior, access permission, and access audit. The first P0 UI supports manual office upload during `登记客户确认`; real WeChat / WeCom message callbacks, automatic chat capture, inline PDF rendering, online Office preview, document parsing, and final retention policy remain later integrations.

## 2026-07-02 - Store Statement Customer Confirmation As Evidence Records

Statement customer confirmation should be stored as its own evidence record, linked to the statement and the related send record. The first P0 record table is `statement_confirmation_records`, including confirmation type, channel, customer-side confirmer, confirmation time, content, optional attachment IDs, registering operator, and operation log. Registering customer confirmation upgrades the related send record to `confirmed` and moves the statement to `客户已确认`, but it does not mean payment has been received or written off. This is manual office registration for a customer reply such as `客户回复确认无误`, not a real WeChat / WeCom send callback, read callback, or automated customer message integration.

## 2026-07-02 - Store Statement Send Receipts On Send Records

Statement customer receipt state belongs to the statement send record, not to a separate statement status. `statement_send_records` stores `receipt_status`, `receipt_at`, `receipt_by`, and `receipt_note`; allowed P0 statuses are `pending`, `delivered`, `read`, `confirmed`, and `no_response`. The first office UI action exposes only `标记已读回执`, guarded by the existing `statement.send` permission, and writes an operation log. Local fallback still creates a stable `SEND-LOCAL-*` send record id so office users can mark read receipts after a local send action during prototype/API downtime. This is manual receipt registration, not automatic WeChat/enterprise-message sending or external read-callback integration.

## 2026-07-02 - Statement Export History Downloads Reuse Stored Tokens

Statement export history rows should re-download the stored export file through `GET /api/statements/{statementId}/exports/{downloadToken}` using the row's `downloadToken`. A history download is a retrieval of an already generated customer-send or internal-archive export, not a regeneration path. If the API is unavailable during a history download, the frontend should show that the archived file cannot currently be downloaded instead of silently rebuilding a local workbook, because local regeneration could differ from the archived file that was sent or reviewed. The same `statement.preview` permission guards preview generation, export-list access, and export-file download.

## 2026-07-02 - Use A Shared Versioned XLSX Template For Statement Excel

Statement Excel generation should use one shared, versioned template builder for both backend download files and frontend local fallback. The default P0 template is `p0-statement-xlsx-v1`, emitted as a real Open XML `.xlsx` binary workbook with two sheets: `对账汇总` and `交付明细`. The summary sheet includes customer / period / preview type, amounts, customer confirmation, internal review, and template metadata; the detail sheet uses stable columns for delivered quantity, chargeable quantity, free / non-chargeable quantity, unit price, adjustments, final amount, and remark. Export metadata should include template ID, template version, workbook format, workbook extension, content type, content encoding, and worksheet names, while the export-list API must still omit file content. SpreadsheetML remains only as a legacy compatibility helper. This is the P0 system-default XLSX boundary, not a final customer-approved visual design, real object-storage live proof, or template-maintenance UI.

## 2026-07-02 - Keep Print-Driver Environment Preflight Non-Printing And Sanitized

The print-driver configuration API may expose a local environment preflight for deployment readiness, but it must remain non-printing. The preflight can check system-printer enablement, adapter kind, whether a command is configured and executable, argument-template presence, printer allowlist presence, local spool directory existence / writability, command redaction, and whether real dispatch is reachable. It must not execute a print job, submit to CUPS, call a vendor SDK, or expose command paths, raw arguments, stdout, stderr, payload contents, or spool paths. Preflight success is configuration evidence only; physical print proof still requires trusted printer status readback and field QA.

## 2026-07-02 - Use Sanitized Readiness Checklist For Print-Driver Configuration

The office print-driver diagnostics panel should expose real-printer readiness as a checklist derived only from sanitized backend configuration. The checklist may show whether backend config is readable, system printing is enabled, `command_bridge` is selected, command / argument templates are configured, printer allowlists exist, spool status readback is available, command values are redacted, and real dispatch is reachable. It must not expose command paths, raw arguments, stdout, stderr, spool paths, or payload contents. A readiness checklist is configuration guidance only; it must not be treated as physical print proof without trusted printer status readback and field QA.

## 2026-07-02 - Surface Print-Driver Diagnostics In Office Print Workflows

The office `打包/标签` surface should show the current print-driver configuration boundary next to printer QA and the print-job queue. Operators may see whether the backend is in guarded mode, dry-run mode, command-bridge mode, whether system printing is enabled, whether printer allowlists are configured, and whether local command-bridge status readback is available. The UI must not expose command paths, command arguments, stdout, stderr, spool paths, or payload contents. A visible driver diagnostic is operational context only: command submission or queue status must not be treated as physical print proof without status readback and field QA.

## 2026-07-02 - Keep Command-Bridge Lifecycle Commands Local And Sanitized

The `print-command-bridge` CLI may expose local lifecycle actions for QA and integration: `status`, `complete`, `fail`, and `cancel`. These actions only update or summarize local spool files inside the configured storage root, and terminal bridge jobs cannot be moved to a different terminal status. Lifecycle responses may include identifiers, status, timestamps, error codes, sanitized messages, and payload digests, but must not expose print payload contents, command paths, arguments, stdout, stderr, spool paths, or real printer configuration. These lifecycle commands create local bridge evidence for the driver-poll readback path; they are not physical printer proof or a replacement for CUPS / vendor SDK status protocols and field QA.

## 2026-07-02 - Treat Command-Bridge Spool Readback As Local Evidence Only

The `command_bridge` adapter may read local spool files created by the first `spool_only` bridge to update ERP print-job state. A queued, pending, processing, or submitted spool record means the ERP job remains `sent`; it must not be upgraded to `printed`. Only explicit local spool evidence such as `completed`, `failed`, or `canceled` may be mapped to `printed`, `failed`, or `canceled` through the driver-poll status machine. This readback is useful for local command-wrapper integration and QA, but it is not a CUPS status protocol, vendor SDK callback, physical printer proof, or final field QA replacement. API responses may expose sanitized status metadata such as bridge status, mode, timestamps, and digest, but must not expose command paths, arguments, stdout, stderr, spool paths, or print payload contents.

## 2026-07-02 - Seed Guarded Office Print Jobs For Local Action QA

The local P0 API may seed explicit `system_printer` print jobs for office action QA, as long as they stay guarded and do not imply physical printing. The seeded queued job verifies that office `派发` reaches the real printer boundary and fails clearly when no real driver is configured. The seeded failed job verifies that office `重试` creates a new queued retry job through the API and repository. These demo jobs are stable, restart-persistent, and non-duplicating, but they are not production print evidence and do not replace CUPS / vendor SDK integration, printer-status readback, or physical QA.

## 2026-07-02 - Keep Office Print-Job Controls Separate From Driver Status Callbacks

Office users may view the print-job queue and call business actions such as dispatching a queued job or retrying a failed / canceled job through `fulfillment.print`. They must not receive the service-account-only `print.job.callback` permission and must not directly fake driver callbacks, status polling, or physical print completion from the office UI. Print-job status completion still comes from the print-driver adapter, trusted driver callback, status-polling API under the print-driver service account, or explicit future operator confirmation with audit evidence. The office `打包/标签` surface can show `queued`, `sent`, `printed`, `failed`, `canceled`, and `preview_only`, but physical CUPS / vendor SDK success remains outside the office button boundary.

## 2026-07-02 - Record Printer Device Field QA Separately From Print Success

Printer device field QA should be stored as manual evidence in a dedicated `printer_device_field_tests` record table, not inferred from print records, print jobs, operation logs, or command-bridge submission. The first API boundary is `POST /api/print-devices/{printDeviceId}/field-tests`, guarded by `print.device_qa.record` for office / management users. Records may reference a print job and capture sample print, paper alignment, barcode scan, driver callback, legibility, void/reprint, device label, driver label, paper label, summary, note, and operation log. This evidence can show that a human tested a label printer or dot-matrix printer in the field, but it must not automatically mark a print job as physically printed or replace future CUPS / vendor SDK status readback. The print-driver service account remains limited to `print.job.callback`.

## 2026-07-02 - Use Backend Lists As The Visible Production And Packing Task Pools

The office `打包/标签` page and workshop / packing mobile task pools should use `GET /api/production-tasks` and `GET /api/packing-tasks` as the authoritative visible task lists whenever the API is available. Frontend-local projections may remain only as fallback for unavailable APIs or prototype startup, not as an additional source that silently adds rows when the backend list returns empty. Production and packing actions should refresh the backend task lists after successful API writes so operators see the same task-pool state that later PostgreSQL-backed reads will return.

## 2026-07-02 - Read Production And Packing Task Pools Through The Same Read Repository

Production and packing task-pool lists should use the same dedicated read repository as task details. `GET /api/production-tasks` and `GET /api/packing-tasks` return paginated list items with task summaries, order-line context, latest production report or package / fulfillment context, and lightweight inventory references. The repository supports local memory and PostgreSQL modes, while production reporting and packing completion writes remain owned by the production-packing transaction repository. The list read model must preserve the business quantity rules: machine count / cycle count is evidence only, and packing completion does not deduct inventory.

## 2026-07-02 - Read Production And Packing Details Through A Dedicated Repository

Production / packing source details should be queried through a dedicated read repository instead of being rebuilt inside API route helpers from the current process workspace. The first repository supports local memory and PostgreSQL modes and assembles production task detail, latest workshop report, machine count evidence, reservations, inventory ledger entries, packing task detail, packages, fulfillment state, and operation logs. `GET /api/production-tasks/{productionTaskId}` and `GET /api/packing-tasks/{packingTaskId}` are the API boundaries for inventory-ledger source detail panes. Production and packing writes remain owned by the transaction repository; this read repository must not mutate inventory quantities, package rows, fulfillment state, or machine-count rules.

## 2026-07-02 - Read Inventory Ledgers Through A Dedicated Repository

Inventory ledger history should be queried through a dedicated read repository instead of being reconstructed inside page code or order-pool detail projections. The first repository supports local memory and PostgreSQL modes, normalizes PostgreSQL `inventory_ledger_entries.id` to API-facing `ledgerId`, joins inventory item / color / operator context for display, and exposes filters for inventory item, source type/id, change type, keyword, date range, and pagination. `GET /api/inventory/ledger-entries` is the API boundary for inventory history panels and later traceability views. Inventory writes still belong to the existing business transactions; this read repository must not mutate quantities or replace inventory ledger creation rules.

## 2026-07-02 - Let Drivers Resubmit Rejected Delivery Evidence Without Re-Completing Delivery

When office review marks delivery evidence as `需重拍`, the delivery itself remains completed but the evidence is not accepted. The driver may use the delivery-complete API again only in that state to resubmit watermarked-photo evidence. This action is recorded as `driver_resubmit_delivery_evidence`, sets review status back to `待复核`, clears the previous issue reason, marks the open `照片待重拍` todo handled by the driver, and skips inventory deduction. Already-completed deliveries without a retake-required review remain blocked from repeat completion so inventory, statements, and fulfillment state are not duplicated.

## 2026-07-02 - Store Fulfillment Exception Codes Separately From Display Reasons

Fulfillment and driver delivery exceptions need both a stable reason code for reporting/rules and a display-ready reason text for operators. `fulfillment_exceptions.reason_code` stores normalized values such as `customer_unavailable`, while `fulfillment_exceptions.reason` stores the office/driver-facing explanation such as `客户不在`; `occurred_at` records the business event time separately from `created_at`. Driver task read models should expose `exceptionReasonCode`, `exceptionReason`, and `exceptionOccurredAt` so a PostgreSQL cold-start read can show the same exception context without falling back to transient API memory.

## 2026-07-02 - Persist Driver Load And Completion Fields On Fulfillment

Driver load confirmation and delivery completion fields belong on the fulfillment record because they describe the execution state of that delivery fulfillment. The first persisted fields are `loaded_at`, `loaded_by`, `driver_remark`, `receiver_name`, and `paper_note_status`. Driver action APIs should write these through the fulfillment action transaction and then return the task through the driver delivery task read repository, so PostgreSQL-backed responses and cold-start reads use the same projection. `driver_remark` is the latest driver-facing remark on the fulfillment; per-action remarks, including the original package-check load remark, remain durable in `operation_logs`. If the product later needs both load remark and completion remark visible at the same time, add separate typed fields rather than overloading `driver_remark`.

## 2026-07-02 - Store Driver Field Device QA In A Dedicated Record Table

Driver field-device QA evidence should have a dedicated persistence table instead of being reconstructed from generic operation logs. The first table is `driver_device_field_tests`, keyed by the `DQA-*` record id and linked to fulfillment, order line, driver, operator, operation log, checked time, device/browser labels, normalized checks, summary, and notes. The driver task read model should join the latest record per fulfillment and expose it as `deviceFieldTestRecord` / `deviceFieldTestSummary`, so PostgreSQL-backed driver task reads still show the latest field QA after an API server restart. Operation logs remain the audit trail, but they are not the primary read model for the latest QA state.

## 2026-07-02 - Persist Driver Field Device QA Through Driver Action Logs

Driver field-device QA records should be saved through a driver-scoped backend action instead of staying only in frontend state. The first API is `POST /api/driver/delivery-tasks/{fulfillmentId}/device-field-tests`, guarded by `delivery.device_qa.record`. The server recomputes normalized checks and summary from the submitted manual evidence, writes a `driver_record_device_field_test` operation log, stores the record through the dedicated field-test repository, and returns the updated driver task projection. Backend permission denials must block the save rather than falling back locally. This record remains manual field evidence, not an automatic hardware certification; real permission prompts, live photos, physical package-label scans, native scanner SDK validation, and native navigation SDK validation remain follow-up work.

## 2026-07-02 - Record Driver Field Device QA As Manual Evidence First

Driver field-device QA should be recorded as manual evidence before it becomes an automatic pass/fail gate. The driver detail may show readiness preflight results and let an operator record phone model, browser version, observed camera permission, watermark photo, package-label scan, geolocation, file-upload fallback, navigation, and notes as a `DQA-*` record. These statuses represent actual field observations and must not be auto-marked as passed only because browser capability checks look available. The first UI version began as a local prototype record; backend action-log persistence has since been added. Responsible operator review, real permission prompts, live photo capture, physical label scanning, native camera / scanner SDK checks, native navigation validation, and durable cold-start readback remain separate follow-up work.

## 2026-07-02 - Use Driver Device Readiness As A Non-Permission Preflight

Driver device readiness checks should be visible before loading and delivery-proof actions, but they must not trigger camera or location permission prompts. The readiness UI may report browser support for delivery-photo camera capture, package camera scanning, geolocation, and file-upload fallback using capability checks only. Actual permission prompts remain behind explicit driver actions such as `打开相机`, `相机扫码`, or `读取定位`. Missing camera, BarcodeDetector, geolocation, or FileReader support should surface as a fallback state instead of blocking the whole driver task, because scanner-wedge input, typed package IDs, address snapshots, and file upload are still valid P0 fallback paths. This preflight does not replace real-device QA, native mobile camera integration, dedicated scanner SDK validation, or field testing on driver phones.

## 2026-07-02 - Require Package Checklist Verification Before Driver Loading

Driver loading confirmation must require package-level checklist verification before a task can move from waiting delivery to in delivery. The driver screen should use real package records when available and generate temporary fallback rows from package count / quantity only when package records are not yet present. The load-confirm request records checked package IDs and a package-check summary such as `6/6包`; if packages, quantity, goods, or documents do not match, the driver reports a loading exception and the issue returns to warehouse / office handling. Drivers still cannot edit quantities, orders, documents, inventory, prices, statements, or package records from the driver surface; office / packing remain the owners of package details.

## 2026-07-02 - Scope Driver Package Checklists To Fulfillment

Driver loading checklists must be scoped to the current fulfillment record, not only to the order line. When real package rows have a `fulfillmentId`, the driver task may include only packages linked to that fulfillment. Matching by `orderLineId` alone is allowed only for legacy or transitional package rows that have no fulfillment link. The visible row label should remain the operator-friendly `第 N/M 包`, while the actual `packageId` stays visible as supporting metadata for comparing with printed labels and audit records.

## 2026-07-02 - Treat Package Scanning As Current-Task Verification

The first package-scanning boundary for driver loading is scanner-wedge or typed input against the selected driver's current package checklist. A scan may auto-check only a package ID that belongs to that task's checklist; it must not search across other tasks, fulfillments, or order lines. Duplicate scans should be harmless and visible as already checked. Unknown or ambiguous scans should leave the checklist unchanged and return a clear error, because loading mismatch must become a driver loading exception rather than an automatic cross-task correction.

## 2026-07-02 - Keep Browser Camera Scanning As An Explicit Package-Check Input

Browser camera scanning for driver loading must be started only by an explicit driver click, because it can request camera permission. The browser implementation may use `getUserMedia` plus `BarcodeDetector` when available, must stop media tracks after a package match or manual stop, and must keep scanner-wedge / typed input as the fallback path. A camera result is only an input to the same current-task package verification rule; it must still pass through `applyDriverPackageScan(...)` and may not cross-match other tasks, fulfillments, order lines, documents, inventory, or package records. This first P0 browser-camera path does not replace a later native mobile camera module, hardware scanner SDK, or real package-label device QA.

## 2026-07-02 - Keep Driver Dispatch As A Route-Sequence Overlay On Fulfillment

Driver delivery tasks should continue to derive from fulfillment records, while office dispatch planning is stored as a separate route-sequence overlay. The first PostgreSQL table is `driver_delivery_dispatches`, linked to `fulfillment_records`, with driver, route date, route batch, stop sequence, dispatch status, planned departure, assigned-by, assigned-at, and remark fields. Driver task reads join the latest active dispatch row and prioritize route date / batch / stop sequence before falling back to fulfillment status and latest-needed time. Creating and editing dispatch plans uses the independent `fulfillment.dispatch.update` permission and `POST /api/fulfillments/{fulfillmentId}/dispatch`; office and management roles can edit it, while warehouse outbound and driver roles cannot. Route optimization, navigation, and real camera capture remain separate follow-up work; they should not be hidden inside fulfillment status fields.

## 2026-07-02 - Treat Driver Navigation As A Link Boundary First

The first driver navigation implementation should expose a controlled external map-search link from the driver task detail, based on the confirmed delivery address snapshot. ERP should show the route date, route batch, stop sequence, previous / next stop, and planned departure before the driver confirms loading, but it should not claim to optimize routes or embed a native navigation SDK yet. Driver load confirmation can record the displayed route / stop in the remark and toast for traceability. A later native navigation integration must stay separate from office dispatch planning and must not give drivers permission to edit route plans.

## 2026-07-02 - Keep Delivery Completion Separate From Office Evidence Review

Driver delivery completion and office evidence review are separate states. A driver can mark a delivery completed only after submitting required watermarked-photo evidence, but that does not mean the office has accepted the evidence. Office / management users must be able to review the watermarked photo, optional signature photo, watermark ID, capture time, location, and paper-note context, then mark the evidence `已复核` or `需重拍`. Rejected evidence should create an office todo such as `照片待重拍` instead of silently changing delivered quantity, inventory, statement, or driver permissions. Driver users can upload delivery evidence, but they must not get the office review permission.

## 2026-07-02 - Require Watermarked Photo For Driver Delivery Completion

Driver mobile delivery completion must require watermarked delivery-photo evidence before a task can be marked delivered. The driver can confirm loading, report loading / delivery exceptions, and submit delivery completion, but cannot edit order quantity, documents, price, inventory, or statements from the driver surface. The first API boundary reuses the fulfillment transaction for delivered / exception state changes and requires driver-scoped permissions: `delivery.view`, `delivery.load_confirm`, `delivery.complete`, and `delivery.exception.create`. Signature photos and returned paper-note status are useful optional evidence; they must not block delivery completion when a watermarked photo is present.

Delivery-photo evidence should use the shared attachment boundary with the fulfillment as owner. Driver-created watermarked delivery photos use `ownerType=fulfillment` and `purpose=delivery_watermark_photo`; optional signature photos use `purpose=signature_photo`. Driver accounts get the narrower `attachment.delivery_evidence.create` permission for these delivery-evidence attachments only. They do not get general `attachment.create`, so payment screenshots, print artwork, and other business attachments remain outside the default driver role.

## 2026-07-02 - Store Driver Watermark Metadata With The Delivery Evidence

ERP-generated driver delivery watermark data must be stored as structured evidence, not only as text burned into a photo. On delivery completion, the system records a `watermarkId`, capture timestamp, readable watermark text, customer/order reference, delivery address, location label, optional GPS coordinate text, and driver identity. The same metadata is saved on the watermarked delivery-photo attachment and on the fulfillment / driver task record so office staff can audit the evidence independently of image rendering. The current P0 can still upload a browser file input as the photo source; real camera capture remains later implementation work.

## 2026-07-02 - Render Driver Watermark Onto Uploaded Delivery Photo

Driver delivery photos should carry a visible watermark image as well as structured metadata. In the P0 browser flow, the frontend renders the selected source image to Canvas, overlays the ERP watermark text at the bottom, exports a JPEG, and uploads that generated file as the `delivery_watermark_photo` attachment. The structured metadata remains the authoritative audit source, and the upload records whether the image was `pixel_watermark_rendered` or fell back to `metadata_only`. Real in-app camera capture, office review tooling, and long-term evidence retention remain separate follow-up work.

## 2026-07-02 - Keep Browser Delivery Photo Capture Behind The Watermark Pipeline

Browser delivery-photo capture is an input source for the existing driver evidence pipeline, not a separate completion path. It must be started only by an explicit driver click because it may request camera permission, and it must stop media tracks when the driver stops, changes tasks, or leaves the camera flow. Captured frames should become a normal image `File` / `Blob` and then continue through the same Canvas watermark rendering, attachment upload, delivery completion, and office evidence-review boundaries as manually selected files. Drivers still cannot mark delivery complete without watermarked-photo evidence, and browser capture does not replace future native mobile camera integration, field device QA, or evidence retention policy.

## 2026-07-02 - Enforce Void Before Reprint For Fulfillment Documents

Fulfillment print-record lifecycle applies to both labels and formal pickup / delivery documents. Once a pickup note, delivery note, or express / LTL label has an active printed record, the user must void the old print record before creating a reprint; a reprint must link back to the voided previous record. While a pickup or delivery document is voided, the office UI must not allow completing the pickup / delivery until a new valid document is printed. UI wording should follow the fulfillment method: `包裹标签`, `自提单`, or `送货单`, instead of using label-only language for formal documents.

## 2026-07-02 - Derive Fulfillment Print Document Type From Fulfillment Method

Fulfillment print routes must derive the document type from the fulfillment method as the authoritative source: `快递快运` uses `express_ltl_label`, `自提` uses `pickup_note`, and `送货` uses `delivery_note`. Request bodies may carry a template hint, but they must not cause a pickup or delivery document to be routed to the express / LTL label printer. Dot-matrix pickup and delivery notes may show price snapshot fields, while express / LTL labels must continue to hide money fields.

## 2026-07-02 - Use The Print Command Bridge As A Spool Boundary First

The first production-oriented command wrapper is `scripts/print-command-bridge.mjs`, and its default mode is `spool_only`. It validates the adapter JSON payload, checks the CLI job/device identifiers against stdin, writes a queued bridge job under `.erp-local-storage/print-command-bridge/queued/`, and returns a stable `externalJobId` to the adapter. This confirms that the local bridge accepted the job record; it does not prove that CUPS, a label printer, a dot-matrix printer, or a vendor SDK physically printed paper. Any mode that touches real devices must be added behind explicit configuration, device allowlists, physical QA, and status readback or callback evidence.

## 2026-07-02 - Treat Command Bridge Success As Submitted, Not Printed

The `command_bridge` printer adapter may execute a configured local command after system printing, command configuration, and device allowlist checks pass. A zero exit code only means the local bridge accepted or submitted the job, so ERP sets the print job to `sent`, not `printed`. Physical completion still requires a trusted driver callback, a future status poller, or an operator confirmation with an operation log. The adapter must not expose command paths, argument values, stdout, or stderr in API responses; it may expose sanitized exit code, signal, and byte counts for diagnostics.

## 2026-07-02 - Keep Real Printer Bridges Explicit And Guarded

Real system-printer integration must stay behind the print-driver adapter configuration boundary. Enabling `ERP_SYSTEM_PRINTER_ENABLED` is not enough to touch a physical device: the runtime must also select a supported bridge kind, configure the bridge, and pass the device allowlist. The API may expose whether a command is configured, but it must not expose the command value. The command bridge can now submit through a configured local command, but it remains unproven for physical QA and printer-status readback; vendor SDK bridges must still fail with explicit guarded error codes until implemented.

## 2026-07-02 - Use A Dedicated Service Account For Driver Callbacks

Print-driver callbacks and polling results must use a dedicated ERP service account with explicit callback permission. The P0 skeleton uses `U-PRINT-DRIVER-A` / `print.driver.a`, whose only action permission is `print.job.callback`; warehouse and office printing permissions such as `fulfillment.print` are not enough to call `/api/print-jobs/{printJobId}/driver-status`. Operation logs and persisted driver-status events use the authenticated service account as `operatorId`; any free-form callback body `operatorId` is retained only as reported metadata. This keeps PostgreSQL foreign keys valid and prevents arbitrary callback payloads from impersonating office users.

## 2026-07-02 - Poll Printer Status Through The Driver Adapter

Printer status polling must go through the print-driver adapter and the `print.job.callback` service-account boundary. Business routes may create, dispatch, retry, or manually mark print jobs, but they must not guess physical print success. A polling task can call `/api/print-jobs/{printJobId}/poll-status` or `/api/print-jobs/status-poll`; when the adapter reports a concrete status, the API writes it through the same driver-status transition rules and operation-log attribution used by callbacks. The dry-run adapter may simulate `sent -> printed` for `DRY-*` jobs, while guarded / unimplemented OS adapters must return no mutation or an explicit adapter-missing result.

## 2026-07-02 - Dispatch Print Jobs Through A Guarded Driver Adapter

Real printer calls must go through a print-driver adapter boundary rather than being embedded in fulfillment, print-record, or print-job route handlers. The default P0 adapter is guarded: preview-only jobs are skipped, pending / manual / browser jobs remain queued, and system-printer jobs fail explicitly until a concrete OS or vendor adapter is installed. A dispatch attempt can move a job to queued, sent, preview_only, or failed, but it must not by itself prove that paper was physically printed. Final printed status should come from a trusted callback, polling result, or manual operator confirmation with an operation log.

## 2026-07-02 - Separate Print Records From Print Execution Jobs

Printing must keep two separate concepts. `print_records` are the business record for a document or label version: template, target order / package / statement, device snapshot, print action, voiding, and reprint lifecycle. `print_jobs` are execution attempts against a device or driver: queue status, driver mode, attempt number, source failed job, error code / message, payload snapshot, and status callbacks. A reprint or retry must not overwrite the old record; it creates traceable new records linked back to the source. While devices remain `preview_only`, jobs may stay in `preview_only`; real OS / driver integration should update the job status rather than directly mutating fulfillment, order, inventory, or statement records.

## 2026-06-25 - Keep This As A Frontend Prototype First

The migrated project remains a frontend-only prototype until the production workflow is stable. This keeps the design and interaction model easy to revise before introducing backend contracts.

## 2026-06-29 - Keep P0 Fixture Data And Parsing Outside App.jsx

The P0 office prototype should keep large fixture datasets and rule-based order parsing outside `src/App.jsx`. Business sample records live in `src/data/fixtures.js`, and local recognition logic lives in `src/lib/orderParser.js`. `App.jsx` should stay focused on page composition and local interaction state so later API contracts can replace fixtures without rewriting the UI surface.

## 2026-06-29 - Separate Custom Print Type From Base Bag Style

In order entry and review, custom printed order lines should show the visible business type `定制印刷` instead of appearing as plain `空白袋` rows in the table. `空白袋` remains the base bag / inventory style (`底袋款式`) and should stay available in detail data for stock matching and production context. This keeps office staff from mistaking a printed custom order for an ordinary blank-stock order while preserving the inventory key semantics.

## 2026-06-29 - Use Factory Shorthand For Custom Fulfillment Specs

Outbound / fulfillment lists, package labels, and document previews should show custom product specs in the factory's compact wording: product name, size, compact color spec, print side, quantity, and notes. Compact color spec uses bag color plus print color, such as `白印黑` for white bag printed black, and bag color plus handle color, such as `白袋黑提` for white bag with black handles. If both print color and handle color apply, show both, e.g. `白印黑 / 白袋黑提`, while detail views keep the structured bag color, print color, and handle color fields for checking.

## 2026-06-29 - Parse Factory Shorthand Into Structured Custom-Print Fields

P0 order entry recognition should treat factory shorthand as structured input, not as a display-only string. When customers write `白印黑`, `黄印黑`, `白袋黑提`, `黄袋红提`, `白色袋子印刷黑色`, or `黑色提手`, the draft should extract bag color, print color, handle color, print side, and notes where possible. The editable side panel remains the place for office staff to correct print color, print side, artwork status, handle color, and note before saving a formal order.

## 2026-06-29 - Treat Ounuo Bag-Making Counters As Machine Cycles

The Ounuo bag-making machine's built-in counter / daily report uses a machine action or cycle count. Even if the machine screen labels the column as output, ERP should not treat that value as qualified finished-goods quantity. ERP screens, reports, and data fields should label it as `机器计数` / `动作次数` / process telemetry, and should compare it against manual qualified bag-making reports, packing counts, inbound records, and abnormal-loss records only for variance analysis. It must not directly create inventory, determine fulfillment quantity, determine chargeable quantity, or become payroll piece-count evidence. If a future machine exposes a reliable cycle-to-piece multiplier, the derived result must still be marked as an estimate until reconciled with qualified reporting or packing counts.

## 2026-06-30 - Preserve Unpaid Statement Variance As Debt

When a customer underpays a statement, P0 must record an explicit variance-handling result before write-off. If the result is unpaid variance carried to debt, the amount must remain visible as debt / variance and be carried into later statements; the write-off action must not silently clear it. Only no-variance statements, or statements with a clearing result such as approved rounding / allowance, can move to a settled / written-off state.

## 2026-07-01 - Register Payment Screenshots Before Payment Records

P0 payment evidence should use the shared attachment API boundary. When office or finance records a received payment with a screenshot, the frontend first registers a `payment_screenshot` attachment against the statement, then passes the returned `attachmentId` into the payment-record API. The first seed permission for this is `attachment.create`, granted to office, finance, and management roles, while warehouse and driver roles cannot create payment evidence.

## 2026-07-01 - Keep Statement Export Lists As Metadata

Statement export listing APIs should return version metadata such as file name, preview type, token, operation log, content type, and creation time, but not the file content itself. File bytes remain behind the tokenized download route. The office detail pane should show the latest generated export status first; a fuller customer-send / internal-archive history can be added later without changing the download boundary.

## 2026-07-01 - Use Local Filesystem As Temporary Attachment Storage

Until object storage is configured, the P0 backend skeleton stores attachment bytes under `.erp-local-storage/attachments/` and returns storage metadata through `storageProvider`, `storageKey`, and `contentDigest`. The frontend should continue to read attachment content through `/api/attachments/{attachmentId}/content` rather than depending on local paths. This keeps the API shape close to a future object-storage implementation while keeping generated runtime files out of Git.

## 2026-07-01 - Use Local JSON As Temporary Attachment Record Index

Until the attachment table is backed by PostgreSQL, the P0 backend skeleton persists attachment summaries and owner links in `.erp-local-storage/metadata/attachment-records.json`. API server startup loads this index so an uploaded local attachment can still be read by `attachmentId` after a restart. This JSON index is a development bridge only; formal attachment records, access scoping, retention, and audit history still belong in the database.

## 2026-07-01 - List Attachment Summaries By Business Owner

Attachment discovery should use owner-scoped metadata queries rather than scanning file paths or embedding file bytes in business records. `GET /api/attachments` returns summaries filtered by `ownerType`, `ownerId`, `purpose`, and `fileType`; file content remains behind `/api/attachments/{attachmentId}/content`. This keeps payment screenshots, print artwork, delivery photos, and future evidence files on one shared attachment boundary.

## 2026-07-01 - Hydrate Statement Payment Evidence From Attachment Queries

Statement screens should restore payment screenshot summaries by querying the shared attachment boundary with `ownerType=statement`, the statement ID, and `purpose=payment_screenshot`. The statement record can keep lightweight payment status and linked IDs, but attachment metadata and content lookup stay on the attachment API so future print artwork, delivery proof, and payment proof follow the same owner-scoped retrieval pattern.

## 2026-07-01 - Preview Payment Evidence Through The Attachment API

Payment proof previews should open through a lightweight in-app viewer after reading content from `/api/attachments/{attachmentId}/content`. The frontend may cache the returned image as a data URL for the current session, but it should not expose local filesystem paths or depend on attachment storage internals. This keeps the current local-file skeleton compatible with future object storage, signed URLs, and owner-scoped access checks.

## 2026-07-01 - Keep Attachment Bytes Outside The Database

Formal attachment tables should store metadata, owner links, storage provider/key, content digest, thumbnail location, signed URL expiry, and audit fields. File bytes should stay in object storage or the current local filesystem bridge, not in PostgreSQL rows. Business modules should find files through `attachment_links` and read content through the attachment API boundary.

## 2026-07-01 - Put Attachment Metadata Behind A Repository Boundary

Attachment API routes should read and write attachment summaries through a repository boundary instead of directly touching local JSON files. The default P0 repository remains `local_json` so the prototype works without PostgreSQL. When explicitly configured with `ERP_ATTACHMENT_STORE=postgres` plus `DATABASE_URL` or `ERP_ATTACHMENT_DATABASE_URL`, the same attachment API can write metadata and owner links to the PostgreSQL `attachments` and `attachment_links` tables while file bytes continue to stay outside the database. This is a transition step toward database-backed attachments, not a replacement for full business transactions, object storage, signed URL generation, thumbnails, or owner-scoped access isolation.

## 2026-07-01 - Use Short-Lived Attachment Access URLs

Attachment previews and downloads should not depend on local filesystem paths or long-lived public URLs. The P0 backend can issue short-lived API-proxy access URLs after `attachment.view` permission has been checked; the content route then accepts the signed token until expiry. In production this boundary can be replaced by object-storage signed URLs while preserving the frontend contract. Short-lived URLs are a delivery mechanism only and do not replace owner-scoped authorization, audit logging, retention rules, or object-storage configuration.

## 2026-07-01 - Put Attachment Bytes Behind An Object-Storage Boundary

Attachment byte writes, reads, content digests, safe storage keys, and short-lived access URL signing should go through an object-storage interface rather than living inside route handlers. The default P0 implementation is `local_fs`, backed by `.erp-local-storage/attachments/`, so the prototype continues to run without cloud storage. A placeholder `object_storage` mode exists to keep the interface explicit, but it is intentionally not configured yet. Production work should replace the local implementation with real object storage while keeping the same `putObject`, `readObject`, and access URL boundary.

## 2026-07-01 - Use S3-Compatible Signing For The First Object-Storage Adapter

The first production-oriented attachment object-storage adapter should be S3-compatible rather than tied to a single vendor SDK. `ERP_ATTACHMENT_OBJECT_STORAGE=object_storage` reads endpoint, bucket, region, access key, secret key, optional session token, key prefix, and path-style settings, then signs PUT / GET requests and direct GET access URLs with AWS Signature V4. This keeps AWS S3, Aliyun OSS S3-compatible endpoints, Tencent COS S3-compatible endpoints, MinIO, and similar storage options behind one boundary. Missing credentials must fail explicitly; the default development mode remains `local_fs`.

## 2026-07-01 - Record Attachment Access Through Operation Logs First

Attachment access URL creation and successful attachment content reads should write audit records before a dedicated access-audit table is introduced. The P0 backend records `attachment_access_url_created` and `attachment_content_read` in the existing operation-log boundary and exposes an attachment-scoped access-log query guarded by `attachment.view`. This gives office users and API checks traceability now, while leaving the production design open for a persistent attachment-access table, owner-level authorization policy, retention rules, and object-storage provider event reconciliation.

## 2026-07-02 - Adjust Formal Order Quantity Through Reservation-Aware Transactions

Formal order-line quantity changes must go through a dedicated order-change transaction rather than directly editing the visible quantity. For not-yet-produced and not-yet-delivered lines, a decrease releases the corresponding excess active inventory reservation, and an increase must re-check available inventory before adding reservation quantity. The same action updates not-yet-delivered fulfillment expected quantity, writes inventory ledger rows, records an order-line change record, and writes an operation log. Warehouse and finance users cannot adjust formal order quantity directly; office and management roles hold the first seed permission `order.quantity.adjust`.

## 2026-07-02 - Reprice Quantity Adjustments With A New Snapshot

Formal order-line quantity adjustment should not overwrite the original confirmation price snapshot. The system creates a new `quantity_adjustment` price snapshot that preserves the original unit-price basis, recalculates final receivable from the new chargeable quantity, and records the reason in the order-line change and operation log. If statement lines already exist for that order line, the same transaction updates the affected statement lines and recomputes the statement receivable / variance so order-pool detail, documents, and reconciliation do not diverge.

## 2026-07-02 - Cancel Fulfillment Without Closing The Order Line

Cancelling a not-yet-delivered fulfillment task is different from voiding the formal order line. The fulfillment cancellation route sets the fulfillment record to `已取消`, releases any active reservation for the same order line, decreases reserved inventory, writes a `fulfillment_cancel` inventory ledger row, and writes an operation log, but it does not close or void the order line. The first seed permission is `fulfillment.cancel`, granted to office and management roles only; warehouse/outbound users should report quantity mismatch or unable-to-outbound exceptions instead of cancelling formal fulfillment tasks.

## 2026-07-02 - Read The Order Pool Through A Repository Boundary

Order-pool list and detail APIs should not depend on the API server's transient workspace once formal order rows exist in PostgreSQL. `GET /api/order-lines` and `GET /api/order-lines/{id}` should read through an order-pool read repository: local mode preserves the P0 workspace projection, while PostgreSQL mode joins formal order rows, latest price snapshots, fulfillment records, inventory reservations, and statement state into a frontend-ready read model. The list response keeps legacy frontend aliases such as `product`, `color`, `status`, and `fulfillment` during migration, but the canonical fields are `productName`, `bagColor`, `lineStatus`, `fulfillmentMethod`, `customerName`, and `financeState`.

## 2026-07-01 - Put Attachment Access Audit Behind A Repository Boundary

Attachment access audit should use its own repository boundary and table shape instead of relying only on generic operation logs. The default P0 implementation writes access events to `.erp-local-storage/metadata/attachment-access-logs.json` so signed URL creation and content reads survive API server restart. The PostgreSQL boundary targets `attachment_access_logs` with a separate access-log ID plus the related `operationLogId`, preserving both a durable security audit trail and the existing generic operation history.

## 2026-07-01 - Show Payment Proof Access Audit In The Viewer

Payment screenshot access history should be visible at the point where office or finance staff inspect the proof, not only in a backend log query. The payment-proof viewer should show a compact recent access list with action, operator, access mode, and time. This keeps sensitive payment evidence traceable during normal reconciliation work while leaving full retention, export, and owner-scoped security audit policies to the backend access-audit boundary.

## 2026-07-01 - Use Docker PostgreSQL For Skeleton Live Checks

Until a shared test database is provided, backend database smoke checks should use a temporary Docker PostgreSQL container rather than only SQL string assertions. The live check applies the same migration files, runs repository writes and queries against real tables, and then starts the API server with PostgreSQL repositories. This catches statement-snapshot, foreign-key, and `psql` output-shape problems before the formal backend stack is chosen, while keeping the normal development default on local JSON / local filesystem storage.

## 2026-07-01 - Put Payment Records Behind A Repository Boundary

Statement payment records should move behind a repository boundary before the full statement transaction is implemented. The default P0 adapter remains in-memory so the prototype runs without a database; the PostgreSQL adapter writes `payment_records` and preserves linked payment screenshot attachment IDs through `evidence_attachment_id`. This is deliberately narrower than full statement persistence: statement status updates, variance handling, todo creation, and write-off transactions still need a later cross-table database boundary.

## 2026-07-01 - Commit Payment Recording As One Statement Transaction

Recording a statement payment should not persist the payment row separately from the statement status, underpayment todo, and operation log. The first PostgreSQL transaction boundary for this flow updates `statements`, writes `payment_records`, optionally writes the `收款差额待确认` todo, and writes `operation_logs` together. Variance handling and write-off remain separate follow-up transaction boundaries because they represent later business decisions, not the initial payment registration.

## 2026-07-01 - Commit Statement Variance And Write-Off As Settlement Transactions

Variance handling and write-off are settlement decisions after payment registration, so they should have their own transaction boundary instead of being folded into the initial payment record. Handling a variance updates `statements`, writes `variance_records`, optionally creates the follow-up todo, and writes `operation_logs` together. Confirming debt or write-off updates the statement and operation log together. Statement export file persistence and statement lines remain separate follow-up persistence boundaries.

## 2026-07-01 - Commit Statement Send Marking As One Transaction

Marking a statement as sent should persist the statement status / last-sent time, the `statement_send_records` row, and the operation log together. The send record may reference the customer-send export token when one exists. Export file metadata/content persistence remains a separate repository boundary so preview generation and send marking can evolve independently while sharing the same download token.

## 2026-07-02 - Persist Statement Export Files Behind A Repository Boundary

Statement preview exports should persist metadata and generated workbook content through a statement-export repository instead of staying in the API server's in-memory array. The default P0 adapter remains in-memory for local prototype speed; the PostgreSQL adapter writes `statement_export_files` with the download token, file name, content type, generated `.xlsx` base64 fallback content, digest, operation log id, creator, timestamp, content encoding, and metadata. Export list APIs should keep returning metadata only, while the tokenized download route reads the stored file object first and falls back to database content only for compatibility or recovery. Generated export files should now be written through the statement-export object-storage boundary first, with database content retained as a compatibility fallback so existing download routes and historical records keep working.

## 2026-07-02 - Store Statement Export Workbooks Before Repository Persistence

Statement export workbooks should have a file-storage record before their metadata is committed. The P0 default writes to local file storage under `statement-exports/<downloadToken>/...`; production-style deployments can set `ERP_STATEMENT_EXPORT_OBJECT_STORAGE=object_storage` and statement-specific S3-compatible bucket credentials. The statement export repository keeps `storage_provider`, `storage_key`, `content_digest`, and metadata alongside the database fallback content. Download routes should prefer the stored object and fall back to `content_text` only for compatibility or recovery. Export-list APIs may expose storage provider, digest, content length, and whether a storage key exists, but should not expose raw object keys or workbook content.

## 2026-07-02 - Commit Statement Preview Lines With The Export

Generating a statement preview is the point where billable statement detail lines are materialized for the current skeleton. The PostgreSQL path should replace the statement's `statement_lines`, write the export file record/content, and write the preview operation log in one transaction. This keeps the exported workbook, the database statement-line snapshot, and the audit event aligned. Future final Excel templates can still reuse the same boundary; broader order, inventory, and fulfillment writes remain separate business transactions.

## 2026-07-02 - Commit Order Confirmation Core Rows As One Transaction

Confirming an order draft is the first point where the prototype creates formal business records. The PostgreSQL path should write the original order, order lines, price snapshots, fulfillment records, and the confirmation operation log in one transaction so the order pool, price history, and fulfillment queue do not diverge. Inventory reservations / ledger entries, shortage todos, and later fulfillment status actions remain separate follow-up transaction boundaries because they have their own stock and exception rules.

## 2026-07-02 - Commit Order Reservations With Order Confirmation

Inventory reservation is part of accepting a stock order, not a later display-only side effect. When order confirmation creates formal order lines, the PostgreSQL path should also write reservation rows, update the reserved quantity on the matched inventory item, write inventory ledger entries, and create shortage todos in the same transaction. Fulfillment completion, cancellation, inventory release, production, packing, and delivery movements remain later transaction boundaries because they happen after confirmation and have different quantity rules.

## 2026-07-02 - Commit Fulfillment Actions As One Transaction

Fulfillment actions are business state changes, not display-only UI events. Printing labels, completing self-pickup or delivery, confirming express/LTL pickup, and reporting fulfillment exceptions should update the fulfillment record and write the related print record, exception record, office todo, and operation log in one PostgreSQL transaction. When a completion action has an active inventory reservation for the order line, the same transaction should also release that reservation, deduct the delivered quantity from on-hand stock, and write the outbound inventory ledger. Cancellation / rollback release flows, legacy unreserved stock deduction, production movements, and packing movements remain separate policy branches because they need explicit quantity and reversal rules.

## 2026-07-02 - Deduct Reserved Inventory On Fulfillment Completion

Inventory should only be deducted automatically at fulfillment completion when the order line has an active reservation created by order confirmation. This prevents old seed or legacy fulfillment records without reservation evidence from being accidentally deducted. For reserved stock, completion marks the reservation as `已出库`, decreases `inventory_items.on_hand_qty` by the delivered quantity, decreases `inventory_items.reserved_qty` by the reserved quantity, and writes an `inventory_ledger_entries` row with `source_type=fulfillment_complete` or `fulfillment_pickup`.

## 2026-07-02 - Allow Explicit Legacy Unreserved Stock Deduction
