Legacy or imported fulfillment records that lack per-order reservation rows must not be silently deducted by default. A completion or pickup confirmation may deduct without a reservation only when the request explicitly enables legacy stock matching, the order line is stock/common-goods or printed stock, the system can uniquely match the finished-goods inventory item by size, color, handle type, and style, and inventory is sufficient. If the legacy record's visible state shows old aggregate reserved stock such as `已占用` or `待提货锁定`, the same transaction also decreases reserved quantity; otherwise it only decreases on-hand quantity. The ledger source type must use `fulfillment_complete_legacy` or `fulfillment_pickup_legacy` so migrated deductions can be audited separately from normal reservation-backed fulfillment.

## 2026-07-02 - Commit Production Reports And Packing Completion Separately

Production report completion and packing completion are separate business events and should not share one implicit quantity update. A production report uses the manually confirmed qualified quantity as the only source for finished-goods inventory increase and customer/order-line reservation; machine count / cycle count is stored only as evidence and must not create inventory, fulfillment quantity, chargeable quantity, or payroll quantity. Packing completion creates package records and moves the order toward label printing or pickup confirmation, but it does not deduct inventory. Inventory deduction remains tied to fulfillment completion or express/LTL pickup confirmation so packed-but-not-delivered goods stay visibly reserved.

## 2026-07-02 - Release Reservations With A Dedicated Inventory Transaction

Manual reservation release, cancellation-style release, and quantity-change release should use a dedicated inventory reservation release transaction rather than being folded into unrelated fulfillment state changes. The transaction updates the reservation status/remaining quantity, decreases `inventory_items.reserved_qty`, writes a release ledger entry, and writes an operation log together. A partially released reservation remains active for its remaining quantity, so later fulfillment completion can deduct only the remaining reserved amount.

## 2026-07-02 - Void Formal Order Lines With A Transaction And Audit Trail

Formal order lines that are not yet produced, closed, or delivered should be voided through an explicit order-line void transaction, not deleted or silently edited. The transaction closes the order line, cancels any not-yet-delivered fulfillment records, releases active inventory reservations for that line, decreases reserved inventory, writes an inventory release ledger entry, records an order-line change record, and writes an operation log together. Warehouse/outbound accounts can report fulfillment exceptions, but formal order-line void remains an office / management action.

## 2026-06-30 - Use Office Mock Service As The API Boundary

Before a real backend exists, P0 office pages should call `src/services/officeMockService.js` for workspace loading and API-shaped business actions. The service can delegate to local fixtures and state helpers during the prototype phase, but page components should progressively stop importing future-API business helpers directly. When the API contract is approved, this service layer becomes the replacement point for a real HTTP client while preserving page behavior.

## 2026-06-25 - Preserve Visual QA Evidence

The `audit/` and `screenshots/` folders are kept in the repository because they document the visual target, responsive checks, and known product-design tradeoffs from the previous conversation.

## 2026-06-25 - Do Not Commit Generated Runtime Folders

`node_modules/`, `dist/`, `.npm-cache/`, and the old absolute-path `.npmrc` are excluded from migration. Dependencies should be recreated from `package-lock.json`.

## 2026-06-25 - Upgrade Vite Patch Version During Migration

Vite was upgraded from `6.4.2` to `6.4.3` in the new repository because `npm audit` reported a high-severity advisory against `vite <=6.4.2`. The app still builds successfully after the patch upgrade.

## 2026-06-25 - Treat The Factory Scheduling Board As The First Product Surface

The first usable screen stays the production board, not a landing page or admin shell. The current scope is scheduling visibility and actionability for small-batch packaging production.

## 2026-06-25 - Archive Old Thread Context In The Repository

The old Codex thread `设计中小工厂ERP系统` cannot be embedded as app-side chat history in this current thread, so its readable history was migrated into `docs/conversation/` and its implementation-facing requirements were distilled into `docs/product/requirements.md`.

## 2026-06-25 - Product Requirements Override Earlier Narrow V1 Plans

Early plans focused only on inventory, out/in stock, and finance. Later user clarifications added online/offline orders, factory production, silk-screen and bag-making scheduling, mobile workshop reporting, delivery/pickup proof, statements, and price-table logic. Future implementation should follow `docs/product/requirements.md`, not the earliest narrow V1 scope.

## 2026-06-25 - Order Entry Should Start With Paste Recognition

Order entry should support a paste-to-parse dialog similar to express address recognition. Customer text should be parsed into size, color, quantity, print requirement, print content, and other order fields, then shown for human review. Multiple styles, colors, or print contents in one pasted message should be split into separate orders or sub-orders.

## 2026-06-25 - Enterprise WeChat Group Orders Are A Primary Input

Many customers place orders in Enterprise WeChat group chats. The order parser should handle copied group-chat text and extract customer name, fulfillment method, and latest delivery time when present, but missing fields should be judged by order type. For stock/common-goods orders, a clear customer/customer group, size/model, color, and quantity are enough to form a valid draft/order; missing fulfillment method or latest needed time is non-blocking pending confirmation, and the system can tell the customer the available stock and estimated ship-ready / pickup-ready time. If extended handle is not mentioned, default to normal handle; if style keywords such as 小熊, 喜, 福, or 覆膜 do not appear, default to plain/blank bags. For custom printed orders, missing print image, artwork/file, print content, print color, print position, or other production-critical print requirements should route to missing information. Missing latest shipment time on a custom order is a scheduling risk / pending confirmation, not necessarily an invalid order if production-critical information is complete. The business can gradually guide customers toward a more consistent order text format.

## 2026-06-26 - Every Worker Uses An Individual Account

Every person in the factory should use an individual account rather than shared person or machine accounts. Permissions vary by role, such as office staff, production supervisor, silk-screen worker, bag-making worker, packing worker, driver, and management. Operation records should default to the current logged-in account and should not be manually editable in normal workflow, including reporting, photos, packing, outbound, delivery confirmation, payment entry, price override, and review. Mobile screens should only show tasks and actions allowed by the current account's role and permissions.

## 2026-06-26 - Bag-Making Work Visibility Follows Machine Shift Schedule

Bag-making workers usually default to fixed machines, but the system should not hard-code a permanent machine-worker relationship because workers may rotate, change shifts, take leave, or temporarily cover another machine. V1 should keep scheduling lightweight: day-level schedule plus temporary substitution, without complex attendance shifts. Because the factory mainly runs a long day shift, the default can be one shift per day. Office staff or the production supervisor can update which worker operates each machine for the current day. A bag-making account can see a machine's work orders only when the current schedule assigns that account to the machine. Schedule records, actual operator, machine, reported quantity, photos, and timestamps should become the data foundation for later piece-rate / attendance / payroll calculation. V1 records only the foundation data; payroll calculation, morning/afternoon shifts, overtime, and leave-hour details remain later modules.

## 2026-06-26 - Silk-Screen Uses A Workshop Task Pool With Fixed Worker-Machine Defaults

The 4 silk-screen machines are generally bound to 4 fixed silk-screen workers, one worker per machine, but office scheduling should use a silk-screen task pool. Office staff assign same-day print orders to the silk-screen workshop / task pool instead of directly assigning each order to machine 1-4 or forcing a fixed sequence. The silk-screen workshop decides actual order and machine arrangement based on plate, print color, material availability, setup state, and delivery urgency. At the start of the workday, silk-screen workers can mark / claim orders from the task pool that they plan to print. A marked order is locked so other silk-screen workers cannot mark it again. Marking / claiming does not mean printing has started; it only means the worker is preparing to handle that order. The worker who marked it can cancel the mark, and office / supervisor can cancel it or assign the order to a specific worker. When the worker clicks `start printing`, the order enters `silk-screen in progress`, and the system records actual operator, actual machine from the worker's bound machine, and start time. The print photo must be uploaded after `start printing` and before `printing completed`; without it, the silk-screen worker cannot submit completion. In rare exceptions, office staff or the production supervisor can override / release the block from the backend, with reason, releaser, and release time recorded. When printing is completed, the worker submits actual printed quantity and finish time. The task pool can show suggested sequencing, but V1 should not force the workshop to follow it.

## 2026-06-25 - Stock Orders Should Auto-Check Inventory

Stock/common-goods orders are roughly 60% of current business. After order entry, the system should automatically check finished-goods inventory by size, color, warehouse/location, and available quantity. If stock is sufficient, the order follows the stock outbound flow. If insufficient, the system should generate or recommend a production work order. The system should also provide inventory warnings and replenishment suggestions by color and size.

For stock/common-goods order lines, V1 can automatically move to waiting for outbound only when parsing is clear, inventory is sufficient and trustworthy, pricing is complete, and there is no strong customer/order risk requiring manual handling. If fulfillment method or latest needed time is missing but the stock-core fields are clear, the line should move to fulfillment pending confirmation rather than missing information; it should not generate a concrete outbound / delivery task until the fulfillment method is confirmed. Ambiguous parsing, mainly workshop-reported / loose / unreviewed inventory, missing or failed price calculation, manual price override needs, strong customer risk, management-confirmation-required customers, abnormal notes, or missing production-critical fields on custom/scheduled-production/rush orders should keep the line pending office confirmation. Normal non-blocking risk hints do not stop automatic waiting-for-outbound.

Customer-group automatic recognition can show only a recognition-time inventory snapshot; it is not an inventory reservation. Order drafts do not reserve inventory, but should store the recognition-time available quantity, source, location, query time, and source confidence. When office staff creates a formal order, the system must re-check inventory and immediately reserve / lock it if still sufficient. A formal stock order in fulfillment pending confirmation should still reserve inventory once customer intent is clear, so goods are not taken by another order while delivery/pickup/needed time is being confirmed. Pending-fulfillment reservations should not auto-release after a timer; instead they enter a reminder list where staff chooses to keep holding, cancel, change quantity, or contact the customer. Initial reminder timing should be urgency-based: urgent/today/immediate/this-afternoon stock orders remind after 10 minutes, normal stock orders after 30 minutes, old-customer non-urgent orders that only miss pickup/delivery/express method after 2 hours, and unresolved items near closing enter an end-of-day pending list. Fulfillment pending confirmation should be a shared task pool, not individual assignment. The pool is visible to the two office clerks, the owner mother's / management mobile account, and production management / supervisor, while the two office clerks remain the primary operators. The system records the actual operator for each confirm/cancel/change/hold/contact action. Manual stock/common-goods order entry should offer two save actions: save and wait for outbound when fulfillment method, needed time, inventory, and price are clear, and save to fulfillment pending confirmation when fulfillment method or needed time is still unclear. The direct outbound save action re-checks and reserves inventory, creates the formal order, and records entry operator/action time/reservation result; the pending-confirmation save action creates the formal order, reserves inventory, and enters the shared pool without creating a concrete outbound / delivery task. Save and wait for outbound must not automatically print outbound/delivery/pickup notes; it only creates the waiting-for-outbound task and inventory reservation. Printing is a separate manual task action controlled by account permissions. V1 defaults to office staff as the only users with document printing permission; outbound workers, drivers, and workshop accounts are not default printers. First print and reprint both require print permission. Reprints should record reasons such as customer quantity change, damaged paper, lost document, information change, or other, with printer, print time, print count, electronic note number, paper note number, and reprint history retained. If inventory changed between recognition and review, the review screen should show the delta, such as recognized-time available 500 and current available 0, and route the line to shortage confirmation / customer decision rather than continuing to promise in-stock fulfillment. A final inventory check is still required before outbound.

When stock/common-goods inventory is insufficient, V1 should not automatically create production work orders. Stock/common-goods orders should check inventory per order line because customers often place many small stock lines together. Lines with stock can enter waiting for outbound, while lines without stock should wait for office / customer confirmation on whether the customer will wait. If the customer waits, create a shortage production / replenishment task linked to the original order line and prioritize the finished goods for that customer. The waiting line status should move from waiting for customer decision to wait, to waiting for replenishment / production, to scheduled, to production completed / waiting for outbound, to completed. Waiting for replenishment / production should show that the customer agreed to wait, the predicted lead time / estimated ship-ready date, and shortage quantity. If the customer does not wait, cancel / close that line while other available lines continue outbound. Printed custom orders do not use this stock-waiting logic because they are made to order. The system should show required quantity, available quantity, shortage quantity, inventory source / confidence, and predicted production lead time or estimated ship-ready date. V1 predicted lead time should be conservative and rule-based: use existing replenishment / machine plans when available, otherwise estimate from capable machines plus earliest schedulable date and expected production duration, add setup buffers for mold / handle changes or material waits, and fall back to estimated time pending confirmation when uncertain. Later versions should improve prediction from accumulated data such as historical production speed, actual machine capacity, mold-change duration, replenishment cycles, seasonality, and order completion records. It should generate copyable WeChat / Enterprise WeChat message text that office staff can edit before sending; V1 should not automatically send it to customers. After customer reply, office staff chooses all-to-production, ship inventory plus produce shortage, change accepted quantity, wait for unavailable lines, cancel unavailable lines, change quantity / color, or confirm later. When the customer changes quantity, color, or style because the original stock line is unavailable, the system should close / cancel the original unavailable line with a reason and create a new line that reruns parsing, inventory lookup, inventory reservation, price snapshot, and outbound / replenishment flow. The new line should default to the original customer order time and original fulfillment method, record the change time, require office confirmation for latest ship date, and use the price table version matched by the original customer order time. If the customer request is actually a later add-on, it should be treated as a new order / new line priced by the new customer order time. When the customer agrees to wait, the system should generate another copyable confirmation message explaining which lines ship first, which lines wait for replenishment / production, and the estimated ship-ready date; this also should not be sent automatically. Confirm later should create a follow-up reminder, defaulting to 2 hours but editable. If the latest delivery / pickup time is approaching or the customer keeps not replying, the system should strongly remind staff and recommend a phone call. Confirm-later records should store last contact time, next reminder time, responsible account, and notes. The system should not automatically split insufficient-stock orders without human confirmation.

If a customer changes quantity, color, or style because of unavailable stock and the newly created line is also unavailable, the system should return to the same waiting-for-customer-decision loop instead of creating a separate special flow. It should generate a new copyable customer message for the new unavailable line and retain the full change chain, including original line, new line, closure reason, customer confirmation record, change time, and current handling status.

When a customer confirms they will wait for an unavailable stock/common-goods line, the generated shortage production / replenishment task should split quantity into customer-locked quantity and opportunistic stock replenishment suggested quantity. Customer-locked quantity must be protected for the original customer. Opportunistic replenishment should be suggested from safety stock, target stock, recent sales, current scheduling, and matching production conditions such as size, color, and handle type, then confirmed or edited by office staff before entering the pending replenishment scheduling pool. If a customer order and stock replenishment can share the same production run, the system should recommend merged continuous production, but customer due dates and locked customer quantities remain the priority. Later predictions should improve with accumulated cycle-sales and seasonal demand data.

When actual qualified production differs from the planned quantity in a merged customer-waiting / stock-replenishment run, the system should allocate customer-locked quantities first and put only remaining qualified goods into stock inventory. For non-custom production such as blank stock/common goods and printed stock/common goods, the planned quantity is a target rather than an exact stop quantity. The factory often prefers to finish the roll because stopping halfway wastes changeover time and half-roll leftovers are hard to store, but finishing the roll is not mandatory. The system should recommend whether to finish the roll from backend data such as current inventory, target inventory, recent sales, cycle-sales patterns, expected inventory turnover time, warehouse pressure, waiting customer orders, later machine plans, and roll-change / mold-change / handle-change cost. After bag-size material-usage formulas and per-roll meters are available, replenishment suggestions should use size-based material usage plus per-roll meters to estimate one-roll output, approximate roll count needed, and whether finishing the roll is reasonable. Early V1 can default to a rough one-roll estimate using `1500 meters per roll`, without aiming for precision. Replenishment suggestions and push messages should show the rough calculation basis, including current inventory, target inventory, shortage quantity, default per-roll meters, rough one-roll output, recommended rolls / approximate pieces, expected inventory after replenishment, and recommendation reason. The result should be marked as a rough estimate, and office / scheduling staff must be able to edit the suggested production quantity. Finish-the-roll recommendations should not depend on manual tags such as common item, slow-moving item, or cautious replenishment. When V1 has limited data, it should make conservative recommendations from existing orders, current inventory, configured minimum / target stock, and short-term sales data, and show manual confirmation recommended when evidence is insufficient. If extra quantity is likely to sit in stock for a long time, or if inventory pressure is high, the size/color is slow-moving, or a more urgent machine task is coming, the system should recommend producing only the target quantity or a small overage instead of finishing the roll. If customer-locked quantity is satisfied, lower-than-planned stock replenishment should record target-stock shortfall and remain in later warning / replenishment logic, while higher-than-planned stock replenishment should enter stock inventory with an over-planned inbound quantity record. Plan-vs-actual differences should record reasons such as finish the roll, roll length difference, avoid half-roll waste, planned quantity entry variance, reported quantity variance, or other. If actual qualified quantity cannot cover customer-locked quantity, the system should mark a customer-locked quantity shortfall and strongly remind office staff / management to choose continuing production, customer-accepted short shipment, or changed due date / continued waiting. This is expected to be rare but is required as a control to prevent customer-locked goods from being mixed into normal stock.

When a stock/common-goods order line enters waiting for outbound or becomes a formal order in fulfillment pending confirmation, V1 should reserve / lock the matching inventory immediately. Inventory should distinguish on-hand, reserved, and available quantities, with available = on-hand - reserved. Reservations are per order line and release or adjust when the order is cancelled, rejected, quantity-changed, moved to all-production, manually released, or completed through outbound. Parsed previews alone should not reserve inventory.

Pending-confirmation stock/common-goods orders that are not yet formal orders should not reserve inventory by default. Office staff can explicitly use hold inventory when the customer asks to hold goods or a short hold is needed. Holds must have an expiry time; V1 can default to same-day 19:30 automatic release. Hold records should store holder, hold time, expiry, reason, order line, and reserved quantity, and expired holds should release inventory automatically unless the order moved to waiting for outbound, became a formal fulfillment-pending order, or the hold was extended.

If outbound staff finds that actual inventory is insufficient for a reserved stock/common-goods order, V1 must not silently mark outbound as completed. It should enter inventory variance handling, showing reserved quantity, actually found quantity, variance, inventory item, warehouse/location, and operator context. Handling options should include actual outbound plus shortage to production, actual outbound with customer-accepted short shipment, cancel this outbound and wait until replenished, or correct inventory quantity / inventory count adjustment. Variance records should link to the order line and inventory ledger for later inventory accuracy statistics. Normal outbound staff can initiate correct inventory quantity, but cannot directly change system inventory. The system should create inventory adjustment pending confirmation, and only office staff or accounts with inventory adjustment confirmation permission can confirm it. Confirmation records should store initiator, confirmer, time, before/after quantity, variance, reason, linked order line, and notes. Inventory adjustment reasons should use fixed options plus optional notes; V1 fixed options should include wrong location searched, inventory count shortage, inventory count overage, outbound not recorded, inbound / workshop report quantity inaccurate, damaged / polluted / unsellable, workshop-reported inventory variance, historical inventory inaccurate, and other. Inventory adjustment pending confirmation belongs in the approval queue. Small quantity / low amount adjustments should enter the list without immediate push; large quantity or high estimated amount adjustments should immediately push to accounts with inventory adjustment confirmation permission. Push thresholds should be configurable by quantity variance and estimated amount. All inventory adjustments must retain records for later variance statistics by size, color, location, worker, and inventory source. Inventory adjustment only corrects inventory book quantity and must not directly advance the customer order status; order progress is determined by the selected variance handling result, such as actual outbound, shortage production, customer-accepted short shipment, or waiting until replenished. For actual outbound plus shortage to production, the internal order should show original ordered quantity, delivered quantity, pending delivery quantity, and shortage production / replenishment status; only actually delivered quantities should enter customer reconciliation by delivery date, and undelivered shortage must not be billed early. For customer-accepted short shipment, the order must keep original ordered quantity and actual outbound / shipped quantity separately. Order status should indicate short shipment confirmed rather than plain completed. Outbound, delivery, inventory deduction, and reconciliation quantities should use actual delivered quantity, while notes preserve the original ordered quantity and customer confirmation. Customer-accepted short shipment requires a lightweight confirmation record with confirmation method and content; screenshot/evidence photo and notes are optional, and the system records entry operator and time. If the goods were merely in another location or the adjustment is invalid, office staff can reject the pending adjustment and choose another handling path.

## 2026-06-25 - Inventory Warning Uses Hybrid Safety Stock And Demand Signals

Inventory warnings should combine manually configured minimum/target stock per size + color with dynamic suggestions from recent 7/15/30 day sales. V1 should generate replenishment suggestions for office confirmation rather than automatically creating production work orders. Replenishment reminders should be tiered: order-entry shortages appear first as desktop in-page reminders because office clerks enter orders on computers; a fixed daily replenishment summary can push to office / scheduling staff around 4 PM; and severe shortages or risks to already-promised customer due dates should immediately strong-remind relevant permission-based recipients. Normal replenishment suggestions can be confirmed collaboratively by office clerks and the production supervisor because they work together and can communicate offline easily; the system should not turn normal replenishment into a heavy approval workflow. Clearly large replenishment quantities, long estimated turnover, long-lived overstock risk, or suggestions that may consume machine time needed for promised customer orders should enter boss review / management watch as a non-blocking reminder. Recipients should be configured by account permissions, not hard-coded person names. Customer purchase history and seasonal cycles should later improve demand forecasting and reduce inventory pressure.

## 2026-06-25 - Confirmed Replenishment Enters A Scheduling Pool

Confirmed stock replenishment suggestions should enter a pending replenishment scheduling pool, not immediately become fixed production work orders. Confirming replenishment and formally scheduling a machine are separate steps: confirmation only accepts the replenishment need into the pool, while formal scheduling happens when the production supervisor or another account with scheduling permission assigns the task to a specific bag-making machine, date, and sequence. If the machine is not yet known, the task should remain in the pool. After a replenishment task is formally scheduled, the production supervisor or office staff may still adjust sequence for urgent customer orders or shop-floor conditions. Normal replenishment defaults below already-promised customer orders, but changes must keep audit records with operator, time, original/new machine and sequence, and reason. If the task includes customer-locked quantity, the system should check whether moving it back risks the promised customer due date, strongly remind staff, and require a confirmation reason. The scheduler should match replenishment tasks with customer orders by size, color, handle/strap setup, and timing so stock replenishment can run continuously after compatible customer orders and reduce changeovers, waste, and setup time.

## 2026-06-25 - Do Not Require Product Use Category For Order Entry

Bag handle material, width, and gram weight are fixed. The only handle variation is normal/default length versus extended handle. Customers generally do not state product use/category when ordering, so V1 should not require a category such as advertising bag or clothing-store bag. Order entry should parse model/size, color, quantity, and handle type. If extended handle is not mentioned, default to normal/default handle; if extended handle is explicitly mentioned, use extended handle. Handle type affects price and batching/continuous-production matching.

## 2026-06-25 - Model And Size Are The Same For Current Orders

For current business, customer-spoken model and size are the same concept, but some common names are aliases rather than physical measurements. `30*38`, `30*38*10`, `30*37`, and `30*37*10` refer to the same common spec group. Actual size format is `width*height*gusset`; the actual production, inventory, and costing standard is `30*37*10`, meaning width 30cm, height 37cm, and bottom/side gusset 10cm. Customer-facing UI and historical documents can preserve the `30*38` wording, but normalized spec, inventory matching, scheduling, material usage, and costing should use `30*37*10`. Current business does not include no-gusset bags.

## 2026-06-25 - Size Parsing Needs Alias Mapping

Customers express sizes inconsistently: full specs, shorthand specs, horizontal/vertical wording, or labels such as small, medium, large, and extra large. The order parser should use a size/spec alias table. V1 can use global aliases, then add customer-specific aliases when different customers use the same label for different actual sizes. Ambiguous parses should require office confirmation.

## 2026-06-27 - Similar Color Or Size Is Only A Hint, Not Inventory Availability

Stock/common-goods inventory matching must be exact on size/model, factory standard color, handle type, and finished-goods style before the system treats an item as in stock. Color aliases map different wordings to the same standard color, while size aliases parse customer wording; neither should be treated as automatic substitution. V1 may show possibly similar inventory as a reference for office staff, but similar color/size hints must not count as available inventory, reserve stock, move an order line to waiting for outbound, or generate customer copy promising in-stock fulfillment. Because most customers place orders after receiving e-commerce orders from their own buyers, color and size usually cannot be changed. Only explicit customer/office confirmation to change color, size, or style should close the original unavailable line and create a new line that reruns inventory and pricing. V1 does not distinguish color differences between batches under the same factory standard color; batches and inbound times should be retained for traceability, FIFO reference, stocktaking, and exception investigation, but not used to block same-standard-color stock matching or require same-batch outbound.

Finished-goods inventory should support practical warehouse zones. Plain stock/common goods are generally stored together and arranged by model/size, while printed stock/common goods are stored separately. V1 location data should stay lightweight and include zone/area type, model/size group, color or finished-goods style, and notes; it should not require shelf number, layer number, or bin/cell number. Example locations can be plain stock area / 30*38 / near red, printed stock area / bear / 25*23, or waiting-pickup area / customer tail number. Shelf/layer/bin or QR-coded location labels can be added later after warehouse organization stabilizes. FIFO can be used as the system's recommended deduction order when multiple batches/locations exist, but it must not be forced; outbound completion records the actual location, quantity, operator, and time, and actual outbound records override the recommendation without being treated as an exception.

## 2026-06-25 - Price And Size Table Captured

The earlier price/size table image was saved as `docs/product/assets/price-size-table-2026-04-19.png` and extracted into `docs/product/requirements.zh-CN.md`; it has now been superseded for new pricing by the 2026-07-03 price image. Vertical size classes start with `25*32`, `30*38 / 30*37`, and `35*41`; horizontal size classes start with `35*27`, `40*30 / 40*32`, `45*37`, and `50*40`, with `40*30` and `40*32` both treated as horizontal medium. Exact size is still retained for inventory, pricing, and production.

## 2026-06-25 - Printed Stock Styles Are Separate From Custom Print Content

`小熊`, `喜`, and `福` are printed stock/common-goods bag styles, while `覆膜` is another product category that is currently rarely produced. These styles use normal/default handle length and should be recognized as finished-goods styles or process types rather than ordinary colors/sizes or custom print content.

## 2026-06-25 - Stock Finished Goods Inventory Dimensions

Stock/common-goods finished inventory should be separated by size, color, handle type, finished-goods style, and warehouse/location. Finished-goods style includes at least plain/blank, `小熊`, `喜`, `福`, and `覆膜`. Finished-goods style must also validate allowed size ranges; for example, 小熊 has no `30*38` size, so the system must not generate `30*38 小熊袋`.

## 2026-06-25 - Finished Goods Inventory Unit Is Pieces

Finished-goods inventory uses pieces as the primary unit. The Chinese UI can display either `个` or `只`, but internal storage should normalize to `个`. Packages, bundles, and boxes belong to packing/outbound details, not the primary inventory unit.

## 2026-06-25 - Finished Goods Inbound Counting Is Scenario-Based

Printed custom orders and printed stock styles usually go through packing, so packing workers can count and confirm final quantities. Blank stock bags often remain loose and should not require mandatory packing or outbound-staff counting before inventory. V1 should allow bag-making workers to report completion quantity into workshop-reported/loose stock inventory, with later outbound, spot count, or inventory count adjustments when differences are found. Exception cases can require review.

## 2026-06-25 - Support Machine-Side Direct Outbound

Blank stock bags may be dispatched directly from the bag-making machine, especially in busy seasons when customers or pickup staff wait beside the machine. The target process is to train customers to place orders in WeChat / Enterprise WeChat groups in advance, with office staff entering the order and warehouse outbound staff preparing goods, performing outbound, and confirming actual taken quantity. Old customers directly entering the bag-making workshop and later reporting quantity should remain a transition-period exception, not the main flow. The system should support machine-side direct outbound by recording production completion and outbound/fulfillment in one linked flow, with machine, operator, confirmer, quantity, fulfillment method, and inventory movement source retained. Machine-side direct outbound must link to a customer and formal order/order line; if no ERP order exists yet, office staff should create a quick stock/common-goods order before completing the outbound. Warehouse outbound staff are the default priority confirmer; if they are not on site, production supervisor or office staff can proxy-enter the confirmation as pending warehouse review. Bag-making workers report production quantity only and should not be the default final confirmer of how many pieces the customer actually took. Customer self-reported quantity is a reference only and should not directly deduct inventory until warehouse / office confirms it. The flow may use electronic confirmation first when the site is busy, with office staff later back-printing or linking the paper outbound/pickup document. The shortest busy-season flow is select customer/order, enter taken quantity, choose machine-side pickup / machine-side direct dispatch, and complete; notes, paper documents, and review can be backfilled. A same-day machine-side direct outbound pending-review list should prevent missed records; unreviewed items after the day ends get a red-dot reminder, and items older than one day enter follow-up. Confirmed machine-side direct outbound should immediately offset that day's workshop-reported / machine-side output. For cross-day tasks, machine-side direct outbound may happen before final report completed quantity; the dispatched quantity should link to that day's output record and the corresponding order / customer, while remaining unshipped quantity stays as task progress and does not become normal available inventory. When the task is finally completed, quantities already dispatched machine-side must be deducted before moving remaining qualified goods to packing, customer-locked allocation, or stock inventory, avoiding duplicate inbound or delivery. Variances are handled through inventory adjustment / cycle-count correction. If a variance can be linked to an order, machine, operator, or outbound confirmer, the system may create a responsibility clue, but it must never auto-deduct pay.

## 2026-06-25 - Workshop-Reported Loose Inventory Is A Labeled State

For blank stock bags, `workshop-reported inventory` and `loose stock inventory` are not conflicting buckets. They describe source/confidence and packaging state: workshop-reported, loose, and unreviewed. This inventory can be shown as available, but must be labeled separately from counted warehouse inventory. Outbound, spot count, or inventory count can later confirm or adjust it.

## 2026-06-25 - Bag-Making Reporting Uses Piece Count With Bundle Support

Bag-making workers receive assigned tasks on the workshop mobile side, submit completion reporting, and then continue to the next task. The mobile task list should distinguish customer orders, replenishment, replenishment with customer-locked quantity, and custom printed orders while keeping the list compact. The list should show scan-friendly fields such as task type, size, color, handle type, planned / target quantity, machine, sequence, rush flag, and latest completion / shipping time. Customer orders and custom printed orders show the customer name; pure stock replenishment does not; replenishment with customer-locked quantity shows the locked customer and locked quantity. Custom printed orders may show only a summary in the list, with full customer, order line, print content, notes, photo requirements, exception records, and process quantities available after opening the task. Task-detail actions vary by type: customer / custom printed orders support start bag-making, upload finished-goods photo, report completed quantity, and report exception; pure stock replenishment supports start production, report completed quantity, and report exception without requiring a finished-goods photo by default; replenishment with customer-locked quantity follows stock replenishment actions, then allocates customer-locked quantity first after completion reporting. V1 should not require workers to maintain frequent pause/resume actions for normal rest, roll changes, or machine repair continuation. If a task is not finished by the end of the workday, the worker can mark unfinished today / continue tomorrow and must record that day's qualified quantity; the same task continues the next day, with planned quantity, cumulative completed quantity, and estimated remaining quantity automatically shown on mobile. Cross-day task quantities should be split by day for more accurate worker daily output and machine daily capacity statistics, but daily quantities should not increase available inventory before final completion. Current-day quantity can be edited by the same worker before the task enters the next day or final completion; historical daily quantities can only be edited by the production supervisor or office staff, with before/after quantity, reason, operator, and time recorded. Final completion should default to summing daily quantities, with one final worker / supervisor correction allowed when a correction reason is recorded; inventory, customer-locked allocation, packing, and reconciliation should recognize only final report completed quantity or final confirmed qualified quantity. Machine breakdown, repair waiting, printing problems, and similar exceptions use report exception or supervisor / office backend records instead of mandatory detailed downtime tracking. The primary submitted and saved quantity should be piece count because partial bundles and tail quantities are common. One bundle usually equals 50 bags, so the UI can support bundle count plus loose pieces as an auxiliary input and convert that to pieces. The reporting form should use prefill + editable from the planned/order quantity. Workers can change the prefilled value before submitting the actual completed piece count. The system should record the final piece count and retain the prefilled value, submitted value, modified flag, quantity source, and modification reason/note. To reduce lazy default confirmation, reports also retain prefill source and unchanged-default submission state. Later packing, outbound, spot count, and inventory-count differences should feed worker/machine reporting quality metrics and exception sampling.

## 2026-06-26 - Printed Orders Use Photos At Silk-Screen And Bag-Making, Final Quantity From Packing

Printed orders should collect evidence across production stages. After silk-screen setup is adjusted, the silk-screen workshop takes a print photo to record print content, color, position, and effect. After silk-screen completion, the silk-screen workshop submits actual printed quantity and the order flows to bag-making. After bag-making setup is adjusted and a qualified finished sample bag appears, the bag-making worker takes a finished-goods photo. For the bag-making finished-goods photo, photographer, photo time, machine, and order line number should be system-recorded automatically, not manually entered by the worker. For printed orders, the bag-making finished-goods photo must be uploaded after start bag-making and before customer shipment-preparation notification. After the finished-goods photo is uploaded, office staff can notify the customer to prepare shipment / arrange express or less-than-truckload, even if the full order is not fully produced or packed yet. For printed orders that require packing, after bag-making uploads the finished-goods photo, the order can enter `packing can start` and appear on helper / packing workers' mobile side. Packing workers can start packing while bag-making is still producing the rest of the order. `Packing can start` does not mean final quantity has been counted. Before clicking `packing completed`, bag-making must have finished or the current deliverable production batch must be confirmed complete, and packing workers must enter complete package details and final actual bag quantity. After a bag-making work order is assigned to a machine, only bag-making workers assigned to that machine in the current schedule / shift can see it in the workshop mobile side. When the worker opens the work order / order, operator is automatically filled from the current logged-in account and cannot be manually edited. The factory often has a time gap between all bags being produced and packing/counting being completed, so statuses should distinguish finished-goods photo available / customer can be notified to prepare shipment, bag-making completed, packing can start, and packing completed / quantity counted. For printed orders that require packing, package-detail total quantity is the main source of final deliverable quantity and statement delivered quantity. Bag-making reported quantity should not be the only final quantity source for printed orders, while blank stock/common-goods or loose inventory can still use bag-making reported quantity as workshop-reported / loose inventory.

## 2026-06-26 - Packing Supports Batches And Late Customer Repack Requests

One order line can have multiple packing batches, so packing can start while production is still running. Final package count and delivered quantity should sum all packing batches. Default packing plans should prioritize explicit order/customer notes, then customer-profile packing preference or historical packing experience, then system defaults such as 1000 pieces per package. Customer profiles can store default packing preferences such as usual pieces per package, weighing requirement, transfer-warehouse / ocean-shipping package weight limit, and common notes. V1 should only support customer-level default packing preference, not customer + size/category packing preferences; size- or order-specific special packing should stay as current-order packing requirement / order note first. If later data shows repeated different packing rules by size/category for the same customer, extend the model then. After customer shipment-preparation notification, customers may rarely call to change packing requirements, such as from 1000 pieces per package to 500 pieces per package. If packing has not started, update the pending packing plan. If goods are already packed but not delivered/picked up, allow a repack / change-packing operation retaining customer request, original package details, new package details, operator, timestamp, and notes. A temporary packing change affects only the current order by default. Office staff may check `save as customer default packing preference`; only then should the customer profile be updated. If not checked, one special request must not pollute the customer's long-term default packing rule. If goods are already delivered or picked up by carrier, record the late request and why packing cannot be changed; do not silently edit historical package details.

## 2026-06-26 - Printed Quantity Tolerance Tracks Silk-Screen Overprint And Process Loss

For custom printed orders, quantity abnormality is often visible at the silk-screen stage. The system should record ordered quantity, actual silk-screen printed quantity, and final packed / delivered quantity, then calculate silk-screen overprint, process loss from silk-screen to packing, and final delivery variance. Early V1 should focus on collecting data and comparing machines/workers rather than using thresholds to punish workers or force approvals too early. The system should collect comparable data for the same spec, print type, and order quantity, including material input, actual printed quantity, final packed quantity, process loss, and final over/under-delivery rate. This data should later help establish production benchmarks: for the same 1000-piece order, how much material another machine usually needs, how many pieces it prints, and how many qualified finished goods it produces while satisfying customer quantity without excessive overrun. Fixed thresholds such as extra 50 or extra 100 pieces should not be hard-coded because they can be too large proportionally, such as 50 extra on a 1000-piece order. V1 can keep configurable percentage plus absolute cap thresholds, but early overprint / loss / over-delivery should be treated mainly as observation data or non-blocking risk hints. Example: for a 1000-piece order, silk-screen printing 1015 or 1020 and final packed quantity of 1005 is usually reasonable. Final packed / delivered quantity below order or above reasonable over-delivery range should still enter quantity exception confirmation because it affects customer delivery and billing.

## 2026-06-29 - Routine Bag-Making Waste Is Scrap-Sale Reference, Not Formal Scrap Flow

V1 should not introduce a formal scrap approval workflow for ordinary bag-making waste because the factory currently has no dedicated scrap handler or mature scrap procedure. Routine bag-making waste is usually bagged and sold as scrap directly. Normal quantity reporting should recognize only qualified finished goods, and workers should not be forced to enter scrap/loss data for normal tasks. V1 may optionally record lightweight scrap-bag / waste-batch records with date, optional workshop or machine, estimated weight or bag count, handler, scrap-sale amount, optional buyer, and notes. These records do not enter customer order revenue, customer statements, available finished-goods inventory, or payroll deduction. Only problem goods that affect customer orders, inventory, amount handling, quality complaints, or responsibility judgment should enter pending handling / pending scrap or abnormal loss flows. Responsibility clues should be generated only for customer, amount, inventory, or performance-relevant issues, with responsible person fillable later and no automatic pay deduction. Daily machine-counter readings stay optional telemetry until the machine reset/daily-count rule is verified on site.

## 2026-06-26 - Raw Material Usage Will Feed Future Gross Profit

The system should calculate material cost and bag gross profit from material-usage formulas per bag size. The first bag body / handle formula has now been captured, while exact roll length and any special-model / new-process constants still need field calibration. V1 cost estimates should be marked as excluding loss, and loss rate should be added later only after calibration from production reporting, leftovers, abnormal loss records, and stocktaking. Until exact roll length and complete actual material data are available, raw material can default to a rough `1500 meters per roll` estimate, and early replenishment suggestions can roughly estimate by one roll, such as one-roll output, whether the shortage is close to one roll, and whether arranging a full roll is worthwhile. This is not precise and should not be treated as final financial cost settlement. Future gross-profit estimation should combine order revenue from the order price snapshot with estimated or actual material cost from size-based usage, roll-length estimate, material unit cost, and actual or estimated material usage. Fabric and handle raw-material purchasing is confirmed to be primarily priced by kg/weight, with raw-material supplier delivery notes recording supplier, document date/number, goods name/color, specification model such as width/GSM/meters, piece/roll count, total kg, yuan/kg unit price, line amount, and per-piece/per-roll weights. These are incoming raw-material supplier documents, not finished-goods outbound / customer delivery notes. Normal/default body GSM is 78g; normal/default handle length is 38cm, handle raw-material width is 5cm, handle GSM is 60g, and each bag has 2 handles unless a special order or actual batch overrides those defaults. The factory currently has 3 fabric suppliers with different prices, and handle prices also vary by supplier, so body fabric supplier/batch and handle supplier/batch must be stored separately in cost snapshots. Main fabric widths are usually two-digit centimeter widths, so specification parsing and labels must distinguish handle material from main fabric material. Raw-material purchase cost excludes freight; freight should be tracked separately if needed, not allocated into the raw-material cost snapshot. Raw-material labels should not show purchase price, amount, or supplier arrears by default; cost data belongs in permission-controlled backend views. Ink and other auxiliary materials stay lightweight in V1 without automatic deduction or order-level cost allocation; if they later need detailed management, the system should support multiple pricing bases rather than hard-coding kg pricing for all materials.

## 2026-06-27 - QR And Barcode Labels Start With Goods Cards And Raw Materials

QR/barcode labels should start with practical batch-level objects instead of individual bags. The first outbound use case is goods cards for prepared goods, waiting pickup / express areas, packages, pallets, baskets, or temporary preparation areas. V1/V1.5 should use two separate templates: an outbound / prepared-goods card template and a raw-material label template. The outbound goods-card template should show customer, order/outbound-note identity, fulfillment method, product summary, package/piece count, customer notes, office notes, and QR/barcode. The raw-material label template should show material type, color/specification, supplier, inbound date, roll/batch number, expected meters/weight, warehouse/location, notes, and QR/barcode, but not purchase price or amount by default. Scanning should open or validate the outbound task / package and warn if it does not match the current customer or document, but V1 should not make scanning mandatory for every outbound action. Labels must include human-readable fields so the factory can continue working if a scanner, phone, or label is unavailable. The encoded QR/barcode value should preferably be only an internal system ID, such as preparation task ID, outbound note number, package ID, or raw-material batch / roll number, not a full copy of mutable business details. QR/barcode goods-card and raw-material label printing should follow document-printing permissions in V1: office staff or accounts with print permission print/reprint labels, while warehouse/outbound staff attach labels, scan, check, and use them. Warehouse label-printer permission can be opened later after the process is stable. Raw-material arrival should be handled by office staff or a designated person checking the raw-material supplier delivery note against physical material fields such as meters, GSM/fabric weight, total kg, color, width, supplier, and roll/batch count. Photo recognition/OCR can prefill inbound drafts and cost snapshots, but it cannot directly create usable inventory; an authorized human must verify and confirm before official raw-material batch/roll numbers and labels are generated. After labels are printed and attached, scanning confirms labeled inbound and records operator, time, and location. If the raw-material supplier delivery note, OCR result, and physical material disagree, the material enters inbound exception / pending confirmation instead of available inventory. For raw materials, fabric rolls should preferably use one label per roll; handles should also be labeled by roll/piece when they arrive by roll or piece; ink and auxiliary materials can be labeled by barrel, box, package, or batch. Raw-material inventory needs its own ledger: inbound, material issue/outbound to production, return/leftover registration, and stock adjustment. Material issue/outbound is not customer shipment; it records which raw-material roll/batch goes to which machine, production task, or purpose, plus receiver, issuer, time, quantity/full-roll or partial issue, and notes. Scanning a raw-material label alone must not automatically deduct inventory; scanner/phone use must be in material issue/outbound mode, or a production task, material issue document, or machine issue page must be selected first. The recommended flow is scanning/selecting the production task or machine/purpose first, then scanning the raw-material roll/batch, validating matching fields, defaulting to full-roll issue where appropriate, and confirming. Leftover registration should record remaining meters/weight, location, status, and reusable specification. Raw-material inbound, issue/outbound, and leftover records should later link to production tasks for material-consumption and gross-profit data. V1.5/V2 can support both 2D scanners and mobile scanning, with scanner input treated as keyboard-entered internal IDs where possible.

## 2026-06-27 - Raw Material Colors Need Supplier Alias Mapping

Raw-material delivery-note color names may differ from the factory's standard color names, and customer color names can also differ from factory and supplier names. The system must store both supplier original color name and factory standard color for inbound materials. Inventory, material preparation, scheduling matching, and replenishment should use the factory standard color, while the supplier original color remains available for traceability, supplier reconciliation, and label context. Color alias mapping should be source-scoped, not text-only: source type (factory internal, customer, customer group, supplier, or global), source object ID, material/product type, original color name, and factory standard color. The same original color wording can map differently by customer or supplier, so automatic recognition should resolve aliases by current context, prioritizing customer/customer-group aliases, then supplier aliases, then factory/global aliases. OCR and customer-group recognition can suggest mappings from history, but new, ambiguous, or low-confidence mappings require authorized manual confirmation before the material becomes available for automatic material-preparation matching or before the customer order becomes formal. V1 should build the color catalog gradually through manual-confirmation-based learning: the system may prompt staff to save a newly confirmed color wording as an alias during order review, customer-group recognition, or raw-material inbound, but it must not automatically write a one-time recognition result into formal alias rules. Regular office staff can confirm the standard color for the current order or inbound document and create pending / permitted aliases, but editing existing aliases, merging standard colors, deactivating colors, changing standard color names, or changing applicable material types requires management permission. Every alias create/update and standard-color management action should keep source scenario or source document/order, before/after values, impact prompt, operator, confirmer, timestamp, and notes/reason. Historical orders, inventory, and raw-material records should retain the color value used at the time; reporting can aggregate through later merge relationships rather than overwriting history. Raw-material labels and issue details should show standard color first and supplier original color second, such as red (supplier: deep red).

The factory should maintain a unified standard color catalog / color card as lightweight master data. V1 fields should include standard color name, display color swatch, applicable material types, common aliases, inactive flag, and notes. The display swatch is only a UI aid, not a strict quality/color-matching standard. The same standard color catalog should support customer order recognition, office order entry, inventory, material preparation, scheduling, customer statistics, and source-scoped color alias mapping. Inactive colors should be hidden from normal preferred selection but remain available for historical order, inventory, and raw-material lookup.

## 2026-06-27 - Raw Material Issue Must Bind Machine; Task Allocation Can Be Estimated In V1

Raw-material issue/outbound must bind at least to a machine. It should bind to a production task / work order / replenishment task / order line whenever practical, but V1 must not force helpers or bag-making workers to scan-switch material for every small fragmented order. The factory often stages 1-3 rolls beside a machine so production can keep moving, and rush insertions or small orders may be interleaved with replenishment. V1 should not require people on site to judge separate states such as delivered to machine side versus mounted / started using. Instead, use one helper action, machine-side material pickup, which moves the roll/batch from warehouse-available raw material to machine-side material / machine-held material. This is not final consumption or final order cost. Leftover registration should also be trigger-based, not mandatory for every small remainder: record it when material returns to storage, moves to another machine, is checked at end-of-day/stage stocktaking, has a clearly reusable large remainder, or office/supervisor asks for it. If machine-side leftover remains beside the same machine across days, V1 should keep it assigned to that machine, show it as yesterday's machine-side leftover on the next day's task view, and create inventory ledger entries only if staff return it, move it to another machine, or stocktake-adjust it. Rough leftover quantities can be marked as estimated and corrected later. After production reporting, end-of-roll, leftover return, loss/exception records, or inventory adjustment, the system should estimate actual consumption and machine material efficiency. Order/task gross margin can initially be estimated from machine-bound material usage, actual qualified output, size-based material formulas, and task/order output allocation; these estimates must be marked as estimated and not treated as final financial cost. Optional task-segmented material usage can be added later if the shop-floor process can support it without slowing production.

## 2026-06-27 - Helper Material Preparation Is Grouped By Workshop

Because the factory currently has 9 bag-making machines across 3 workshops and 3 helpers, with each helper usually covering one workshop / around 3 machines, helper work should be organized by workshop/zone rather than by individual machine only. The system should support configurable workshop-zone responsibility mapping, initialized in V1 as machines 1-3, 4-6, and 7-9 but not hard-coded. A helper account sees the material-preparation and packing task pools for their current workshop/zone, while office, production supervisor, and management accounts can view all workshops. Temporary support for another workshop should be authorized by a permitted account and audited. After bag-making tasks are published, the system should automatically generate today's / tomorrow's material-preparation pool by workshop, machine, time window such as morning/afternoon/current batch, and sequence, showing raw-material color, width, type, estimated rolls/meters/weight, planned start or latest-ready time, linked production tasks/orders, and whether a specific roll/batch is reserved. Draft schedules should not push helper tasks. Early V1 may use rough one-roll estimates and manually maintained material-width rules, but must mark rough hints as pending confirmation. The captured bag-size material formula should feed material preparation, replenishment suggestions, material-cost estimates, and order/machine gross-margin estimates after entry. Even with formulas, office staff or the production supervisor must be able to edit preparation quantities because actual roll length, leftovers, finish-the-roll decisions, and schedule changes can affect real material needs. Material preparation can recommend or reserve a roll for a workshop/machine or near-term task group, but it should not force every task to lock a specific roll before pickup; the helper's actual scanned roll becomes the authoritative machine-side pickup record. Inventory movement into machine-side material happens only after helper-confirmed machine-side material pickup. V1 should allow helpers to stage 1-3 rolls at the machine side for small orders, custom printed orders scheduled for that machine, and near-term tasks; delivering those materials early is acceptable when the machine is going to consume them in that time window. Rush insertions should usually be handled by changing production order rather than by a complex material transfer flow. V1 should not require the bag-making worker to scan-confirm the material after the helper delivers it, because machine operators already have heavy work and helpers often mount the roll together with them. The helper should confirm a single machine-side material pickup action on mobile by selecting the machine or machine task group and scanning the raw-material roll/batch; the bag-making worker task detail can show the linked material read-only, with exceptions reported only when a mismatch is noticed. Schedule changes and rush insertions must update helper preparation tasks, but should not create heavy mandatory transfer operations in early V1. If material has already been delivered to machine side when the schedule changes, it enters machine-side pending handling; office or production supervisor decides whether to keep it, move it, return it, or leave it waiting. A machine-side roll that remains for more than one day without a linked current or next-day task should enter a review reminder only, with no automatic return and no automatic responsibility deduction.

## 2026-06-25 - Daily Machine Counter Snapshot Is Production Telemetry, Not Inventory

Bag-making machine counters cannot reliably split quantity by individual order, so V1 should not require per-order counter photos or use machine counter values as per-order reporting evidence. Instead, at end of day each bag-making machine can have one machine action / cycle counter snapshot photo, machine daily-report value, cumulative-reading difference, or manually entered counter value. This daily machine cycle count is not finished-goods quantity and must not directly create inventory, fulfillment quantity, chargeable quantity, payroll piece count, or deductions. It should be compared with qualified bag-making reports, packing counts, inbound quantities, and abnormal loss records to estimate process variance/loss and detect anomalies.

Counter behavior still requires on-site verification: machines may expose both lifetime cumulative count and daily count, and they may or may not be reset daily. The implementation should record counter reading type and avoid hard-coding a reset assumption.

## 2026-06-25 - No Formal Scrap Procedure In V1

The factory does not currently have a mature scrap procedure, so V1 should not introduce formal scrap documents, approval, or cost accounting. V1 should capture lightweight abnormal loss records only when exceptions occur. Defects discovered during bag-making may originate from upstream silk-screen printing, so abnormal loss records should separate discovery step from responsible/source step. Daily machine-cycle counter differences should be treated as process variance, not automatic scrap quantity, because they may include setup/testing, semi-finished goods, tail quantities, counter error, repeated actions, or delayed reporting.

## 2026-06-25 - Fixed Abnormal Loss Options And Supervisor Confirmation

V1 abnormal loss reasons should use fixed mobile-friendly options rather than free-form text first. When bag-making workers discover printing problems, the default flow is report exception and pause for production supervisor confirmation, matching the current shop-floor habit of stopping the machine and asking the supervisor before continuing. V1 allows the bag-making worker to proxy-record `supervisor confirmed` with supervisor name, proxy recorder, and confirmation timestamp; later versions can add supervisor self-confirmation. The system should not automatically return orders to silk-screen; continue, partial completion, silk-screen review, or office handoff should be confirmed by the supervisor or office.

## 2026-06-25 - Inventory And Fulfillment Use Qualified Finished Goods Only

Bag-making reported pieces, inbound inventory, available inventory, and fulfillment quantities should count qualified finished goods only. Defective bags, printing-problem bags, and unresolved problem material belong to abnormal loss records or process variance reporting, not available inventory or deliverable quantity.

## 2026-06-25 - Abnormal Quantity Requires Office And Customer Confirmation

Normal in-tolerance workshop reports can auto-flow, but short quantity and excessive overrun should not automatically complete or move to fulfillment. Management contacts the customer by phone/WeCom/WeChat, while office clerks can proxy-enter the confirmation because the current management contacts, the owner's mother and sister-in-law, may not use computers. Most short-quantity cases are handled through price reduction / receivable adjustment rather than a new production run. When supplemental printing is needed, it usually uses blank bags issued from finished-goods inventory and manual small-plate printing; those bags must exactly match the original order's size, color, and handle type, and the system should record stock outbound for supplemental printing linked to the original order. If exact matching blank stock does not exist, or the order is a special model or large-area full-coverage print, supplemental stock printing should be considered unavailable by default. Only rare cases become manually created abnormal remake tasks; the system should not auto-generate normal production orders or insert them into the normal scheduling queue. For excessive overrun, record whether the customer accepts extra pieces, pays extra, refuses extra payment, receives free extras, or receives only the ordered quantity.

## 2026-06-25 - Abnormal Discounts Preserve Original Price Snapshot

When abnormal quantity or quality issues lead to price reduction / receivable adjustment, the system should preserve the original order amount and price snapshot. It should add a separate abnormal discount/allowance record linked to the original order and customer confirmation, so statements can show original receivable, discount/allowance, extra payment if any, and final receivable. Customer-facing statements should use short neutral adjustment reasons such as quantity difference adjustment, while internal records retain detailed abnormal reasons, process attribution, management confirmation, and office entry. V1 should not require mandatory approval, but abnormal discounts/allowances exceeding configurable amount or percentage thresholds should enter a non-blocking boss review queue. V1 defaults to amount over 50 CNY or percentage over 5%, with either condition triggering review. This queue is a reminder, not an approval gate, and should not change order state or block production, fulfillment, statement generation, or payment recording.

## 2026-06-26 - Customer Complaints Need Lightweight Records And Responsibility Tracing

V1 should support lightweight after-sales / customer complaint records for cases such as short shipment, wrong goods, print problems, broken packages, and quantity disputes. Records should link to customer, original order, order line, delivery / outbound / carrier record, customer screenshots or photos, internal verification evidence, handling result, amount impact, entry operator, and time. Complaint handling may affect customer statement amount through an after-sales adjustment amount. Local stock/common-goods short shipment usually requires supplemental shipment linked to the original order / delivery note, usually without direct price reduction. Print problems usually require compensation or discount / allowance that affects final receivable. Wrong goods for local customers can often be resolved by exchange without amount impact. Self-operated online-store cases may require compensation or exchange, and the system should record which outcome applies. If verification confirms factory responsibility, the record must include responsibility tracing: responsible step, responsible account / operator, machine if applicable, linked reporting / packing / outbound / delivery record, verifier, verification time, and internal notes. Examples: wrong goods may trace to packing, outbound, or delivery check; print problems may trace to print photo, silk-screen operator, and silk-screen machine; short shipment may trace to package details, outbound check, or carrier handoff. Because performance bonuses and penalty deductions exist, complaint records should retain whether performance / deduction is affected, deduction amount or result, confirmer, and confirmation time. V1 should not automatically deduct money. Office staff can proxy-enter customer feedback, verification evidence, suggested responsible person, and suggested responsible step, but final decision on whether to deduct, how much to deduct, or record only without deduction must be confirmed by the boss or a management account with performance / deduction confirmation permission. V1 records these results first and does not need automatic payroll calculation yet.

## 2026-06-26 - After-Sales Supplemental And Exchange Tasks Are Fulfillment, Not New Sales

After-sales supplemental shipment / exchange should create an after-sales fulfillment task linked to the original order, order line, original delivery / outbound / carrier record, and complaint record. It can reuse normal fulfillment capabilities such as inventory outbound, packing if needed, delivery / pickup / express handoff. Examples include delivering 50 missing local stock pieces, exchanging wrong black goods for red goods while recording whether black goods are returned, or sending self-operated online-store supplemental goods through express / less-than-truckload. These tasks must not count as new sales orders or duplicate revenue. Inventory movement source should be after-sales. Customer statements should show only after-sales adjustment amount or notes when applicable, not new sales revenue from the supplemental / exchange fulfillment.

## 2026-06-26 - Exchange Tasks Track Returned Wrong Goods

Exchange tasks should track returned wrong goods separately from the supplemental outbound. Required tracking includes expected return quantity, actual returned quantity, return status, and returned-goods handling method. Return status options include pending return, returned, customer will return next time, customer refuses return, unable to return, and no return required. Handling options include restock, downgraded restock, scrap, internal handling, and pending handling. If the driver delivers correct goods and brings wrong goods back, record actual returned quantity and handling result. If the customer promises to return later, keep a follow-up state. If goods were opened or used and cannot return, record unable to return with reason. Returned goods can be restocked only if they are saleable as stock/common goods; otherwise use downgraded restock, internal handling, scrap, or pending handling.

## 2026-06-26 - After-Sales Follow-Up Reminds But Does Not Block

V1 should include an after-sales follow-up list for unresolved items, such as wrong goods promised for later return but not returned after the agreed time or default 7 days, short shipment without confirmed handling, online-store compensation amount not confirmed, confirmed factory responsibility without performance / deduction confirmation, or after-sales supplemental / exchange fulfillment not completed. The follow-up list is a reminder for office / management and should not block customer order entry, normal production, or normal fulfillment by default.

## 2026-06-26 - After-Sales Statistics Support Customer And Internal Management

After-sales / complaint data should feed business statistics. Customer-level metrics should include complaint count, issue-type distribution, short-shipment count, wrong-goods count, print-problem count, supplemental-shipment count, exchange count, compensation / discount amount, and unresolved after-sales count. Internal responsibility metrics should include responsible step, responsible account / operator, responsible machine, responsibility count, performance / deduction count, deduction amount, and common issue types. These metrics support both customer management, such as identifying customers who frequently request compensation, and internal performance / process improvement, such as finding packing workers with frequent short shipments or silk-screen machines with many print problems.

## 2026-06-26 - Customer Risk Hints Do Not Block Order Entry

Order entry should show non-blocking customer risk hints, such as current debt amount, overdue debt, unresolved after-sales count, high complaint count in the last 30 days, or frequent recent temporary packing changes. These hints must not block order saving. Office staff can continue entering the order, while management decides manually whether to pause cooperation or handle the customer specially.

V1 should use two levels: normal hint and strong hint. Normal hints cover cases such as one unresolved after-sales item, recent temporary packing changes, or small non-overdue debt. Strong hints cover cases such as debt above threshold, overdue debt, high recent complaint count, or large after-sales / compensation amount. Strong hints should be highlighted on the order-entry screen and added to the boss-review list, but still must not block order saving.

## 2026-06-26 - Review Items And Approval Items Are Separate

V1 should separate non-blocking `review / watch` items from blocking `approval` items. Review / watch items are reminders, such as customer debt above threshold, overdue debt, strong customer risk hints, large rounding / write-off, large after-sales compensation or allowance, current-order manual price override above threshold, or abnormal discount / allowance above threshold. They should not block order entry, scheduling, production, outbound, delivery, statement generation, or payment recording by default.

Approval items require authorized confirmation before the related item can take effect, such as price-table version review before publishing / becoming effective, or performance / deduction confirmation.
