Approval should only block the related item itself. For example, an unapproved new price version cannot become effective, but existing-price orders can still continue through production and delivery. An unconfirmed performance / deduction result cannot enter payroll settlement, but after-sales supplemental shipment, exchange, or customer reconciliation can continue.

When multiple authorized accounts receive the same approval item, V1 should treat the first valid handling result as final and should not require multi-approver signoff. If one authorized person confirms, returns, or otherwise completes the item, other recipients should see who handled it, when, and with what result, and they should not be able to repeat the action. If two people submit almost simultaneously, only the first valid submission is accepted.

Approval items should be actively pushed to mobile accounts with the matching approval permission. Review / watch items should default to the list plus a daily summary, and only severe cases should immediately push, such as customer debt over 20,000 CNY, long-overdue debt, or very large after-sales compensation / allowance. These immediate-push thresholds should be configurable; debt over 20,000 CNY is an initial V1 example threshold.

V1 Enterprise WeChat integration should use self-built application app messages for internal notifications to specific employees. The system should bind ERP accounts to Enterprise WeChat `userid`, store `CorpID`, `AgentID`, and app `Secret`, obtain `access_token`, and send messages with a title, key context, and ERP handling link. Enterprise WeChat group robots can be optional for internal daily summaries, but should not be used for named approvals. SMS and automatic customer-group notifications are out of V1 scope.

Notification recipients should be matched by permission rather than hard-coded person names. Price-table approval pushes to accounts with price-table review permission; performance / deduction approval pushes to accounts with performance / deduction confirmation permission; severe debt / overdue debt and severe after-sales compensation review push to accounts with boss-review permission. Accounts can temporarily disable notifications or have permissions adjusted during leave, but handling actions must still validate the required permission.

Approval and review / watch items should have configurable reminder rules by item type. Price-table approval should remind once 30 minutes before the planned effective time if still unapproved; if the effective time arrives without approval, the new price version must not become effective and should remain marked as approval pending. Performance / deduction approval can use daily summary reminders and only strong-remind before payroll settlement. Severe debt / overdue debt review is not approval and should not block flow; it can remain in daily summary and remind again only after it remains unviewed for the configured number of days. Reminder logs should record time, recipient, trigger reason, delivery channel, and send result.

After a review / watch item is viewed, V1 should record a lightweight handling result without making it a formal approval workflow. Default handling results should include viewed with no action needed, customer contacted, continue follow-up, convert to approval, and pause cooperation / warn before order entry. The last option should keep showing strong warnings on future order entry but should still not automatically forbid order saving in V1.

V1 can reserve a stricter customer status for management confirmation required before order acceptance, but it should be off by default and rarely used. Only accounts with boss-review or customer risk handling permission can set or remove it. If enabled, office staff can save an order draft / pending-confirmation order, but it cannot move to accepted, scheduled, outbound, or delivered until authorized management confirms. The management confirmation screen only needs two actions: accept order and reject order. Accepting moves the order to accepted and resumes the normal order flow. Rejecting closes / cancels the order and prevents production, inventory reservation, outbound, and reconciliation. This confirmation is at the original-order level, not per split order line / sub-order. If the customer relationship is broken or management has clearly decided not to cooperate, management can directly reject the whole original order. The confirmation record should store confirmer, confirmation time, result, and reason / notes.

Normal office staff must not be able to remove pause-cooperation / warn-before-order-entry status. They can see the warning, add notes, or forward it to management. Only accounts with boss-review or customer risk handling permission can remove it, and removal must record remover, removal time, reason, and linked evidence / notes.

## 2026-06-25 - Statement Export Uses Excel First

V1 customer statement export should prioritize Excel because customers can verify rows, add notes, and return the file more easily. PDF export can be added later for fixed-layout, stamp/archive, or customer-specific requirements.

## 2026-06-25 - V1 Customer Statement Columns

V1 customer-facing Excel statements should use two sheets. `Statement summary` aggregates one row per order line / style / print content, not one merged row per original order, with order date, original order number, line number, product / print content, size, color, total delivered quantity, delivery date / batch summary, fulfillment method, bag unit price, bag amount, print unit / step price, print amount, other fees, original amount, adjustment amount, final receivable, and notes. For non-print orders, print unit price and print amount should be blank or 0; for print orders, show the matched print step unit price and print amount. Multiple rows from the same customer order keep the same original order number and their own line number for traceability. `Delivery details` expands by delivery batch / outbound record with original order number, line number, delivery date, delivery batch / outbound note number, delivered quantity for this batch, fulfillment method, and notes. Amounts default to order-line/style summary rather than batch-level splitting, so customers do not need to manually total split rows. Internal order amount structure should be more detailed than the customer-facing statement: bag revenue detail should store size, color, handle type, quantity, bag unit price, bag amount, matched bag price table, and price version; print revenue detail should store print content / merged pricing group, pricing quantity, step tier, print unit price, print amount, matched print price table, and price version; other fee detail should store fee type, amount, and notes. This revenue breakdown prepares for later profit calculation when costs are added, while V1 does not implement full cost accounting. Total delivered quantity should use final qualified delivered quantity, not original ordered quantity; differences from ordered quantity should be reflected in notes or adjustment amount. The summary sheet should include a totals row for total delivered quantity, bag amount total, print amount total, other fees total, original amount total, adjustment amount total, and final receivable total.

## 2026-06-25 - Statement Period Filters By Delivery Date

Customer statement date ranges should filter by delivery date, not order date. For split deliveries, each delivery batch belongs to the statement period matching that batch's delivery date. If one order is delivered across multiple statement periods, V1 defaults to splitting it into the matching statement periods by delivery date and marking the statement summary note as split delivery. Order date remains a display field for identifying the original order. Customer acceptance should be observed, and later versions can add customer-level statement preferences.

## 2026-06-25 - Statement Confirmation Via Chat Or Payment Screenshot

V1 should not require customers to return the Excel statement. Not every customer has an order WeChat / Enterprise WeChat group; only some customers have customer groups recorded, while some settle on site or through private/manual confirmation. Customer profiles should support optional customer order group records, but in V1 this means business metadata only, not technical WeChat / Enterprise WeChat API integration. In business terms, each customer normally has at most one customer group. Communication/channel values should include no customer group, regular WeChat group, Enterprise WeChat group / Enterprise WeChat customer group, private chat, on-site settlement, phone confirmation, and other. Customer group records should store group name, platform, default statement-send group, owner/maintainer, whether the group name follows the standard naming rule, notes, and whether it is a V1.5 screenshot-recognition candidate. Customer group names should be set by the creator according to a unified naming rule and should not be changed casually; authorized renames and customer-profile group corrections must retain old/new group info, operator, timestamp, and reason. Group members may include the customer, family members, finance staff, customer workers, third-party print workers, and similar participants, so the system must not assume the message sender or payment-screenshot sender is the customer personally. Payment matching should primarily use customer group, customer profile, unpaid documents, and amount; sender identity is only an auxiliary signal. Regular WeChat groups should not get robot/API binding in V1; they are manual records only. Enterprise WeChat groups/customer groups also start manual, and later automation should evaluate Enterprise WeChat customer-group capabilities first rather than assuming all WeChat groups can use robots. For customers with a recorded group, office staff send the statement to that customer order group and attach the two factory-level fixed collection QR codes: one WeChat Pay code and one Alipay code. Customers check the statement, scan the QR code to pay, and post a payment screenshot in the group. The office may also hear the payment notification speaker or wait for the owner's mother's remote confirmation. A payment screenshot can count as both statement confirmation and payment evidence, then an authorized account records/confirms the payment and marks the statement paid / settled. Customers without a group use manual payment entry / confirmation, such as on-site settlement, private chat, phone confirmation, or office-uploaded screenshot. V1 should not connect payment interfaces, perform automatic write-off, or automatically send messages to customer groups. The system should record send group/channel or other send method, send time, sender, whether QR codes were included, confirmation screenshots or notes, payment screenshot, confirmer, confirmation time, and system entry operator.

V1.5 should first try customer group + payment screenshot recognition + recommended matching + human-confirmed write-off. For bound customer groups, OCR can recognize payment amount, method, time, transaction number, and other fields from screenshots posted in the group, then recommend matching by customer group, recent unpaid statements/documents, and amount. For customers without a group, the system can make low-confidence recommendations by customer, amount, and recent unpaid documents, or send the item directly to manual matching. Exact amount with only one recent unpaid document is high confidence. Close-but-not-identical amount enters variance pending clarification. Multiple same-amount documents, OCR failure, unclear screenshot source, or possible split payments enter pending manual matching. V1.5 must not do fully automatic write-off; authorized human confirmation is required before final write-off. WeChat Pay / Alipay merchant interfaces and aggregated-payment dynamic QR codes are not a near-term priority because fees may be high, customers are mostly small fragmented buyers, and bank transfer is not realistic for many; dynamic QR / aggregated payment remains a future V3 option.

## 2026-06-27 - Customer Group Automation Splits Reading From Sending

Customer-group automation must be treated as two different module designs. The conversation archive reading and business recognition module reads and retains customer-group messages, classifies message types, recognizes orders/payment screenshots/complaints, and recommends matches; it does not send messages or finalize payment write-off. The customer-group auto-reply / sending-agent module sends ERP-generated fixed reply templates through dedicated Enterprise WeChat employee accounts; it does not perform OCR write-off or business decisions. It has been confirmed on the actual Enterprise WeChat customer-group / external-group client that there is no add-robot, group robot, message push, or Webhook entry in chat info or group management, so Enterprise WeChat customer-group robot / Webhook is not the main route.

For conversation archive payment handling, the archive reads the original payment screenshot message, ERP stores the original image, OCR recognizes amount, method, time, and transaction/flow number, ERP recommends matching by customer group, customer profile, recent unpaid statements/orders, and amount, then the item enters pending write-off. Office staff or an account with payment permission must confirm before final write-off. High confidence requires a linked customer group, only one recent unpaid receivable, exact amount match, and a payment time after statement send or order completion. Medium confidence covers close amounts, possible rounding/write-off, unpaid balance, split payments, multiple receivables, or third-party payer situations. OCR failure, unclear amount/source, same-amount documents, split payments, wrong group, or no receivable match routes to manual handling. Payment screenshot recognition may trigger a fixed "submitted for back-office checking" reply, but must not directly reply "payment received"; only after staff confirms write-off may the system send a fixed payment-confirmed reply. Payment evidence must retain original group message, group/customer IDs, sender, time, original screenshot, OCR result, recommended match, confirmer, write-off time, linked statement/order, variance handling, and follow-up group replies.

For sending, the route is conversation archive + ERP recognition + Enterprise WeChat employee-account sending agent. ERP generates structured results and fixed templates; a sending agent on a dedicated PC/Mac controls 2-3 dedicated Enterprise WeChat employee accounts such as order assistant A/B/C to search the target group, verify group name, paste the template, send, screenshot, and write back status. Customer groups store available sending accounts, priority, and auto-send enablement. If one account goes offline, fails, or is restricted, pause it, switch to backup, mark backup-account sending, and alert staff. Phase one is whitelist test groups only. Every send must validate target group, customer, order draft ID, content, current account, and whitelist status. Logs must include send task ID, customer group, customer profile, sending account, content, time, linked order/statement/payment record, before/after screenshots, and success/failure reason. The sending module sends no free-form content, does not promise accepted order or production scheduling, stops on low confidence / abnormal amount / shortage / split or complex note / repeated customer modifications / mismatched payment / login expiry / captcha / popup / network exception, uses unique task IDs to prevent duplicate sending, rate limits messages, and merges short-window replies.

Internal work should still rely on ERP workbench, role task pools, and red-dot todos rather than internal group robot reminders. Only severe exceptions should target-push responsible accounts. Rollout should start with test groups, then self-operated store groups, then 5-10 stable cooperative customers, then broader rollout. Customer automation level defaults to disabled for every customer. Only customers in the test group, self-operated store group, or stable old-customer whitelist can be upgraded by an authorized account. The upgrade path should be disabled -> recognize only -> recognize plus confirmation reply -> recognize plus auto-reply plus manual order review -> high-trust automatic order entry. Bulk-enabling automatic replies or automatic order entry for all customers is not allowed. Every automation-level change must record customer, previous level, new level, operator, timestamp, reason, and notes. One-click downgrade/disable must be available; recognition errors, customer complaints, repeated send failures, or payment matching anomalies should downgrade first to recognize only or disabled.

## 2026-06-27 - Auto-Recognized Orders Default To Draft Review

Customer-group order recognition should default to order drafts pending review. In V1/V2, a customer reply of confirm only confirms the recognized content; it does not mean the factory has formally accepted the order. Office staff or another account with order-review permission must confirm the draft before it enters the formal order / accepted-order flow. High-trust automatic order entry is a later restricted capability, not a default automation mode.

Later high-trust automatic order entry can be considered only for simple stock / standard-goods orders where the customer is explicitly at the high-trust automation level, stock-core parsed fields are complete and unambiguous, inventory is sufficient and trusted, price can be calculated from a price snapshot, and there is no strong customer risk, required manual debt handling, complaint, pricing exception, shortage split, manual price change, abnormal amount, complex packing requirement, or special note. Missing fulfillment method or latest needed time should route the line to fulfillment pending confirmation rather than blocking simple stock automatic entry; it must not generate a concrete outbound / delivery task until fulfillment is confirmed. Any failed condition routes the item to order draft pending review / missing information / manual review. Automatic entry must retain source group message, recognition result, satisfied auto-entry conditions, customer automation level, entry time, and execution log, with office correction / rollback available.

Automatic recognition and auto-reply wording can only reference an inventory snapshot and must not promise that stock has been locked. Formal order creation, including future high-trust automatic order entry, must re-check inventory and create the reservation at the same point it creates the formal order.

The order draft review page should compare source evidence and parsed results side by side: original customer-group message/images/files/OCR on the left, recognized customer/order lines/fulfillment/inventory/price/risk/missing fields on the right. Missing, uncertain, low-confidence, stock-shortage, price-exception, and customer-risk fields should be highlighted, but whether a missing field blocks the order depends on order type. For stock/common goods, clear size/model, color, and quantity are enough; missing fulfillment method or latest needed time is non-blocking pending confirmation. For custom printed orders, missing production-critical fields such as print image, artwork/file, print content, print color, or print position route to missing information. The page should expose only three primary actions: create formal order, save pending information, and void draft. Creating a formal order records reviewer, time, source draft, and generated order number. Saving pending information keeps the draft for customer follow-up. Voiding handles invalid, duplicate, canceled, or incorrectly recognized messages with reason logging. If the message contains multiple styles, multiple print contents, or mixed stock/custom items, office staff can split the parsed result into multiple orders or sub-orders while preserving the same source draft and original group message for traceability.

## 2026-06-29 - Manual Order Entry Also Starts With Natural-Language Parsing

V1 manual order entry should not primarily rely on office staff filling dropdowns field by field. The manual order-entry page should provide a blank natural-language input area at the top, where office staff can type a phone order, on-site order, private-chat order, or verbally summarized order as one full sentence or paragraph. The system then parses it into editable structured order lines below. Manual entry and pasted recognition share the same parse-and-review flow: source input / paste / upload at the top, parsed lines, inventory, price, confidence, and risk results below, with office staff correcting parsed fields. Direct line add, manual split / merge, and field-by-field dropdown selection remain fallback controls for low-confidence or missing fields, not the daily primary workflow.

## 2026-06-27 - Automation Modules Need Shared Safety And Operations Requirements

Conversation archive reading / business recognition and customer-group auto-reply / sending-agent modules must share one horizontal safety and operations layer. Before relying on conversation archive automation, the factory must confirm archive enablement, covered employee accounts, external customer-group message/image/file/voice coverage, customer-side notice or consent requirements, fees, retention period, and download validity. Customer mapping assumes each customer normally has at most one customer group, but group members may include the customer, family members, finance staff, workers, and third-party print workers; sender identity is only an auxiliary signal. Group names should follow a unified naming rule, renames and customer-group corrections must be authorized and logged, and manual correction must always be possible. Automation must be idempotent: duplicate screenshots, duplicate archived messages, and repeated successful send tasks must not create duplicate write-offs, order drafts, complaint records, or customer messages. Every order draft, pending payment write-off, reply task, and send task needs a unique ID, source message ID, state, and log. The ERP must expose human workbench queues such as pending recognition, order drafts pending review, missing information, payments pending match, OCR failures, low-confidence matches, replies pending send, send failures, and manual takeover. Sending must stop and route to manual takeover on group/customer/order/whitelist mismatch, login expiry, captcha, popup, network failure, frozen client, or repeated send failures. Each customer group, customer, sending account, and automation level needs a kill switch, including recognize-only mode. Auto-reply templates must be versioned with scenario, version, effective time, editor/reviewer, enabled state, and actual sent content retained per send. Recognition results must be explainable: payment matches show amount, group, customer, time, recent receivable, confidence, and risk reasons; order recognition shows parsed fields, missing fields, and uncertain fields. Rollout metrics should include OCR success, match accuracy, manual correction, send success/failure, customer complaints/misunderstandings, manual intervention count, and labor saved. If metrics degrade, automation should pause or downgrade to manual handling without blocking normal order entry, outbound, statements, or payment.

## 2026-06-25 - Payment Records Allow Variance

Payment recording should store final receivable and actual received amount separately. The system should calculate variance as actual received minus final receivable. Lower actual received should first become variance pending clarification so the office can confirm whether there is statement misunderstanding or customer dispute. If there is no misunderstanding, classify the variance as rounding/write-off or unpaid balance based on customer communication. Clear partial payments, such as 108,000 CNY final receivable with 80,000 CNY paid, should become unpaid balance and customer debt. Higher actual received should be classified as overpayment pending handling and resolved by customer prepayment/balance, customer refund, or applying it as current extra payment against matching debt/missing charge/extra-payment records. Overpayment should not be mixed directly into current statement revenue. V1 write-off boss-review defaults should be based on statement summary total delivered quantity: 1-100 pieces up to 1 CNY no alert; 101-500 up to 5 CNY; 501-1000 up to 10 CNY; 1001-5000 up to 20 CNY; more than 5000 up to 50 CNY. Also alert when write-off is over 1 CNY and over 5%, or when write-off is over 50 CNY regardless of percentage. This is a non-blocking reminder and does not block payment recording.

## 2026-06-26 - Support Per-Fulfillment Immediate Payment

A small number of customers settle immediately per order or fulfillment record instead of waiting for a period statement. Typical cases include self-pickup cash-on-pickup / payment-on-pickup, transfer after delivery, or transfer after express / less-than-truckload shipment. V1 should support per-order / per-fulfillment payment linked directly to an outbound note, delivery note, pickup record, express / less-than-truckload handoff record, or order line. This does not replace period statements; it is an immediate-payment entry point for a small customer group or specific delivery batches. If a fulfillment record is fully paid this way, later period statement generation must not include it again as unpaid receivable. It may appear as a paid reference item for customer checking, but should not enter unpaid receivable totals by default. If paid amount differs from final receivable, the same variance flow applies: variance pending clarification, unpaid balance, rounding/write-off, or overpayment pending handling. Supported payment methods include cash, WeChat Pay, Alipay, bank transfer, and other. Payment records should store source type, linked document number, customer, final receivable, actual received, variance, handling outcome, payment method, evidence, received time, operator, and notes.

## 2026-06-26 - Payment Permissions Do Not Require A Professional Finance Role

V1 can include finance/payment permissions without assuming the factory has a professional finance employee. Finance-related permissions should be configurable separately, including payment entry, payment confirmation, statement generation/sending, variance handling, debt viewing, and boss-review access. These can be assigned to office staff, management, or the owner's mother's mobile account. The current workflow is that the owner's mother receives money remotely, while a payment notification speaker in the office announces arrivals. The speaker is only a payment-arrival clue and must not automatically write off receivables by itself. Payment state changes only after an authorized account records or confirms the payment. If the owner's mother confirms remotely, the payment record should retain remote confirmer, confirmation time, confirmation method, and notes. Later payment automation should follow the customer-group payment screenshot OCR and recommended-matching route first, with authorized human confirmation before final write-off.

## 2026-06-25 - Unpaid Balance Becomes Customer Debt

When payment variance is classified as unpaid balance, the system should create customer debt balance linked to the original statement and order/delivery records. Newly created debt should not immediately enter boss review. It should enter boss review when it remains unpaid past its billing cycle regardless of amount, or when customer debt balance exceeds the configurable threshold, default 5000 CNY. For period-settlement customers, the billing cycle starts from the date the statement is sent to the customer; for immediate-settlement customers, debt is due on delivery completion date or statement creation date. This overdue rule can be adjusted later based on back-office data and customer behavior. Debt, overdue debt, or boss-review flags should not hard-block new order entry, scheduling, outbound stock, delivery, or statement generation in V1; the system should show strong warnings and let office staff continue after human judgment. Next statement generation should show that the customer has outstanding debt and default to including it in the customer-facing statement summary as the last row. The product / print content cell should read `previous unpaid balance`, and notes should include the original statement date range and statement number. Office staff can manually remove the prior debt from the statement, but should provide a cancellation reason/note. The back-office customer list should show current debt balance, oldest debt date, overdue flag, and boss review flag. Customer prepayment/balance exists for uncommon overpayment cases and should not be treated as a daily main flow.

## 2026-06-25 - Support Standalone Debt Payment

If a customer pays an unpaid balance before the next statement, V1 should allow standalone debt payment that directly reduces customer debt balance and links to the original debt record, original statement, paid amount, payment screenshot, received timestamp, system entry operator, and notes. Fully cleared debt should not be carried into the next statement; partially cleared debt should carry only the remaining balance.

## 2026-06-25 - Customer Settlement Profiles Separate Statement Cycle And Payment Due Rule

Customer settlement should not be modeled as only immediate settlement versus monthly settlement. V1 should support immediate-settlement customers and period-settlement customers. Period-settlement customers may receive statements every 5 days, every 7 days, every 15 days, monthly, manually, or on a custom cycle. Customer profiles should separate `statement cycle` from `payment due rule`: statement cycle controls how often the office sends a statement, while payment due rule controls when unpaid amounts become overdue after the statement is sent. Customer profiles should also store whether prior unpaid balance is included by default.

## 2026-06-25 - Statement Cycle Creates Drafts, Not Automatic Sends

When a period-settlement customer reaches the statement cycle date, V1 should automatically generate a statement draft pending office confirmation. The draft should include delivered orders in the cycle and prior unpaid balance when applicable. Statement drafts should use system delivery/outbound records as the source of truth and should not require office staff to manually review every order for missing inclusion or verify every delivered quantity. Draft confirmation should use an exception-review model. V1 hard errors that block sending are missing price, failed order amount calculation, abnormal discount/allowance without confirmed final amount, and delivery record without final quantity. Warnings that do not block sending include split/cross-period delivery, prior unpaid balance carry-forward, customer debt/overdue/boss-review flags, and customer-visible notes needing office review. Office staff must resolve hard errors and manually send the statement to the customer; V1 should not automatically send statements.

## 2026-06-26 - Order Numbering Uses Original Order Number And Line Number

One customer order should create one original order number, for example `ORD-0628-001` or `DD20260626-001`. Each split style, size, color, handle type, print / non-print item, print content, finished-goods style, or fulfillment method should create an order line number, for example `ORD-0628-001-01`, `ORD-0628-001-02`, and `ORD-0628-001-03`. Production scheduling, inventory reservation/outbound, workshop reporting, packing, delivery, and statement details should primarily link to the order line number. The original order number groups multiple lines from the same customer order for customer recognition and internal traceability.

## 2026-06-26 - Order Line Status Flows Independently

Order status should flow independently by order line number, so different styles, stock availability, print contents, or fulfillment paths from the same customer order do not block each other. The original order status should be automatically rolled up from its lines, such as partially in production, partially delivered, partially abnormal, or fully completed. If any line has risk such as abnormal pause, quantity variance, material shortage, or missing price, the original order should show a rolled-up risk indicator, but other normal lines should continue flowing.

## 2026-06-26 - Packing And Fulfillment Can Merge Multiple Order Lines

Internal traceability should remain based on order line number, but shop-floor packing and fulfillment can merge multiple lines when they share the same customer, fulfillment method, and address or pickup batch. Packing records, delivery notes, outbound notes, and pickup records may include multiple order lines, but quantities must still be allocated back to each line. Customer statements continue to split rows by order line / style / print content even when actual packing or delivery was merged.

## 2026-06-26 - Customer Profiles Support Multiple Contacts And Addresses

Customer profiles should support multiple contacts, phone numbers, and delivery addresses with configurable defaults. Order entry should prefer parsed contact/address information from the customer's pasted message when present, otherwise it should prefill the customer's default contact/address for office confirmation. Delivery notes, outbound notes, pickup records, and driver tasks use the confirmed contact/address snapshot on the current order or fulfillment record. Later changes to customer-profile defaults must not overwrite historical order, delivery, or statement evidence.

## 2026-06-26 - Delivery Handoff Uses Warehouse-To-Driver Check

Delivery flow should match the current factory process: warehouse picks and packs according to the order, generates/prints an outbound or delivery note, hands goods and note to the driver, the driver checks goods against the note before loading, then delivers to the specified location. Driver pre-loading check is a required recorded step with driver, check time, and result; mismatches return to warehouse handling or create an exception record. The driver mobile task should show customer, delivery address, phone number, package / quantity, customer notes, delivery notes, and document notes. Watermarked delivery photo is the primary required V1 delivery-completion evidence. When completing a delivery task, the driver must upload a watermarked photo and may optionally upload a signature photo. Receiver signature or returned duplicate paper note is useful but not mandatory because some customers resist signing. If a signature is obtained, the driver should upload a signature photo from the mobile side and later bring back the duplicate paper note for office filing; if not, record a short reason. Delivery completion should not wait for the paper duplicate note to return to the office. Returned-note state can be tracked separately as pending return, handed to office, archived, or no signature / no returned note.

## 2026-06-26 - Delivery Notes Support Paper Transition

V1 should support system-generated electronic delivery / outbound notes and printing, while allowing the factory to keep using existing duplicate paper notes during transition. The system must store electronic note number, order-line details, package / quantity details, and fulfillment information. Paper note number can be manually entered or automatically generated when printing from the system. Paper duplicate notes and electronic notes should be linked for returned-note lookup, delivery photos, and statement evidence.

## 2026-06-26 - Outbound And Fulfillment Notes Show Price

Outbound notes, delivery notes, and pickup records should show unit price and total amount because some customers reconcile immediately after returning and transfer payment directly. V1 does not need a price-hidden handoff copy: both copies of outbound notes, delivery notes, and pickup records show price by default. Local / same-village customers and third-party outsourced printing partners generally know factory prices, and delivery drivers are internal factory employees, so V1 does not hide unit price or total amount from drivers or local third-party pickup people. Factory prices follow unified price tables, and quantity differences such as 10 pieces versus 10,000 pieces do not automatically mean different unit prices or require hiding prices. Prices must come from the order's saved price snapshot, not the current price table at print time. Amounts must be calculated from the current note's actual outbound / delivered quantity, not the original ordered quantity. The original ordered quantity remains in order details and internal traceability, and may appear in note remarks or details if needed, but it must not affect current-note amount. Each line should show product / print content, size, color, handle type, quantity, combined unit price, and line amount, with a note-level total. Handle type should show the actual order-line handle type, such as normal/default handle or extended handle; if the customer did not request extended handle, the line defaults to normal handle for pricing and outbound. Bag unit price follows the saved price snapshot matched by size, color, and handle type, and extended-handle pricing must not be mixed with normal-handle pricing. Lines with the same size and color but different handle types must remain separate on outbound notes, delivery notes, pickup records, inventory, outbound, and statements. Price columns should be dynamic by order-line type: blank stock / non-print lines show bag unit price, combined unit price, and line amount without any print unit price field; printed custom and printed stock lines show bag unit price, print unit price, combined unit price, and line amount. If one note mixes printed and non-print lines, the layout should render applicable price fields per row or group, and non-print line areas should not show a print unit price field. For split delivery, the note shows this batch's actual delivered quantity and amount, while the order and statement keep original ordered quantity, cumulative delivered quantity, cumulative amount, and any undelivered remainder. Normal one-time fully delivered notes do not show original ordered quantity / cumulative delivered / remaining undelivered fields. Only split delivery, short shipment, shortage-to-later-fulfillment, and similar cases show progress fields such as original order 1000, this outbound 600, cumulative outbound 600, remaining 400; those fields explain delivery state and do not affect current-note amount.

## 2026-06-26 - Outbound Worker Mobile Uses A Short Task Flow

Formal outbound / pickup starts from the office: customer or third-party pickup person reports customer name / order, office staff confirms the order and manually prints a two-copy outbound note from the outbound task when needed, and the note syncs to the outbound worker mobile pending picking / outbound task pool. Entering waiting for outbound must not automatically print paper notes; the task should show not printed / printed / reprinted state to prevent premature printing, duplicate printing, or stale paper notes after customer changes. V1 can add lightweight preparation states under waiting for outbound: pending preparation, where office staff or another permitted account marks the task for advance picking, and prepared, where outbound staff has found the goods and placed them in a temporary preparation area. Pending preparation / prepared does not mean outbound is complete, does not deduct actual inventory, and does not count as delivered / reconciled quantity; inventory remains reserved. Completion still requires the outbound worker to tap complete outbound when the customer arrives, driver loads, or express / less-than-truckload carrier picks up. Preparation records should store preparer, time, temporary preparation area/location, prepared quantity, and notes. If the customer changes quantity, fulfillment method, or cancels after preparation, the system should prompt staff to keep holding, return goods to warehouse/location, change quantity, or move goods to another task. The mobile task pool should be grouped by status and use a compact two-line item: `[fulfillment method] customer #tail number` plus item summary / multi-item summary and necessary flags. The list page should stay compact and not require warehouse/location display. Detail pages show full outbound note number, all lines, recommended warehouse zone / location, inventory source, customer notes, and office notes. Recommended location is only a picking aid, not a forced outbound source; outbound completion can default to the recommended location but actual location is not required in V1. If staff pick elsewhere, they can record the actual location, quantity, operator, and time; if the site is busy or locations are not cleaned up yet, they can choose not recorded, pending cleanup, or temporary location without blocking completion. Inventory source should distinguish counted warehouse inventory, workshop-reported / loose / unreviewed inventory, prepared temporary location, waiting-pickup area, waiting express / less-than-truckload area, and similar sources. Customer notes come from the customer order or follow-up requirements; office notes are internal-only notes for office additions, temporary communication, and operational reminders. V1 office notes can start as free text; later the system should analyze frequent note text and turn common patterns into preset options / quick phrases to reduce office entry time. Warehouse/location fields should be reserved for later inventory cleanup, stocktaking, and fixed-location management. Because current locations are messy, V1 can allow blank, pending-cleanup, or temporary locations; missing location should not block outbound but should remain visible as an inventory cleanup item. Outbound worker mobile keeps only three primary actions: complete outbound, quantity mismatch, and unable to outbound. Quantity mismatch must not directly edit the order, inventory, or statement quantity; the outbound worker only records actual found quantity and a short reason/note, such as ordered 500 but found 430. Because the warehouse is close to the office, the current phone-to-office confirmation process can continue, but the system should still keep the mismatch record. The order then enters office exception handling where office staff or a permitted account chooses customer-accepted actual quantity, continue searching, wait for replenishment, cancel, change quantity, shortage confirmation, or inventory adjustment. Unable to outbound must not close the order, release inventory, or modify the customer order. V1 fixed reasons include goods cannot be found, wrong location, unclear customer / pickup person information, document issue, goods not ready, and other; office exception handling then decides reconfirmation, continued search, location correction, reprint/document correction, wait for preparation, cancellation, or inventory adjustment. Mismatch and unable-to-outbound records should later feed inventory accuracy, warehouse/location cleanup, and advance-picking quality metrics. V1 should not force start-picking or verified buttons; tapping complete outbound means the outbound worker checked product and quantity and handed goods over. Customers or third-party pickup people only provide customer name / outbound note, sign, and take goods. Third-party pickup signatures mean the batch was picked up; size, color, and quantity checking remains the outbound worker's responsibility. If goods are insufficient, not found, or the pickup person cannot identify the order, the flow goes through quantity mismatch, unable to outbound, or office reconfirmation.

## 2026-06-27 - Complete Outbound Quantity Defaults To Expected Quantity

On normal complete outbound, actual outbound quantity defaults to the expected / reserved quantity, so outbound staff do not need to re-enter quantity for normal orders. If the actual found or delivered quantity differs from the expected quantity, outbound staff must not directly edit the quantity and force completion. They must use quantity mismatch and enter the actual found quantity, for example ordered 500 but found 430. Office staff or another permitted account then decides whether the customer accepts the actual quantity, staff continues searching, waits for replenishment, cancels the remainder, changes quantity, or creates an inventory adjustment. Only after that confirmation should inventory deduction, reservation release, statement quantity, and order progress follow the confirmed result. This keeps original ordered quantity, actual delivered quantity, and reconciliation quantity from becoming ambiguous.

## 2026-06-27 - Changed Outbound Documents Must Be Reprinted

After an outbound note, delivery note, or pickup record has been printed, any change to order quantity, actual outbound quantity, fulfillment method, price, customer information, or key notes must void the old document and require a revised electronic document plus a newly printed paper document. Handwritten quantity changes on the old paper document are not accepted as the formal fulfillment or statement basis. The main real-world case is factory self-pickup: after the order is entered, warehouse picking may find insufficient goods, the outbound worker contacts the office in person or by phone, and the office negotiates with the customer to change quantity, wait for replenishment, or cancel the shortage. If the customer accepts a changed quantity, such as original order 500 and actual outbound 430, the office changes the order, prints a new 430-piece pickup / outbound note, and marks the original 500-piece note voided. Delivery should normally hand the driver packed goods plus the matching document; the driver checks before loading but does not change the order or document. If delivery goods/packages do not match the document before loading, the flow returns to warehouse / office handling and a new delivery note is printed if needed before the driver leaves. The voided document is retained only for traceability, while the new document is used for customer signature, driver handoff where applicable, customer statement, and payment collection. Voiding old documents, generating revised documents, and reprinting new formal documents are office-side / document-printing-permission actions only. Outbound workers, drivers, and workshop accounts cannot directly void or reprint formal documents. Reprint history should record old/new note numbers, key before/after differences, void reason, operator, time, and print count.

## 2026-06-27 - Self-Pickup Quantity Change Confirmation Is Lightweight

When a factory self-pickup order has a quantity mismatch and the customer accepts a changed quantity, V1 should not require photo or signature evidence by default. The office must record the customer confirmation method, with fixed options such as confirmed verbally on site, confirmed by phone, confirmed by WeChat, or other, plus confirmer / operator and confirmation time. High-risk evidence triggers should be configurable rather than hard-coded. V1 defaults can require a note and optionally screenshot / attachment when amount difference exceeds 100 RMB, quantity variance exceeds 20%, the customer is marked dispute-prone, or office staff manually checks stronger evidence required. A matched trigger does not necessarily require approval, but it does require additional evidence/notes. The revised pickup / outbound note, order exception record, and inventory ledger should link to this confirmation record so later statements or complaints can show original quantity, actual quantity, confirmation method, confirmation time, operator, and evidence where available. For example, 500 ordered versus 430 actual at 0.36 RMB only differs by 25.2 RMB and can use simple on-site verbal confirmation for an ordinary customer; 5000 ordered with 800 short, or a variance worth several hundred RMB, should require notes or WeChat evidence.

## 2026-06-27 - Self-Pickup Quantity Shortage Has Three Outcomes

When factory self-pickup finds insufficient quantity, office exception handling should offer three explicit outcomes. First, customer accepts short shipment and cancels the remainder: ship, deduct inventory, reprint documents, and reconcile only the actual quantity, while the remainder is cancelled. Second, customer takes the actual quantity now and waits for the remainder: keep original ordered quantity, ship and reconcile the current actual quantity only, and move the remainder to waiting for replenishment / waiting for outbound, with a later outbound / pickup note when replenished. Third, customer does not pick up now and waits until full quantity is ready: current outbound is not completed, no current outbound quantity is deducted, nothing enters the statement yet, and the order remains waiting for replenishment / waiting for outbound. All outcomes must retain original ordered quantity, on-site actual quantity, variance quantity, customer confirmation method, operator, and timestamp so order details, documents, inventory ledger, and statements remain clear.

## 2026-06-27 - Self-Pickup Remainder Uses Original Price Snapshot

When a factory self-pickup shortage outcome is `take actual quantity now, wait for the remainder`, the remainder still belongs to the original order / original order line and must use the original order price snapshot. It must not be priced as a new order because the replenishment or later pickup happens on another date. Each delivered batch enters the customer statement period by its actual delivery / pickup date. Example: original order 500 pieces, 430 picked up on June 1, remaining 70 picked up on June 3. Both batches use the original price snapshot; June 1 statement period includes 430 pieces, June 3 statement period includes 70 pieces, and order details show original 500, delivered 430, pending 70 until replenished, then cumulative delivered 500. Delivery date, replenishment date, or price-table changes during the waiting period do not change price; real adjustments require current-order manual override or an exception adjustment record.

## 2026-06-27 - Self-Pickup Remainder Needs Follow-Up But No Auto-Cancel

When a factory self-pickup shortage outcome is `take actual quantity now, wait for the remainder`, the remaining quantity should enter a remaining-quantity waiting for replenishment / outbound todo. V1 should remind the office once after 1 day and move unresolved items into a follow-up-required list after 3 days. Office actions should include keep holding, contact customer, customer no longer wants it so cancel remainder, and replenished so move to waiting for outbound. The system records operator, operation time, reason / note, and customer confirmation method. The system must not automatically cancel the remainder just because it is overdue; cancellation requires office confirmation based on customer communication.

## 2026-06-27 - Full-Quantity Pickup Holds Already Prepared Goods

When factory self-pickup finds only part of the quantity and the customer chooses `do not pick up now, wait until full quantity is ready`, the goods already found on site should move to prepared / temporary preparation area and remain reserved for that customer. They must not be released to other orders automatically. The system should record temporary preparation location, prepared quantity, preparer / operator, preparation time, and notes. After the remainder is replenished, the final pickup note can be generated / printed and outbound completed for the full quantity. Prepared goods waiting for full quantity should remind the office after 1 day and move to follow-up required after 3 days, but the system must not automatically release them; release or cancellation requires office confirmation.

## 2026-06-27 - Self-Pickup Follow-Up Uses Shared Office Todo Pool

Self-pickup remainder follow-ups and prepared-goods holds for full-quantity pickup should enter a shared office todo pool in V1 rather than being assigned to one person. Office clerks, management / owner-mother accounts, and production-management accounts can all see these items, and the account that handles an item is recorded as the actual handler. Items unresolved for more than 3 days, items with large amount / quantity impact, or items matching high-risk rules enter boss / management review. This review list is for visibility and follow-up, not approval, and does not require boss confirmation. It must not cause automatic cancellation, automatic inventory release, or process blocking; office handling continues based on customer communication and permission records. Thresholds should be configurable; V1 can initially reuse the high-risk evidence triggers for self-pickup quantity changes.

## 2026-06-26 - Outbound Signed-Note Photo Is Risk-Based

V1 should not require outbound workers to photograph the signed paper outbound-note copy for every normal outbound / pickup order. Normal orders rely on retained paper notes, with system records for outbound operator and completion time. Stronger evidence is required only for risk / dispute-prone cases: third-party pickup, customer or pickup person refusal to sign, quantity mismatch where the customer still accepts and takes actual quantity, high-value orders, dispute-prone customers, or orders marked by the system as requiring stronger evidence. In those cases the mobile flow should require a signed-note photo or at least a note with reason.

## 2026-06-26 - Pickup Completion Does Not Require Signature

Pickup orders should create a pickup note after packing/outbound. When the customer arrives, warehouse or office checks goods against the pickup note and hands over the goods. Customer signature is useful but optional. If the customer signs, record signature state or photo. If not, upload a handoff photo or record that the customer picked up the goods, including pickup person, pickup time, and notes. Completed pickup becomes delivered and can be used as statement evidence.

## 2026-06-26 - Express And Less-Than-Truckload Use Customer-Arranged Pickup

Express / less-than-truckload pickup often happens after workers have left, so V1 should not require real-time mobile confirmation by workshop or warehouse workers, and next-day detailed office backfill should not be the main flow. Orders can move through `waiting to notify customer to prepare shipment`, `waiting for customer-arranged express / less-than-truckload`, and `waiting for express / less-than-truckload pickup`. For printed orders, after bag-making setup produces a qualified finished sample and the finished-goods photo is uploaded, office or management can notify the customer in the customer WeChat / Enterprise WeChat group that the bag has a finished sample and the customer can prepare pickup. Only after packing is completed, quantity is counted, and goods are placed in the waiting-pickup area should the order enter `waiting for express / less-than-truckload pickup`, so the customer or carrier does not arrive before goods are packed and counted. V1 should support a `waiting to notify customer` list and generate copyable WeChat / Enterprise WeChat notification text including customer, order / delivery note number, product / print content, size, color, quantity, package count, and pickup instruction. For printed orders, customer notification must include the bag-making finished-goods photo; the print photo is internal production-process evidence and must not substitute for the finished-goods photo. If a printed order lacks the finished-goods photo, the system should block clicking `customer notified to prepare shipment` and ask staff to take or upload it first. The notification panel should bring up the order's sendable finished-goods photo so office staff can send it together with the copied message, then click the customer-notified action; V1 should not automatically send WeChat / Enterprise WeChat messages. The customer arranges the carrier; when the carrier arrives, they usually ask whose goods they are picking up and identify goods by customer name or goods label. If the customer does not respond or arrange pickup, the owner's mother calls the customer, but she should not be responsible for system recording. After-hours physical handoff is usually handled by the on-duty management person, currently mainly the owner's mother. V1 should not require the on-site handoff person to use the system, take photos, enter waybill numbers, or confirm from mobile. V1 should support a waiting-pickup list and paper goods label / pickup card showing customer, order / delivery note number, package count, quantity, contact information, and notes. Carrier name, waybill number, license plate, driver phone, handoff photo, and notes are optional fields; V1 should not require them for completion because they are often unavailable or nobody records them on-site. Completion should use a lightweight pickup-area check: the next morning or at a suitable time, warehouse or office checks the waiting-pickup area; if goods are still there, they remain waiting for pickup, and if goods are gone, the operator clicks `picked up`. Clicking `picked up` should not require waybill number, driver, license plate, or photo; the system records confirmer and confirmation time. If the customer later provides waybill information or a dispute occurs, staff can add carrier information or notes later. Completion means handed to express / less-than-truckload or picked up by carrier, not final logistics delivery.

## 2026-06-28 - Finished-Goods Photo Notifications Stay Human-Led In V1

Finished-goods photo and goods-ready notifications remain human-led in V1. Finished-goods photos are managed per order line, so if one original order is split into multiple custom printed lines, each custom printed line needs its own photo. Office staff or production management can reject unclear or wrong photos and require a retake while preserving the old photo, new photo, returner, reason, and time. After a valid finished-goods photo is uploaded, the order line enters a shared waiting-to-notify pool and ERP generates copyable text plus the photo prompt. Office or management manually sends the photo and message through WeChat, Enterprise WeChat, or private chat, then clicks the customer-notified confirmation. The resulting state means only that the customer has been told goods are ready / carrier can be arranged; it does not mean packing completed, shipped, or handed to carrier. Customer-group robot or sending-agent automation must not be the V1 main flow for these notifications. Later automation can only assist fixed-template sending in whitelisted groups with kill switches and manual takeover; complex custom orders, unclear photos, missing information, uncertain dates, repeated customer modifications, or low-confidence cases stay manual.

## 2026-06-28 - Packing Tasks Stay Lightweight And Exception-Driven

V1 packing tasks should appear only when packing is actually needed. Custom printed orders and printed stock/common-goods usually enter packing, while blank stock/common-goods orders enter packing only when packing is required by the order, express / less-than-truckload fulfillment, customer notes, office instruction, or later fulfillment needs. Packing workers do not need a separate `start packing` action; the main action is entering real package details and submitting `packing completed`. Package details remain authoritative for package count and actual packed quantity, and repacking before fulfillment voids old package details and any printed express / less-than-truckload labels. Packing exceptions use fixed reasons such as insufficient quantity, customer changed packing method, package damaged, weighing required, label issue, and other, then enter the office shared pool. Packing workers report the on-site issue only; office or authorized accounts handle customer contact, order changes, label reprints, quantity-difference routing, and after-sales routing.

## 2026-06-28 - Office Todos Default To Shared Workbench Queues

V1 office operational todos should default to the office shared todo pool, visible to office clerks, owner-mother / management mobile accounts, and production management / supervisor. Todos are not assigned to one named person by default; whoever handles the item is recorded as actual handler. The office workbench should prioritize items that can block same-day flow, such as today shipment / pickup, quantity differences, waiting label print, waiting goods-ready / carrier notification, inventory shortages, production exceptions, after-sales / complaints, and price approval. Ordinary todos use workbench and red-dot reminders rather than boss push. Boss review / management watch is reserved for price approval, large allowances, overdue debt, serious production exceptions, repeated complaints, out-of-range free overdelivery, and overstock-risk replenishment suggestions, and remains non-blocking unless the item is a true approval. Manual office order entry with complete information should support `save and confirm` and should not re-enter a review pool; automation-created drafts from customer-group recognition, OCR, robots, or conversation archives still require order-draft review. Todos support snooze / remind later with quick options such as 30 minutes, 2 hours, tomorrow morning, or custom time.

## 2026-06-29 - Office Todo Workbench Uses Typed List-Detail Handling

The desktop office shared-todo workbench should use a left todo list and right detail panel. The list groups or filters todos by type and keeps each row compact with type, customer, order tail number, goods / spec shorthand, waiting duration or latest needed time, and urgent / exception / approval marks. The detail panel shows full order lines, customer notes, office notes, related photos or documents, and handling history. Actions are based on todo type: label printing, quantity difference handling, customer notification, snooze, and other actions should appear only where relevant. V1 should not add task claiming; the shared pool remains shared, and the system records the account that actually handled the item plus the latest handling time. Batch actions are limited to low-risk operations such as label printing and snooze, while payment confirmation, price approval, quantity differences, and other amount / inventory / customer-commitment items stay single-item handling. Completed todos move to a handled view that can be checked during the same day for office handoff and traceability.

## 2026-06-28 - Customer Profiles Provide Defaults, Orders Preserve Snapshots

Customer profiles should support multiple contacts, phone numbers, addresses, contact roles, fulfillment preferences, settlement defaults, price-table links, packing preferences, and risk state. Defaults are for prefill and operational guidance only: explicit current-order requirements win. Orders and fulfillment records must snapshot the confirmed contact, phone, address, fulfillment method, customer notes, office notes, and price result used at that time so later customer-profile changes do not rewrite historical documents, delivery evidence, or statements. Customer profiles default to bag price table 1 and print price table 1 but can link to price table 2/3. Settlement defaults support immediate settlement, every 5 days, every 7 days, every 15 days, monthly, and custom terms. Customer risk indicators such as debt, overdue debt, unresolved after-sales, recent complaints, temporary packing changes, pause-cooperation warning, and management-confirmation requirement are shown during order entry and in customer details. Normal risk is non-blocking; only rare high-risk customers use management confirmation before order acceptance.

## 2026-06-28 - Customer Orders Split Into Independent Lines Under One Original Order

One customer message / customer order should create one original order by default, with split order lines / sub-orders underneath it. Lines must split when size, color, handle type, print / non-print status, print content, finished-goods style, or fulfillment method differs. The original order number groups the customer's one-time order for recognition and traceability, while production, inventory, packing, fulfillment, exceptions, and statements primarily operate on order line numbers. Different lines under the same original order can flow independently: stock lines with inventory can move to outbound, unavailable stock lines can wait for customer / replenishment confirmation, custom printed lines can enter scheduling, and non-print lines can use inventory. Customer statements should show one row per order line while retaining the shared original order number and totals, so customers can see both the line-level details and the one-time order context.

## 2026-06-27 - Express Pickup Area Requires Package Labels

The lightweight next-day check for express / less-than-truckload pickup only works if goods in the waiting-pickup area are identifiable. Any goods entering that area must have a paper package label / goods card. Express / less-than-truckload shipment is package-based, not bundle-based, so the label should be attached to each package. If packages are temporarily grouped in a basket, on a pallet, or in the same area, that grouping must not replace per-package labels. Multi-package shipments should show package sequence such as 1/3, 2/3, and 3/3. Express / less-than-truckload package labels must not show unit price, total price, print price, receivable amount, statement amount, or other pricing / finance information; labels are only for on-site identification, locating goods, and pickup. Prices remain in outbound notes, order details, statements, or permission-controlled finance screens. Most direct factory express / less-than-truckload shipments are custom printed orders, so V1 labels are mainly printed by the office or printed in advance and handed to packing workers. Packing workers staple / attach the label to the matching package during packing before moving it to the waiting-pickup area. Unlabeled or unclear goods should remain in waiting for labeling / pending confirmation and should not formally enter waiting pickup until an office account or another print-permission account prints or reprints the label and hands it to packing workers to attach. This prevents the next-day office or warehouse check from being unable to identify whose goods remain in the area.

## 2026-06-27 - Express Pickup Confirmation Controls Inventory Deduction And Statement Date

For express / less-than-truckload fulfillment, packing completed, package labels attached, and goods placed in the waiting-pickup area only reserve / lock inventory. The goods remain physically in the factory, must not be allocated to other orders, but should not be deducted from inventory, treated as formally delivered, or included in customer statements yet. Inventory deduction, express / less-than-truckload handoff record creation, and statement inclusion happen when office / warehouse confirms the goods have been picked up, usually through the next-day waiting-area check. The system should store both confirmation time and actual delivery / pickup date. If the next-day check can determine that pickup happened the previous evening, staff can set the actual delivery date to the previous day; if unknown, the confirmation date is used. This keeps statement periods aligned with actual pickup while avoiding forced after-hours system operation.

## 2026-06-27 - Express Package Labels Are Package-Record Vouchers

The factory should treat express / less-than-truckload labels as the paper voucher for ERP package records, not as a separate system and not as a delivery-note substitute. Self-pickup continues to use outbound / pickup notes; driver delivery continues to use delivery notes; express / less-than-truckload uses one shipment label per package. The flow is order -> packing record -> package details -> package ID -> print label -> attach label to package -> waiting pickup area -> pickup confirmation -> handoff / delivery record -> unified customer statement. Labels should show customer name, shipping display name, size, bag color, print color, print side, package quantity, package sequence, fulfillment method, order tail number, phone tail / notes, barcode / QR code, and package ID. Shipping display name is a non-pricing field extracted from product / print-content naming or manually filled by office staff; it supports labels, locating goods, statement display, and internal recognition. For express / less-than-truckload custom printed orders, shipping display name is required before formal package labels can be printed or goods can enter the waiting pickup area, but it should not block initial order saving or production scheduling. Self-pickup, driver delivery, and ordinary stock/common-goods orders can treat it as optional. Labels should use small waterproof, tear-resistant label / wash-label-like material where practical, be stapled or hung onto packages, and must not show pricing or finance information. Month-end statements remain unified by ERP order lines and delivery / handoff records regardless of whether the on-site voucher was a pickup note, delivery note, or package label.

## 2026-06-28 - Express Labels Can Be Preprinted But Must Match Actual Packages

Express / less-than-truckload labels can be printed after actual packing details are entered, or preprinted by the office based on expected package count and handed to packing workers. Preprinting is allowed to fit the factory workflow, but actual package details remain authoritative. When packing is complete, labels must be checked against actual package count, package sequence, and quantity per package. If actual packing differs from preprinted labels, old labels must be voided and new labels reprinted from actual package details. Void / reprint records should retain old and new label IDs, package IDs, reason, operator, and timestamp. Voided labels must not be attached to packages or enter the waiting pickup area. V1 only requires system voiding for unused / voided paper labels; it should not force physical collection, photo proof, or signed destruction confirmation. Office staff or packing workers should tear up / discard old paper labels.

## 2026-06-28 - Express Packing Completion Does Not Need Extra Label-Attachment Confirmation

V1 should not add a separate packing-worker action for "label attached" or "moved to waiting pickup area." Packing workers already confirm package count and per-package quantity when submitting packing completion. If valid labels were preprinted / printed, the packing-completion submit action implies the labels were attached and packages were placed in the waiting pickup area. If labels have not been printed yet, packing completion moves the order / packages to waiting label print; after office staff prints labels and confirms the physical print result, the system moves confirmed printed labels to waiting express / less-than-truckload pickup without requiring packing workers to confirm again. Label status should track print lifecycle only, such as pending print, waiting label print, preprinted, printed, voided, and reprinted. If labels are missing, voided without reprint, incomplete, unclear, or attached incorrectly, the package stays in waiting labeling / pending confirmation or label exception rather than entering normal waiting pickup.

## 2026-06-28 - Waiting Label Print Reminds Office, Not Boss

Express / less-than-truckload `waiting label print` should enter the office shared todo pool and should not be assigned to a named person or pushed to boss review by default. Because carriers may arrive in the evening, this todo is more time-sensitive than ordinary back-office work: if labels remain unprinted after 30 minutes, the office UI should show a red-dot reminder; if still unprinted before end of day or before the same-day pickup window, it should move to the top of the office todo list. Confirmed printed labels move the package / order into normal waiting pickup without requiring another packing-worker confirmation.

## 2026-06-28 - Office Label Print Queue Uses Workshop Context

V1 should keep express / less-than-truckload package-label printing with office / print-permission accounts, but reduce search work by making the waiting-label-print queue workshop-first. When a packing worker submits package details, the print task should carry source workshop, related machines, submitter, submit time, order, customer, package count, and quantity. The office print page should default to workshop entries / tabs such as Workshop 1, Workshop 2, Workshop 3, and All; each workshop entry should show waiting-label count, earliest submit time, and print exceptions. Office staff should normally enter one workshop queue and print that workshop's labels. If one order spans multiple workshops or machines, V1 groups the print task by the actual packing submit location or submitter account's workshop, while still showing related machines in the detail, to avoid duplicate print tasks. Future packing-worker self-service printing can reuse this queue and permission model.

## 2026-06-28 - Express Label Handoff Stays Physical And Lightweight

Inside each workshop queue, waiting label tasks should sort urgent / ships-today first and then by submit time, while keeping packages from the same order adjacent. V1 should not add a system confirmation for packing workers receiving printed labels. Packing workers either come to the office to pick up labels, or ask office staff to print the relevant labels when they arrive. If labels are printed before pickup, the factory can use a physical workshop folder / tray convention without a system field. Packing workers report label problems with a single lightweight label-issue action; office staff handles voiding and reprinting. Label loss, wrong-package attachment, damage, and unreadable labels all enter label exception. For overtime shipments after office hours, preprinted labels or an account with print permission should be used where possible. Without a valid label, goods must not normally enter waiting pickup in the system; if the carrier takes them anyway, the next-day office flow records a picked-up-without-valid-label exception and actual delivery date.

## 2026-06-28 - Office Label Printing Supports Batch Print With Preview

The office label-print page should support batch printing inside a workshop queue. Staff can select multiple waiting-label-print tasks or multiple packages under the same order. Before printing, the UI must show a preview / confirmation summary with customer, order tail, workshop / machine, package count, package sequence, label count, and whether the batch includes reprints after voiding. Confirmed batch prints should output labels by order and package sequence and create a print-batch record with batch ID, printer user, print time, printer device, order / package / label IDs, label count, success / failure status, and failure reason. If staff selects tasks across workshops from an all-waiting view, the system should split them into separate print batches by workshop. Labels confirmed as physically printed move to waiting pickup; failed / unprinted labels remain pending or enter print exception for reprint.

## 2026-06-28 - Express Label Print Result Confirmation And Template Rules

Clicking print must not automatically make labels valid, because paper jams, missing label paper, or unclear output can happen. Office staff should confirm print result as all printed, partially printed, not printed, or uncertain. Only labels confirmed as physically printed can move from waiting label print to waiting pickup. Partial success is supported: printed labels become valid, while unprinted labels remain waiting label print. If staff is uncertain which labels printed, the whole batch enters print exception until manually checked. Reprints require fixed reasons such as paper jam, out of labels, label damaged, wrong package, package count changed, information changed, or other, and must record operator, time, reason, old label ID, new label ID, and order / package ID. One package can have only one currently valid label; reprinting voids / replaces the previous label. V1 batch print should be limited to 20 labels per batch, normally print one workshop per batch, use print order workshop / zone -> order -> package sequence, and support test print only for printer / template calibration. V1 should not print an additional batch checklist or label handoff sheet; the preview, print-batch record, and labels themselves are enough. V1 has one express / less-than-truckload label template with final paper size determined by real printer tests; no customer-specific or multi-size templates are needed in V1. Printing remains limited to office / print-permission accounts in V1, while V1.5 packing-worker self-service printing can reuse the same queue, permission, reprint, voiding, and record rules.

## 2026-06-27 - Express Pickup Uses Package-Level Batch Confirmation

Next-day express / less-than-truckload pickup confirmation should enter the office shared todo pool, not a named person's task list. Office clerks, production supervisor, owner-mother / management accounts can confirm; the system records the account that clicked the action as confirmer. No mandatory on-site checker field or separate on-site-check step is required. The UI should support batch confirmation, but package status remains package-level: picked up, still in waiting pickup area, or label exception. Order-level state is summarized from package statuses: all picked up, partially picked up, or waiting pickup. The same batch can share an actual delivery date, such as yesterday, while disputed or unclear-label packages stay in exception handling.

## 2026-06-27 - Unpicked Express Custom Packages Enter Pending Handling

If express / less-than-truckload packages remain in the waiting-pickup area, the system reminds the office after 1 day and moves unresolved items to follow-up required after 3 days. It must not automatically cancel or release inventory. Office actions include continue waiting, contact customer, customer cancels remainder, change to delivery / pickup, or notify customer again to arrange carrier. For custom printed goods, customer-cancelled unpicked packages generally become custom cancellation pending scrap / pending handling, not available inventory. Only reusable non-custom stock can return to available inventory. Final scrapped status can be confirmed by production supervisor or management account, without boss approval. Scrap records retain order, customer, package ID, quantity, reason, initiator, confirmer, and timestamps; photos are optional. Scrap quantity enters responsibility tracing but does not automatically create payroll deduction; customer cancellation alone is not workshop responsibility.

## 2026-06-25 - Pricing Uses Unified Tables, Not Customer-Specific Overrides

V1 pricing should use shared unified bag price tables and print step price tables. Bag price tables and print step price tables should use the same price-version, mobile review, effective-time, and history retention rules.
