The factory does not use one-off special unit prices for individual customers, and the owner's own online stores also use unified price rules. Price tables should use numbered names such as price table 1, price table 2, and price table 3, rather than names based on customer region or business source. Customer profiles should default to price table 1 and can be linked to price table 2/3 when a customer needs a higher product unit price. The higher price is the bag product unit price and should be represented in the linked unified bag price table, for example price table 1 at 0.35 CNY and price table 2 at 0.36 CNY for the same size. Freight, express, less-than-truckload, and delivery fees should be separate order fee items and should not be mixed into bag unit price; this is a rare customer scenario. V1 should not support customer-specific price overrides, avoiding an overly heavy pricing system and long-term individual-customer price management. Price-table changes should create a new price version. Publishing mode should support effective immediately or specified effective date and time, with daily recommended default next-day 07:30 because the factory production day starts at 07:30. If review confirmation happens after the specified effective time, the price version becomes effective after review confirmation and must not become effective before review. When updating prices on the current day, the office should first finish entering prior-day orders that were already placed but not yet entered, using their actual prior-day order time; orders placed today then use the new price. V1 should use account-based permissions for price review. Office staff enter a price-version draft based on management's verbal or WeChat instruction, such as which size should increase/decrease or which print step rule should change, and the system pushes the draft to accounts with price-table review permission for mobile review. The mobile review screen should show only changed sizes or print step rules, old price, new price, and effective time, with actions to confirm correct or return for edits. After any authorized price reviewer confirms, the price version can be published/effective according to the configured effective time; V1 should not require multi-approver signoff. If returned, it goes back to draft for office revision and resubmission, with optional return notes but no required note entry. The back office should record entry operator, reviewer account, reviewer name, review time, review result, and notes. V1 should retain price-version history by price table, including changed sizes or print step rules, old price, new price, effective time, entry operator, reviewer, and review time. Draft price versions can be deleted. Reviewed, published, or effective price versions cannot be deleted, to preserve historical order price snapshots and reconciliation evidence. Reviewed but not-yet-effective versions can be disabled/voided; already effective versions should be corrected by creating a new price version. Order price snapshots should link to the matched price version, so finance can explain why an order used a specific unit price, and should store bag revenue detail, print revenue detail, other fee detail, and total amount separately. Price versions not confirmed through mobile review cannot become effective or be used for automatic pricing on new orders. V1 manual order entry should allow office staff to set customer order time to the actual customer order time; if future customer-group automation through conversation archive reading, ERP recognition, and an Enterprise WeChat employee-account sending agent supports customer self-confirmed order entry, the system can use customer message / order confirmation time directly, reducing the gap between customer order time and system entry time. Order price should be calculated by customer order time, and the system should match the price version effective at that customer order time. Shipment date, delivery date, or split-delivery dates should not automatically change order price; use current-order manual override when adjustment is needed. If an old customer or a specific order has low/no profit and needs a one-time negotiated price, V1 should handle it only through current-order manual override; it must not change the customer's long-term pricing rules. Normal price-table orders should not require a manual override reason. If office staff changes the auto-loaded price, the manual override reason is required; V1 reason options can include old-customer negotiated price, large-order low profit, balance adjustment / extra payment, entry correction, and other. The system should store before price, after price, difference, reason, operator, and operation timestamp. Current-order manual override should not block order flow, scheduling, delivery, or statement generation. If the override exceeds threshold, it should enter boss review queue; V1 defaults to amount difference over 50 CNY or difference percentage over 5% of original amount, with either condition triggering review. Below-threshold overrides should only be recorded and should not alert the boss. Order price snapshots should record the price source as the customer's linked unified price table or current-order manual override, plus matched price version and effective time; later price changes or abnormal discounts should not overwrite the original snapshot. For example, if 30*38 changes from 0.36 CNY to 0.40 CNY on the 6th, orders saved before the change still reconcile at 0.36 CNY, while new orders use 0.40 CNY.

## 2026-06-28 - Packing Actual Quantity And Chargeable Quantity Stay Separate

Packing workers should submit the real package count and real quantity per package, even when the result is below the ordered quantity or slightly above it. The system should warn and require a reason when actual packed quantity is below the ordered quantity, but it should not block packing completion; office or management handles customer communication, allowance, supplemental shipment, or actual-quantity billing. Orders retain original ordered quantity, actual packed quantity, actual delivered quantity, chargeable quantity, free / non-chargeable quantity, and variance reason as separate fields. Inventory, fulfillment, and responsibility tracing use actual quantities, while outbound notes, delivery notes, pickup records, and customer statements use chargeable quantity when extra pieces are confirmed as normal overrun / gift / non-chargeable quantity. For example, ordered 1000 and delivered 1005 can show delivered 1005, chargeable 1000, and 5 free / non-chargeable pieces. Packing weight is required only when the customer, order, transfer warehouse, ocean-shipping, carrier, or office instruction requires it. Customer packing history and default packing preference are only prefill hints; current-order explicit packing requirements always win. Repacking before fulfillment voids old package details and, if labels were printed, requires old express / less-than-truckload labels to be voided and reprinted.

## 2026-06-28 - Quantity Difference Handling Is Non-Blocking But Explicit

Normal overdelivery inside the configured tolerance can be marked by office staff as gift / non-chargeable with the default reason `normal production overrun, not charged`; it does not need per-order free-text explanation or boss review. Overdelivery beyond the configured reasonable range can still be shipped and reconciled, but if it is handled as free / non-chargeable, it enters boss review or management watch as a non-blocking alert. When actual quantity is below ordered quantity, the system should create quantity difference pending handling and must not automatically change statement amount, create an allowance, or bill by actual quantity. Office or management chooses the outcome: customer accepts short shipment, discount / allowance, bill by actual quantity, stock supplemental shipment / fill shortage, custom small-plate manual supplemental printing, wait for remake, or other. Packing workers submit actual quantities only; they do not decide customer communication, amount handling, or remake approach. Quantity differences first become responsibility clues, not automatic punishment or payroll deduction. Only after verification clearly identifies the responsible step, operator, machine, or handoff should the record move into performance / responsibility confirmation.

## 2026-06-28 - Below-Order Quantity Blocks Formal Fulfillment Until Handled

Below-order quantity does not block packing completion because packing workers must submit actual quantity and move on, but it does block formal outbound, delivery, pickup completion, express / less-than-truckload picked-up confirmation, and statement inclusion until office or management resolves the quantity difference. V1 handling permission is account-based and defaults to office staff, production supervisor / management, and owner-mother / management accounts; workshop workers submit only actual quantity and reason. Quantity-difference pending handling enters the office shared todo pool, shows a red-dot reminder after 30 minutes, moves same-day shipment / pickup cases to the top, and becomes follow-up required after 1 day. Customer confirmation method uses fixed options such as phone, WeChat, Enterprise WeChat, on-site, management confirmed, and other; screenshots and attachments are optional unless high-risk evidence rules require them.

## 2026-06-28 - Stock Shortage And Custom Printed Shortage Use Different Outcomes

Short quantity handling must branch by order type. For stock/common-goods orders, especially local delivery and self-pickup, short quantity usually means supplemental shipment / filling the shortage. The supplement links to the original order and original fulfillment record, locks exact matching finished-goods inventory when confirmed, and does not create a new sales order or duplicate revenue. If matching stock is not currently available, it becomes pending replenishment / pending supplemental shipment rather than silently becoming a discount. For custom printed orders, short quantity caused by silk-screen, bag-making, or packing count is usually billed by actual qualified delivered quantity or handled through discount / allowance; supplemental shipment is not the default. Only exceptional custom cases, such as self-operated online-store orders or customer/management-confirmed must-fill cases, can use exact matching blank stock plus small-plate manual silk-screen as an abnormal supplemental batch. Manual supplemental printing and abnormal remake tasks remain linked to the original order, create their own packing / delivery records, do not count as new revenue, and are manually scheduled by office / supervisor rather than automatically entering the normal production queue. Once the handling result changes quantity or package records, any already printed outbound notes, delivery notes, pickup records, or express / less-than-truckload labels must be voided and reprinted.

## 2026-06-28 - Supplemental Fulfillment Is Traceable But Not New Revenue

Stock supplemental shipment, abnormal supplemental batches, after-sales supplemental shipment, and exchange fulfillment should show goods and quantity on the fulfillment document, but should not create duplicate sales revenue. Customer-facing supplemental notes should show amount `0` or a clear note such as `original order supplemental shipment, not charged again`. If supplemental delivery crosses statement periods, delivery details use the actual supplemental delivery date, while statement summary aggregates it back into the original order line rather than creating a new sales row. For custom printed short quantity billed by actual quantity, set chargeable quantity equal to actual delivered quantity and do not add a negative adjustment amount; for custom printed short quantity handled as allowance, retain original ordered and actual delivered quantities and put the allowance in adjustment amount with a concise note such as `quantity difference adjustment`. Internal records still keep full supplemental batch, inventory deduction, packing, delivery, and responsibility-tracing details.

## 2026-06-28 - Supplemental Tasks Are Created From Confirmed Handling Results

When office or management confirms stock supplemental shipment / filling shortage from quantity-difference handling or after-sales / complaint handling, the system should automatically create the supplemental task and reserve exact matching finished-goods inventory. If stock is insufficient, the task becomes pending replenishment / pending supplemental shipment instead of silently changing into an allowance. Stock supplemental shipment states should be pending preparation, pending outbound / delivery / pickup / express or less-than-truckload, and supplemental shipment completed. When office or management confirms custom small-plate manual supplemental printing, the system should automatically create an abnormal supplemental task linked to the original order, original line, quantity-difference record, and blank-stock issue record. Its states should be pending blank-bag issue, manual supplemental printing in progress, pending packing / fulfillment, and supplemental shipment completed. If the customer later cancels the supplement, office staff can cancel the task, release reserved inventory, and record cancellation reason, customer confirmation method, operator, and timestamp. Completing supplemental shipment does not require another customer confirmation; normal delivery, pickup, or carrier evidence is enough unless high-risk evidence rules require notes or attachments.

## 2026-06-28 - Supplemental Costs Stay On The Original Order

Supplemental shipment / filling-shortage cost belongs to the original order or original after-sales / complaint record, not a new sales order. Stock/common-goods supplemental shipment deducts finished-goods inventory normally and attributes the cost to the original order's after-sales / supplemental cost or quantity-difference cost. Custom small-plate manual supplemental printing uses blank bags supplied by this factory's stock, while the small plate and manual silk-screen work are handled by another / associated factory. V1 cost standards are 15 CNY per small plate, 0.10 CNY per piece for single-sided manual printing, and 0.13 CNY per piece for double-sided same-content manual printing. V1 records those associated-factory costs on the original order and supports monthly summary export, but does not introduce full accounts payable, payment approval, or complex processing-factory settlement yet. Manual printing cost is calculated by actual qualified supplemental print quantity, while final shipped supplemental quantity is recorded separately by fulfillment. The unit cost is auto-filled from the original order's print side / content and can be overridden by office staff with before/after value, operator, timestamp, and reason. The system should snapshot blank-bag SKU / size / color / handle type, issued quantity, blank-bag cost, actual qualified supplemental print quantity, final supplemental shipped quantity, plate fee, print-side / content type, unit print cost, associated factory, linked original order / line, operator, and timestamp. Later cost changes affect only new tasks and must not overwrite historical snapshots. If the customer pays extra for supplemental printing / shipment, record it as an extra-payment adjustment on the original order, linked to the quantity-difference / after-sales record and supplemental task. These costs support internal costing, responsibility tracing, and gross-margin analysis, and do not automatically create customer receivables. Responsibility-source options for quantity differences are fixed clues: production short-made, packing under-packed, outbound under-shipped, delivery / express or LTL lost, customer feedback pending verification, inventory record error, and other; they do not automatically trigger punishment or payroll deduction.

## 2026-06-28 - Responsibility Tracing Does Not Auto-Deduct Payroll

V1 responsibility tracing records clues, not automatic punishment. Quantity differences, after-sales issues, or complaints can record responsible stage, optional responsible person, notes, reference loss, and handling history, but payroll deduction requires manual confirmation by an account with management / performance-confirmation permission. A record can name only a stage when the person is not clear, and can later add one or more responsible people after investigation. Multiple responsible people are supported, but allocation ratio and deduction amount are optional until management decides to deduct. Deduction amount is manually entered; system-provided loss values such as supplemental shipment cost, allowance amount, scrap cost, customer compensation, extra plate cost, and manual supplemental printing cost are references only. Employee mobile V1 should not show real-time deduction popups or notices; confirmed deduction summaries belong in a later payroll / performance module. Responsibility confirmation and deduction confirmation must not block order flow, outbound, fulfillment, statements, or collection.

## 2026-06-28 - Payroll Formula Starts From Attendance Hours

The current payroll structure uses punch-clock attendance hours as the base source: `hours * (base wage + position subsidy) + performance`. Detailed payroll fields, import format, performance items, and deduction / adjustment items should be modeled from the user's existing payroll spreadsheet when provided, instead of inventing a full payroll-slip model upfront. Before that spreadsheet is reviewed, V1 should only prepare source data: attendance hours, employee base wage, position subsidy, confirmed performance / deduction records, production quantity, and responsibility-tracing data. The near-term output should be payroll / performance data summaries or Excel export rather than final automatic payroll calculation.

## 2026-06-28 - Payroll Wage Rates Use Employee And Position Snapshots

Employee profiles maintain the employee's hourly base wage and default / current position, while position records maintain hourly position subsidies. Payroll drafts combine the employee's base wage and the applicable position subsidy for the payroll period. Position, base-wage, and subsidy changes are effective-dated and snapshotted so later edits do not rewrite settled months or historical payroll drafts. Temporary position changes in V1 are handled as manual HR payroll-review adjustments with notes rather than automatic daily production-task splitting. Detailed payroll columns, import format, and any more granular segmented wage rules should be confirmed from the user's existing payroll spreadsheet when it is provided.

## 2026-06-28 - Bag-Making Scheduling Sends Mold-Change Work Orders To Technicians

Bag-making scheduling should assign production to a concrete machine, date, and sequence, while silk-screen scheduling stays as a workshop task pool. The large TV board should remain scannable, showing machine, current job, next job, customer / shipping display name, size and color, quantity, estimated completion time, and exception flags, with detailed notes and finance/cost hidden in backend details. Office rush insertion or sequence changes should directly update the queue and highlight the change without requiring workshop confirmation, while retaining operator, timestamp, before/after sequence, and reason. For mold changes, the ERP should follow the real process: production management calculates required mold-change timing from dispatch, then creates or updates a lightweight mold-change work order for the technician. The work order includes machine, planned time, current and target model/size, linked production task/order line, estimated duration, priority, creator, and notes. V1 sends mold-change work orders to a technician task pool, even though the factory currently has only one technician; whoever clicks start becomes the actual technician. The system may detect model/size changes and suggest a possible mold change, but office or production management must confirm before a formal work order is created. Technician actions stay lightweight: start, complete, or cannot do on time / needs confirmation. Completion records actual start/finish time, technician, machine, target size, and notes; photos are optional for normal work and reserved for exceptions or disputes. If a bag-making worker starts production before a required mold-change work order is completed, V1 shows a strong warning and requires a reason to continue, but does not hard-block. Near-due reminders go to the technician task pool and production management / office first, not boss by default. If dispatch changes alter mold-change timing, the linked technician task should be updated, cancelled, or voided with history. These work orders are reminders and trace records, not automatic optimization; V1 still lets office / production management decide timing, using 1-2 hour buffers and later actual records to improve estimates.

## 2026-06-28 - Bag-Making Start And Completion Stay Lightweight

Bag-making workers should tap `start bag-making` / `start production` once when starting a task, so the ERP records start time, machine, operator, task / order line, and current schedule assignment. If they forget and only report quantity at completion, V1 allows backfilled reporting and does not block completion, but marks the task as missing a normal start record for data-quality statistics. Completion auto-routes by task type without per-task office confirmation: custom printed orders move to waiting for packing / packing can start; blank stock or loose inventory becomes workshop-reported / loose inventory; stock replenishment enters inventory; replenishment plus customer-locked quantity allocates locked customer quantity first and puts remaining qualified quantity into stock. Below-planned or below-ordered completion quantity should not stop the machine from moving to the next task; the current task enters quantity-difference / exception pending handling for office or management. V1 also allows workers to skip the current task and do the next task, but requires a reason such as waiting for material, mold change not completed, rush job inserted, machine issue, supervisor arrangement, or other.

## 2026-06-28 - Bag-Making Exceptions Use Continue Or Pause Paths

Bag-making exception reporting should split into `report exception and continue` and `report exception and pause for confirmation`. Minor scrap or light material issues can continue; major printing problems, size / mold issues, or serious machine issues should pause until production management / office confirms handling. V1 fixed reasons are printing issue, material issue, machine issue, size / mold issue, quantity exception, customer / order information unclear, and other. Exceptions enter a shared production management / office exception pool: production management handles the on-site judgment, while office staff records system results and handles later customer communication, amount impact, statement impact, or after-sales / quality routing. Ordinary exceptions do not notify the boss by default; serious cases such as whole-order print errors, likely customer due-date delay, estimated loss above threshold, or repeated similar issues enter boss review or management watch. Every exception should close with a clear outcome: continue production, change task sequence, wait for material / repair, route to quantity-difference handling, route to after-sales / quality issue, or cancel / void task.

## 2026-06-28 - Deli Attendance Final Target Is Direct ERP Sync

The current Deli punch-clock machine should be supported as a direct attendance source for ERP payroll, not as a long-term manual export / upload process. The device model is confirmed from a field screenshot as Deli face attendance machine D5FN, with a masked long-lived-doc SN of `DL-D5FN_25907934****18F3`, and it appears online in Deli e+. Deli's official cloud open documentation lists `DL-D5FN` in the supported comprehensive sign-in API device models, so API integration is feasible in principle. The target workflow is that workers punch on the existing physical device, then ERP automatically syncs, or HR manually triggers `sync attendance`, and ERP directly shows latest punches, exceptions, make-up-punch items, hour summaries, and payroll drafts. Excel / CSV import remains only a transition and emergency fallback for missing API permission, API outage, historical backfill, or reconciliation. The ERP still must not assume automatic sync is available until the factory's Deli account obtains open-platform permission and App-Key / App-Secret credentials.

Attendance integration must be implemented as a replaceable provider boundary. Deli D5FN is the first provider to try because the factory already owns it, but Deli is not a permanent dependency of the payroll / attendance product. If Deli cannot provide acceptable open API access, pricing / contract terms, data completeness, sync reliability, or employee / punch-record synchronization, the business should switch to another attendance platform with an open API. The ERP data model should normalize all providers into the same raw punch records, employee mapping, device/source metadata, sync batches, exceptions, manual corrections, hour summaries, and payroll drafts. A candidate platform must be verified for raw punch records, employee identity, device / organization metadata, timestamps, punch source, online status, historical backfill, incremental or scheduled sync, retry behavior, and credential management before purchase or migration.

Because the factory already uses Enterprise WeChat, the attendance-provider evaluation should prioritize Enterprise WeChat-compatible hardware or Enterprise WeChat check-in / hardware check-in APIs. Enterprise WeChat can provide identity / address-book matching and notifications, but the employee-facing attendance product surface belongs in ERP. Employees should punch on physical devices and then view their own attendance, exceptions, make-up-punch status, monthly hours, and payroll-review summary in the ERP mobile side. Employees must not edit raw punch records or view other employees' attendance; their make-up-punch and exception notes route to HR. Enterprise WeChat should be treated as an integration source and notification channel, not as the primary employee attendance-viewing UI.

ERP should preserve raw punch records, import batches, source file or API source, employee matching, exception handling, and manual corrections. Payroll output should remain a draft or Excel summary until authorized staff confirms attendance exceptions, performance, deductions, and final payroll amounts.

## 2026-06-28 - HR Owns Attendance Exceptions And Payroll Review Reminders

Attendance exceptions are owned by a dedicated HR / personnel role. HR handles missed punches, make-up punches, abnormal punch records, employee matching exceptions after import or sync, and payroll review reminders. HR-confirmed attendance results feed the payroll calculation draft and export. Performance / deduction finalization still follows the existing performance / deduction confirmation permission model unless the HR account is explicitly granted that permission.

Except for silk-screen machine workers, the current attendance baseline is four punches per day: two clock-ins and two clock-outs. ERP should map these to morning clock-in, morning clock-out, afternoon clock-in, and afternoon clock-out, so later payroll handling can support morning / afternoon attendance, fixed lunch-break deduction, and half-day leave. Standard start/end times are fixed but configurable by role or employee rather than hard-coded globally, and the exact ordinary-employee four punch times should be configured later from the factory's current schedule or payroll spreadsheet. For ordinary employees, 12:00-13:00 is lunch / rest time and is unpaid. Missing expected punches enter the HR attendance exception flow and must not automatically deduct wages or mark absence. Extra punches must remain preserved as raw records; the system should match valid segment punches and show extra records to HR. Same-day temporary leave / return segments are valid real-world cases, such as punching out at 14:00 for a family matter and punching back in at 16:00, but early punch-out requires prior verbal permission from management or prior leave/explanation in the large group. Payroll hours are accumulated from valid work segments at minute precision, so the away segment is excluded from punch-clock wage hours by default, and payroll export should display hours with 2 decimal places unless the actual payroll spreadsheet requires another convention. HR confirms permission / leave notice, segment type, and any special paid-hour adjustment; temporary leave reasons use fixed options such as family matter, feeling unwell, outside errand, supervisor arrangement, and other. V1 evidence stays lightweight: HR marks verbally told management or explained in the large group, with screenshots not required by default. These records must not be deleted or ignored. Silk-screen machine workers default to two punches per day, clock-in and clock-out. Late arrival, early leave, and half-day leave are allowed and should be tracked statistically without automatic extra penalty because wages already follow actual punch-clock hours. Overtime has no premium; when a machine, person, or the whole factory works late, management announces the end time in the large group and the later punch-out contributes normal work hours. Leave and overtime are manually entered or confirmed by HR in V1, not inferred automatically from punch records. The payroll cycle is the natural month; HR reviews the previous month from the 1st to 5th of the next month, then payroll is released. Base wage and position subsidy are hourly amounts, e.g. 300 hours * (10 CNY/hour female-worker base wage + 5 CNY/hour bag-machine subsidy) + 300 CNY performance = 4800 CNY. Performance is manually entered/imported in V1, not automatically generated from production or responsibility data.

Missed-punch and make-up-punch requests should normally be initiated by the employee or supervisor with a reason. HR can directly process routine missed punches, forgotten punches, and device recognition failures. Repeated missed punches, unusual-time make-up punches, cross-day make-up punches, or records that conflict with schedule / production reporting require supervisor confirmation or added explanation. Payroll review reminders go to HR first and escalate to management / finance or payroll-settlement permission holders when unresolved items remain close to payroll settlement. HR does not have performance / deduction final-confirmation permission by default; that permission must be granted explicitly if the business later wants HR to confirm wage deductions.

## 2026-06-28 - Bag Cost Formula Is For Internal Costing, Not Customer Pricing

The first bag cost calculation workbook has been captured as `docs/product/assets/bag-cost-calculation-2026-06-28.xlsx`. Its formula calculates bag body material cost from bag width, height, gusset, fold, seam, body GSM, and fabric unit price, and handle cost from handle width, length, GSM, unit price, and handle count. This formula feeds internal material cost, replenishment/material estimates, and gross-margin estimates. Customer-facing bag prices still come from unified customer-linked bag price tables and order price snapshots; the cost formula must not overwrite customer unit prices. Fold is fixed at 3cm and seam is fixed at 2cm for stock/common-goods bags, laminated bags, printed stock/common-goods bags, and non-special custom silk-screen orders. Normal/default body GSM is 78g; normal/default handle length is fixed at 38cm, handle raw-material width is fixed at 5cm, handle GSM is 60g, and each bag has 2 handles. Bag body fabric roll width must exactly match the calculated body required width; the system must not automatically substitute wider or near-match widths for material matching, replenishment estimates, or cost estimates. Body fabric unit price must be selected from supplier/batch pricing because the factory has 3 fabric suppliers with different prices; handle unit price must likewise be selected from handle supplier/batch pricing and recorded separately. These are V1 default costing constants for those product classes, while special models, special machines, or future processes may still need configurable/versioned constants. V1 cost estimates should be marked excluding loss; special models should require manual constant confirmation using the machine-capability table's `other special models` or a manual flag. Cost snapshots should preserve formula version, material unit prices, source batch or estimate basis, fabric supplier, handle supplier, and whether the result is actual, estimated, or machine-allocated.

Bag material costing should explicitly separate `rough estimate cost`, `actual batch cost`, and `machine/task allocated cost`. Before material issue, rough estimates use the latest confirmed inbound price for the same material spec or the supplier price selected by office staff, with supplier/date/source visibly marked. After raw-material labels are scanned in material issue/outbound mode, the system should automatically pull the inbound batch cost snapshot into the related machine, task, order, or replenishment cost calculation. If the actual scanned batch price differs from the earlier estimate, the estimate is preserved and an actual/allocated cost version is created. Scanning solves batch-price accuracy, but if one roll is shared across multiple orders or replenishment tasks, per-order material cost can still require allocation by machine, task, qualified output, formula usage, and leftover records.

The accepted implementation flow for more accurate bag costing is `inbound cost snapshot -> raw-material label -> material issue scan -> production reporting / leftover -> cost allocation -> order gross-margin snapshot`. Orders should preserve multiple gross-margin versions: estimated margin before production, batch-cost margin after material issue scanning, and allocated margin after production reporting and leftover registration. Estimated margin supports order-taking, quotation, and scheduling decisions; batch-cost margin corrects supplier/batch price variance after material issue; allocated margin supports production review, month-end analysis, machine loss review, and material-efficiency analysis. This allows ERP to compare expected margin against actual or allocated material cost without overwriting earlier business assumptions.

Accounting intervention should be minimized. Users must not directly edit calculated cost, gross margin, allocation amount, or loss result. Manual changes are allowed only at source: bag sales price versions / order price snapshots, and raw-material price changes or corrections. If source data changes, ERP creates a new cost/gross-margin snapshot or variance record with audit history instead of overwriting prior snapshots.

## 2026-06-28 - V1 Core Data Model Centers On Order Lines

V1 keeps one original order for each customer order, while production, inventory, packing, fulfillment, exceptions, statements, and payment matching primarily flow by order line. Orders and fulfillment documents preserve customer, contact, phone, address, and fulfillment-method snapshots so later customer-profile edits do not change history. Finished-goods inventory is keyed by size/model, color, handle type, finished-goods style, and warehouse zone/state, with separate on-hand, reserved, available, waiting-pickup locked, and pending-handling/scrap quantities. Order lines preserve price snapshots split into bag price, print price, other fees, adjustments, and final receivable. Packages link to order lines and store package sequence, quantity, weight, label ID, and status. Self-pickup, driver delivery, and express / less-than-truckload all create unified delivery records for reconciliation even though their paper vouchers differ. Workshop reports link to order lines, production tasks, machine, operator, time, quantity, photos, and exceptions. Core V1 tables should first cover customers, original orders, order lines, inventory, inventory ledger, price snapshots, production tasks, workshop reports, packages, delivery records, statements, payments, and after-sales / exceptions; payroll, deep cost accounting, and automation bots remain later extensions.

## 2026-06-28 - V1 Uses Role-Based Surfaces And ERP-Owned Watermark Photos

V1 should organize the app into six surfaces: desktop office ERP, management mobile, large TV production board, workshop mobile, warehouse / outbound mobile, and driver mobile. The office desktop home page is a shared operational todo workbench, while the order pool remains a separate full-search and traceability page. Management mobile stays lightweight for approvals, review items, serious exceptions, debt, and production progress. Production management gets its own workbench for scheduling, mold changes, production exceptions, machine progress, photo retakes, and cross-day tasks. The large TV board is read-only. Workshop mobile, warehouse mobile, and driver mobile show role-specific short task flows based on the current account and permissions. The permission model starts from role defaults and allows account-level extra permissions such as price review, printing, payment confirmation, inventory adjustment confirmation, performance / deduction confirmation, and boss-review access. Driver delivery evidence should primarily use ERP in-app camera capture with ERP-generated watermark photos linked to the order / fulfillment record, driver account, capture time, location, and watermark ID. External watermark-camera photos are allowed only as a transition fallback when in-app camera or location permissions are unavailable.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 Page Entries Use Role-Specific Shells Before APIs

V1 page entry points are confirmed. The office desktop home page is the shared operational todo workbench, and the main navigation should include order entry, order pool, scheduling, inventory, outbound / fulfillment, packing / labels, statements / payments, customers, price tables, and master data. Management mobile should not become a full ERP; it should show lightweight pending review / approval, price review, large allowance, serious exception, debt / overdue debt, and production progress entries. Workshop mobile should automatically route each account to its role-specific task pool: silk-screen, current-machine bag-making, helper material / packing, or technician mold-change tasks. Warehouse / outbound mobile should stay short-flow with waiting pick / outbound, prepared goods, quantity mismatch, unable outbound, and inventory lookup. Driver mobile should only cover today's delivery tasks, task detail, watermark photo, optional signature photo, and complete delivery. The large TV board remains read-only and shows only machine, current / next task, size and color, quantity, estimated completion, and exception marks. The first implementation should build page shells and fake-data flows before connecting real APIs, so navigation, task pools, detail pages, and key action paths can be validated before API, permissions, and database contracts harden.

## 2026-06-28 - V1 Master Data Starts From Excel Imports

V1 master data should use Excel import plus backend editing for customers, colors, specs, machine capacity, employees, initial inventory, suppliers, and raw materials. Customer import should cover contacts, phones, addresses, fulfillment defaults, settlement and statement defaults, linked price tables, customer-group information, and notes. Colors start from factory standard colors with customer, supplier, and internal aliases; aliases support recognition but never automatic similar-color substitution. Sizes and finished-goods styles belong in one standard spec table shared by inventory, pricing, scheduling, costing, and paste recognition. Machine capacity starts as manually entered rough daily/hourly output by machine and common size, then can be calibrated from the user's later production summary sheet covering daily model, speed, and output for machines 1-9 from the start of the year to the current date. Employee accounts initialize from an employee table and bind all operations to individual accounts. Initial finished-goods inventory imports by exact inventory item, including size, color, handle type, finished-goods style, warehouse zone/state, quantity, and source note. Supplier/raw-material data starts lightweight but must include supplier/batch pricing such as CNY/kg so costing does not rely on one global material price.

The first import workbooks should be multi-sheet where the business object is naturally one-to-many. Customer import uses customer entity, contacts / phones / addresses, and customer notes / preferences sheets. Color import uses standard color and source-scoped alias sheets, with alias source types including customer, customer group, supplier, factory internal, and global. Size and finished-goods style import uses size spec and finished-goods style sheets, with allowed-size validation for styles such as 小熊, 喜, 福, and laminated bags. Initial finished-goods inventory imports exact inventory keys. Machine capacity imports machine, workshop, capable sizes, default worker, rough daily/hourly output, and mold-change notes. Employee import includes position, default role, default workshop/machine, enabled state, and special permissions. Supplier/raw-material import separates suppliers from raw-material batches; batches keep supplier original color, factory standard color, width, GSM, total kg, CNY/kg price, freight-included flag, inbound date, batch/roll number, and photo/OCR source.

Initial master-data imports should first run in a test tenant or test environment before the formal database is populated. Customers can import with only a customer name, using price table 1 and cash / unset settlement defaults until office staff completes the profile. Standard colors should exist before inventory import; unknown colors become pending-confirmation rows and do not become available stock. Unknown sizes become special-model pending-confirmation rows and do not automatically feed pricing, inventory, scheduling, costing, or replenishment. Initial inventory can distinguish counted stock from estimated / pending-review stock, and the UI must show that trust level. Employee imports default to disabled until an admin checks roles, machines, and permissions. Machine capacity starts as manual estimates until production-summary data is imported. Valid ordinary master-data rows can import while failed rows produce an error report, but high-risk inventory, price, and permission imports require pre-check and explicit confirmation before taking effect.

## 2026-06-28 - V1 Print Outputs Start With Four Templates

V1 should first implement outbound / self-pickup notes, delivery notes, express / less-than-truckload package labels, and customer statement Excel export. Self-pickup and outbound can share one template with the title changing by fulfillment method. Delivery notes use a separate template focused on address, phone, driver, packages / quantities, customer notes, and office notes. Outbound, self-pickup, and delivery notes show bag unit price, print unit price, other fees, and total amount, but non-print orders hide the print unit price. Line items should show handle type because normal and long handles affect both checking and price. Paper notes default to two copies, one for the customer and one retained by the factory, with signatures optional by default. If key information changes after printing, the old note must be voided and a replacement note printed with history retained. Express / less-than-truckload package labels remain price-hidden and show only on-site identification fields such as customer, shipping display name, size/color, quantity, package sequence, phone tail, and barcode / QR code.

Outbound / self-pickup notes and delivery notes should use the factory's existing dot-matrix printer and current paper stock, not a forced A4/A5 laser-style layout. These note templates should prioritize continuous paper / two-copy usage, high-density tabular layout, print preview, dynamic field hiding by order type, and manual paper-note-number support when existing duplicate notes have paper numbers. Multiple order lines can be printed on one note only when they belong to the same customer, same fulfillment method, and same fulfillment batch; this does not merge order-line state, inventory, delivery, or statement handling. V1 customer statement Excel uses two sheets, statement summary and delivery details. Template maintenance is limited to office / admin / template-maintenance permission accounts.

## 2026-06-28 - Field-Level Permissions Depend On Role And Order Stage

V1 field editing should be controlled by both account permission and order stage. Office staff can edit confirmed orders, but ordinary fields, production-critical fields, and post-fulfillment corrections use different rules. Production-critical fields include size, color, handle type, finished-goods style, quantity, print flag, print content, print color, fulfillment method, and latest required delivery / shipment time. Finance-critical fields include bag unit price, print unit price, other fees, adjustment amount, chargeable quantity, collected amount, variance handling, and statement status. Warehouse workers report quantity mismatch instead of editing order quantity. Workshop workers report start, photos, quantities, and exceptions but do not edit customer order fields. Management mobile actions such as price-version review, inventory adjustment confirmation, and performance / deduction confirmation are visible only to accounts with that specific permission. Every key-field change must keep before / after values, operator, time, reason, and linked note / label void-and-reprint history.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 Button Permissions Use Role Defaults Plus Account Permissions

V1 button permissions should start with role defaults and use account-level extra permissions for sensitive actions. Office accounts can create orders, save and confirm, edit orders, and void unscheduled / unproduced orders; later key changes use change history, exception handling, or void-and-reprint. Office and production-management accounts can create scheduling drafts, publish schedules, reorder tasks, insert rush tasks, and create mold-change work orders. Workshop accounts only operate their role tasks: start, upload photos, report quantity, report exceptions, and pause / continue. Outbound workers can complete outbound, report quantity mismatch, report unable to outbound, and look up inventory, but cannot edit order quantity, prices, or statement amounts. Inventory corrections can be initiated by outbound, office, or production supervisor accounts but only become effective after office or inventory-adjustment-confirmation permission. Printing, payment confirmation, and performance / deduction confirmation are separate permissions. Office can create after-sales / complaint records and handling outcomes; production management or permitted accounts confirm responsible step, while performance deductions remain separately confirmed.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 Review Thresholds Stay Non-Blocking By Default

V1 review/watch thresholds are confirmed as defaults, not hard business stops. Newly created debt does not enter boss review immediately; debt enters boss review when it is more than 7 days past the customer's billing cycle and over 2000 CNY, or when total debt exceeds 10000 CNY. Ordinary debt creates risk hints but does not block orders; only the customer state `management confirmation required before order acceptance` blocks direct acceptance until authorized management confirms. Rounding, write-offs, discounts, and allowances enter boss review when the adjustment exceeds 5% or 100 CNY, without blocking payment or statement flow. Free overdelivery enters boss review only when unpaid excess quantity exceeds 50 pieces below 5000 ordered pieces or 100 pieces at 5000+ ordered pieces. Price-version approvals remind approvers 2 hours before planned effectiveness; if still unapproved at the effective time, the old price continues and the approval todo is pinned. Inventory adjustments enter boss review when a single item variance exceeds 500 pieces or 300 CNY estimated value, without blocking the adjustment. Serious production exceptions enter boss review when they affect same-day shipment / pickup, involve compensation or allowance, repeat twice for the same machine or employee in one day, or exceed 300 CNY estimated loss. Ordinary todos use 30-minute red dots, same-day shipment/pickup pinning, and follow-up after 1 day unresolved.

## 2026-06-29 - Warehouse Mobile Outbound Task Pool Uses Compact Status Groups

The warehouse / outbound mobile task pool should stay compact and action-oriented. It is grouped by waiting pick / outbound, prepared goods, quantity mismatch, and unable to outbound. Each list row uses two lines: fulfillment method, customer, and order tail number on the first line, then goods / spec shorthand, total quantity, and urgent / exception marks on the second line. Sorting prioritizes same-day shipment / pickup, urgent orders, and latest needed time before creation time. Detail pages show customer, fulfillment method, contact / phone, customer notes, office notes, latest needed time, line details, recommended zone / location, and inventory source. Line details include size, color, handle type, finished-goods style, expected quantity, prepared / outbound quantity, recommended location, and inventory source. Inventory source marks distinguish warehouse-counted, workshop-reported / loose, waiting-pickup locked, and pending handling; pending-handling stock cannot be used for normal outbound until resolved. Outbound workers can lightly mark prepared goods with optional temporary location. Quantity mismatch collects actual found quantity, fixed reason, and note, with photos optional by default.

## 2026-06-29 - Workshop Mobile Task Details Are Role-Specific But Consistent

V1 workshop mobile task details should keep a consistent structure while showing role-specific fields and actions. Task lists show task type, machine, sequence, order tail, customer name or replenishment mark, size, color, handle type, quantity, latest needed time, and urgent / exception marks. Silk-screen details focus on customer, order tail, print content / shipping display name, bag size / color, print color, one-side / two-side mode, planned quantity, customer notes, and artwork, with actions to start printing, upload setup photo, report printed quantity, and report exception. Bag-making details focus on machine, sequence, size, color, handle, finished-goods style, planned quantity, source type, upstream print quantity / print photo, and finished-goods-photo requirement, with actions to start, upload finished-goods photo, report today's quantity, report completed quantity, and report exception. Helper material-preparation, packing, and technician mold-change details each show only the fields needed for that role. All workshop details share one exception entry, but reasons vary by role; exception handling can continue production or pause for confirmation. Cross-day tasks are pinned the next day and show yesterday completed quantity, remaining quantity, and continue today; bag-making cross-day tasks must record daily qualified quantity.

## 2026-06-29 - Driver Mobile Delivery Flow Stays Short And Evidence-Focused

V1 driver mobile only handles delivery tasks and must not allow drivers to edit orders, documents, prices, inventory, or statements. Driver task states are waiting delivery, in delivery, completed, and delivery exception. The task list uses compact two-line rows and follows the manual delivery sequence set by office / outbound handoff, falling back to latest needed time, urgent mark, and creation time; V1 does not do route optimization. The detail page shows customer, contact, phone, full address, navigation, delivery note number, packages, order-line summary, customer notes, and office notes. Before loading, the driver taps confirm loaded after checking packed goods against the document; mismatches become loading / delivery exceptions returned to warehouse or office. Completion requires an ERP-owned watermarked delivery photo, optional signature photo, automatic driver/time/location recording, optional receiver name and notes, fixed exception reasons, and optional duplicate paper-note state. Paper-note return status does not block delivery completion.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 State Dictionaries Separate Main State From Exception Tags

V1 uses state dictionaries rather than one-off page labels. Original orders are roll-up objects with summary states only, while order lines own the main business state. The accepted order-line main states are draft, pending confirmation, pending scheduling, scheduled, silk-screen in progress, waiting bag-making, bag-making in progress, waiting packing, waiting outbound, fulfillment pending confirmation, waiting fulfillment, delivered, and closed. Exception tags are additive and do not overwrite the main state; V1 tags include missing information, insufficient inventory, quantity variance, production exception, photo retake required, waiting customer confirmation, after-sales in progress, and responsibility pending confirmation. Production tasks, packing tasks, packages, fulfillment records, statements, payments, after-sales records, and responsibility records each have their own first-pass state dictionaries. Customer-facing after-sales handling remains separate from internal responsibility / performance handling, so customer issues can be resolved without waiting for responsibility or deduction confirmation.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 API And Database Boundaries Preserve Source Records

V1 implementation should preserve source records rather than overwriting values as work moves through the factory. Original ordered quantity belongs to the order line, workshop quantities to workshop reports, packed quantities to package records, delivered quantities to delivery records, and chargeable quantities to price / statement records. Inventory totals are never directly hand-edited; inventory changes go through inventory-ledger entries for increases, reservations, releases, outbound, returns, stocktake corrections, pending handling, and scrap. Key actions write operation logs with actor, time, surface, object, before / after state, and reason. Photos and files use one attachment table linked by business type and ID. Order lines keep price snapshots that later price-table versions do not overwrite. Outbound notes, delivery notes, pickup notes, and package labels use versioned document records with print batches, void/reprint history, and replacement links. Operational todos use one todo table with type-specific handling. The first API phase should cover only the main-flow capabilities needed by page shells: order drafts / formal orders, inventory lookup / reservation / release, scheduling, workshop reporting, packing packages, outbound and delivery, statements and payments, attachments, and operation logs. Payroll, deep costing, Enterprise WeChat automation, complex profit allocation, and advanced forecasting remain later extensions.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 ERD Rules Prefer Traceability Over Hard Uniqueness

V1 ERD implementation should use readable business numbers plus internal database primary keys. Original orders use `ORD-YYYYMMDD-sequence`, order lines append line numbers, and packages, delivery records, statements, and payments use separate prefixes such as `PKG`, `DLV`, `STMT`, and `PAY`; on-site displays may show only document tails. Customer names are not globally unique, and repeated phones or contacts create suspected-duplicate prompts rather than forced merges. Finished-goods inventory is uniquely summarized by standard size/model, factory standard color, handle type, finished-goods style, warehouse zone, and inventory state; color aliases never become inventory keys. Attachments link by business type, business ID, and purpose, with optional multi-link records when one file must support several objects. Formal business records are voided, closed, or replaced instead of hard-deleted. Key records preserve both business occurred time and system recorded time. Excel imports must pre-check errors and duplicates and require a user choice before skip, merge, or insert. Operation logs are required for money, inventory, production, fulfillment, responsibility, and audit-impacting actions, while ordinary view-only activity does not need logging.

## 2026-06-29 - Replenishment Suggestions Combine Safety Stock, Sales Signals, And Waiting Customer Demand

V1 stock replenishment should be advisory and confirmed by people before it becomes scheduling work. Safety stock and target stock are configured by exact inventory key: size/model, factory standard color, handle type, and finished-goods style. If an item has no minimum / target stock configured, it should not produce automatic warnings; it remains available for sales statistics and manual planning. Replenishment checks use available stock, calculated as in-stock quantity minus reserved quantity, waiting-pickup locked quantity, and pending-handling / scrap quantity. Dynamic suggestions use 7/15/30-day sales, with the recent 7-day window weighted highest in early V1 and low-confidence marking when data is insufficient. Suggested quantity starts from target-stock shortage plus customer-confirmed waiting quantity, with a rough one-roll output hint. Customer orders and stock replenishment may be merged into one production run only when size, standard color, handle type, finished-goods style, machine capability, and timing match; customer-locked quantities are protected first. Priority is customer already waiting, urgent stock shortage, below minimum stock, dynamic sales suggestion, and opportunistic finish-the-roll production. Slow-moving items with very low 30-day sales do not replenish if they are not below minimum stock; if below minimum stock, they replenish conservatively near minimum stock. The daily 4 PM replenishment list enters the office / production-management shared todo pool with confirm, ignore, and remind-later actions, and ignore requires a reason.

## 2026-06-29 - Raw-Material Tracking Starts With Coarse Operations And Cost Traceability

V1 raw-material tracking should support material-cost traceability without overloading shop-floor work. Fabric rolls and handle rolls/pieces use one QR/barcode per roll/piece; when one raw-material supplier delivery-note line contains multiple rolls or pieces, the inbound line is split into multiple roll/piece IDs under the same document line. Raw-material locations stay coarse: raw-material warehouse, workshop temporary storage, machine-side, and leftover area, plus notes. OCR/photo recognition only prefills inbound drafts from incoming raw-material supplier notes or material labels; an authorized account must confirm before official batch/roll numbers are created, and failed OCR or raw-material delivery-note/physical-material mismatch stays pending instead of becoming available inventory. These raw-material supplier notes are separate from finished-goods outbound / customer delivery documents. Office staff may enter inbound documents, but confirmed kg prices, amounts, and cost snapshots require purchasing / cost / raw-material-price permission to correct, while ordinary label/inbound-scan/helper accounts cannot change prices. Material issue defaults to full-roll/full-piece movement to machine-side; partial issue quantities are recorded only when the roll is actually split, weighed, metered, or a clear remaining ratio exists. Leftover registration remains trigger-based: return to warehouse, transfer to another machine, end-of-day/stage stocktake, large leftover, or supervisor/office request. Ink and auxiliary materials remain lightweight in V1 as basic inventory/notes and labels, with no automatic deduction or order-level cost allocation. Scanning starts with USB/Bluetooth scanner keyboard-input mode plus mobile scan, not dedicated PDA devices.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 Bag Gross Margin Is Material-Only And Snapshot-Based

V1 bag gross margin should mean material gross margin only. It excludes labor, electricity, rent, equipment depreciation, management overhead, and other indirect costs. Orders keep estimated margin, batch-cost margin, and allocated margin as separate snapshots, and old snapshots are not overwritten by later cost or price corrections. Negative estimated material margin should strongly warn office / management, and low material margin should show a yellow warning, but neither should automatically block order entry. Revenue uses chargeable quantity and order price snapshots, while material cost uses actual produced, packed, or delivered quantity. Therefore, free overdelivery lowers margin: if 1005 pieces are delivered but only 1000 are charged, revenue uses 1000 and material cost uses 1005. Stock/common-goods inventory cost does not need to trace each finished bag to a raw-material roll in V1; it may use weighted-average material cost or the latest available cost by exact inventory key. When one roll is shared across orders, replenishment, or rush insertions, allocation should prefer size-based usage formula times actual qualified quantity, not a simple piece-count average. Returned or transferred leftover material should be valued from the original roll cost and remaining weight, meters, or ratio, then subtracted from the current machine/task cost. Special models, special machines, or new processes with unconfirmed constants can continue through order and production flow, but their margin must be marked `cost pending confirmation / rough estimate`.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 Acceptance Tests Cover Critical Operating Flows

V1 launch acceptance should cover the critical factory flows rather than only screen-by-screen checks. The required scenarios are order recognition / manual natural-language entry, in-stock stock/common-goods reservation, out-of-stock multi-line stock order handling, custom printed order full flow, quantity variance for overdelivery/non-charge and underdelivery/actual billing or allowance, pickup/delivery shortage with old note voiding and dot-matrix reprint, express / less-than-truckload package labels with pickup confirmation, and statements by actual delivery date plus payment variance handling. Acceptance records should keep test data, operator/account, expected result, actual result, and failed items should enter a fix list before launch.

## 2026-06-28 - Order Lines Own State, Original Orders Roll Up

V1 order flow should treat the order line as the main state object. Original orders only roll up child-line states such as partially in production, partially delivered, partially abnormal, and fully completed. Business state and exception state should remain separate: a line can be in bag-making while also carrying quantity-variance, production-exception, or missing-photo tags. One line can have multiple parallel todos, such as customer notification and label printing. Delivered lines should not be directly rolled back; later corrections use additive records such as after-sales, adjustments, supplemental shipment, void-and-reprint records, and payment variance handling. Key state changes must log before/after state, trigger source, actor, time, and reason. System auto-transitions are allowed only after explicit actions such as reporting completion, packing completion, print confirmation, pickup confirmation, or payment confirmation. Customer, amount, inventory, shortage, quantity-variance, and responsibility decisions enter human todo pools. Cancel / close states must keep distinct reasons including customer cancellation, stock-unavailable cancellation, duplicate void, recognition-error void, and management rejection.

## 2026-06-28 - Finished-Goods Inventory Starts With Coarse Zones And Cycle Counts

V1 finished-goods inventory should use coarse zones plus model/size grouping and notes, not detailed shelf/layer/bin locations. Finished-goods status must distinguish warehouse-counted, workshop-reported / loose, reserved, waiting-pickup locked, and pending-handling / pending-scrap inventory. Workshop-reported / loose inventory can appear in lookup and replenishment decisions, but it should be shown as less trusted than warehouse-counted stock. V1 stocktaking should use cycle counts and correction workflows rather than forced full-warehouse monthly counts. Outbound workers, office staff, and production supervisors may initiate a correction, but the quantity change takes effect only after office staff or an account with inventory-adjustment permission confirms it. Corrections must create inventory ledger entries with system quantity, counted physical quantity, difference, reason, initiator, confirmer, and timestamps. Normal corrections do not need boss approval; adjustments above configured quantity or value thresholds enter boss / management review without blocking the correction. Safety stock is configured by exact inventory key: size/model, color, handle type, and finished-goods style. Replenishment logic creates suggestions with calculation basis first, and office staff or production management must confirm before a replenishment suggestion becomes a production task.

## 2026-06-28 - Customer Profiles Separate Entities, Contacts, Groups, Notes, And Risk

V1 customer profiles should separate the customer entity from contacts. A customer entity owns defaults such as settlement rules, price tables, risk state, customer-group record, and long-term preferences, while multiple contacts under that customer can represent the owner, finance staff, family, workers, third-party pickup people, or third-party print workers. Each customer normally has at most one order group in business terms, recorded as customer-profile metadata rather than technical WeChat / Enterprise WeChat integration. Group records should include platform, group owner / maintainer, member-role notes, whether the group is used for ordering or statements, automation-candidate status, history, and manual correction records. Customer notes should be separated into customer-visible notes, office internal notes, finance notes, and packing / fulfillment preferences. Risk states should be normal, watch, management confirmation required before order acceptance, and order suspended. Watch states only hint and do not block order entry; management-confirmation and suspended states are rare high-risk controls requiring customer-risk handling or management permission. Customer-risk changes require before/after state, operator, timestamp, reason, and notes.

## 2026-06-28 - Order Recognition Drafts Preserve Sources And Require Review

V1 order recognition drafts should preserve original source content, including original text, images / files / OCR results, source customer, source customer group, sender, sent time, and source message ID. The draft review page should use a side-by-side layout with original source content on the left and parsed ERP order lines on the right. Parsed fields carry high / medium / low confidence; low-confidence fields require manual correction or confirmation, medium-confidence fields are highlighted, and high-confidence clean drafts can support one-click formal-order creation while still recording reviewer and review time. Suspected duplicate messages are only flagged, not automatically voided. Customer follow-up changes create modification records with before/after values, source message, confirmer, confirmation time, and reason rather than silently overwriting production-critical fields. Draft states include pending review, missing information, formal order created, voided due to recognition error, and suspected duplicate pending handling. Manual corrections may become future recognition hints, but V1 learned hints are suggestions only. Before formal order creation, inventory and price must be rechecked because draft inventory and price are recognition-time snapshots only.

## 2026-06-28 - Bag-Making Scheduling Uses Published Machine Queues, Not Minute Plans

V1 bag-making scheduling should assign tasks to machine, date, and sequence rather than forcing minute-level start and finish times for every task. Planned times are mainly for mold-change work orders, special rush tasks, and tasks requiring technician coordination. Scheduling should have draft and published states: draft schedules are visible only to office staff / production management, while published schedules are what workshop mobile and the large TV board show. Published schedule changes must keep audit history. Scheduling priority is promised customer due date, rush orders, customer-locked waiting quantities, ordinary custom orders, then normal stock replenishment. Continuous-production suggestions for same size, color, handle type, or handle setup are advisory and must not override customer due dates or rush priority. Estimated completion time is a reference value calculated from machine capacity, quantity, mold-change buffer, and material-wait buffer, and can be manually edited with reason. Cross-day unfinished tasks stay on the original machine queue or continue-task area with yesterday's completed quantity, remaining quantity, state, original sequence, and impact on today's queue.

## 2026-06-29 - Production Board Estimates Stay Coarse And Traceable

Initial machine capacity is maintained manually by `machine + size/model` as rough daily/hourly output until the later production-summary sheet calibrates it; missing values may use same-size or similar-machine defaults but must show low confidence. V1 estimated completion is calculated from `current queue sequence + quantity / default capacity + mold-change buffer + material-wait buffer + rest / end-of-day impact`. The large TV board should not imply minute precision; it should show coarse values such as expected morning, expected afternoon, expected tonight, expected tomorrow, or a rough time window. Manual ETA edits by office / production management require fixed reasons such as rush order, waiting material, mold change, machine issue, customer chasing, or other. If estimated completion is later than the latest needed time, it enters the office / production-management todo pool; only serious same-day promised-delivery risks enter management watch. Formal mold-change work orders are created only when technician coordination is actually needed, not for ordinary continuous production. Published rush insertion directly changes the visible queue, auto-refreshes workshop mobile / TV, and records operator, time, reason, original sequence, and new sequence.

## 2026-06-28 - Silk-Screen V1 Uses Task Pools, Disposable Screens, And Print-Only External Processing

V1 silk-screen work should use a same-day task pool with a tomorrow preview. Workers may mark / claim tasks, but unstarted marked tasks should not be automatically released; reminders are enough, while workers, office staff, or supervisors can cancel or reassign. At least one print photo is required after setup, with multiple photos allowed for double-sided, different-content, multi-color, or high-risk jobs. The actual silk-screen worker reports actual printed quantity; quantity exceptions enter variance / production-exception handling but should not block later bag-making by default. Plates / screens are generally one-time consumables, so V1 should not build plate / screen inventory, reuse reminders, or mandatory plate-number management. Ink is not deducted from inventory in V1; silk-screen tasks only record print color and necessary notes. External-processing / peer-processing print orders are a separate task type that only covers printing and delivery / return / pickup. They do not enter bag-making, stock replenishment, or finished-goods inventory, and completion creates printing-service fulfillment / statement records only.

## 2026-06-28 - Silk-Screen Exceptions And External-Processing Statements Stay Lightweight

Silk-screen print photos can be returned by office staff / production management when they are wrong, unclear, linked to the wrong order, or do not show the print result clearly enough. The system should preserve old photo, new photo, returner, return time, and reason. A returned photo blocks normal printing completion until a replacement is accepted, with supervisor / office override available by reason. Silk-screen exceptions split into continue versus pause-for-confirmation paths, with fixed V1 reasons such as wrong print color, position offset, missing print / ghosting, dirty screen, print content mismatch, material issue, quantity exception, and other. External-processing print jobs commonly use one print layout and several material rolls supplied by the customer / processing party. V1 should not manage leftover material for those jobs and should not require remaining-roll, remaining-meter, or leftover-return records. The main quantity is actual printed quantity, with optional lightweight supplied-material notes or roll-count notes. External-processing print-service receivables use print unit price times actual printed quantity, support per-order manual price override with snapshot, and appear on statements as customer, print content / item name, print color, actual printed quantity, print unit price, amount, delivery date, and notes. Quality issues route to after-sales / complaints with linked silk-screen task, operator, photos, quantity, and amount adjustment.

## 2026-06-28 - Bag-Making Reporting Requires Accepted Finished-Goods Photos And Daily Quantities

Bag-making mobile task visibility should follow published machine queues and the current schedule: workers see only published tasks assigned to their current machine / shift plus that machine's cross-day continue tasks. Temporary substitutions are handled by the schedule record rather than hard-coded default machine binding. Custom printed orders require a finished-goods photo after the qualified sample appears; blank stock replenishment does not require a finished-goods photo by default. Office staff or production management can return wrong or unclear finished-goods photos, preserving old photo, new photo, return reason, returner, and timestamp. Only an uploaded and not-returned / accepted finished-goods photo can move an order line into the shared customer-notification-ready pool, and that state still does not mean packing is completed. Completed quantity is submitted by the current logged-in actual bag-making worker, with machine, task / order line, time, and schedule context recorded automatically. Short or excessive quantities are submitted as actual quantities and flagged by variance rules without blocking the worker from moving to the next task. Cross-day tasks require the worker to enter that day's qualified quantity before ending the day; the next day shows yesterday's completed quantity and remaining quantity. Bag-making exceptions use the lightweight continue / pause paths with fixed reasons.

## 2026-06-29 - Statements Separate Customer Send, Internal Archive, And Payment Confirmation

V1 statements should start with a system default Excel template and later adapt to the factory's current template once provided. The product should distinguish a concise customer-send version from an internal archive version that keeps delivery batches, adjustment reasons, evidence, customer confirmations, debt carry-forward, payment matching, and internal notes. Statements still enter periods by actual delivery date; cross-period split deliveries are summarized for customers while internal records retain each batch. Payment entry and payment confirmation are separate permissions: office, outbound, or driver accounts may submit payment clues, but only payment-confirmation accounts can confirm arrival and write off receivables. The owner's mother / management mobile account can confirm payment. Statement sends, recipients, exported files, customer replies, and payment screenshots should be linked to the statement. Routine bag-making waste sold as scrap can be recorded as simple other income, not customer revenue or order-level cost offset.

## 2026-06-29 - External-Processing Print Orders Stay Service-Only

External-processing / peer-processing print orders are service-only print orders. They do not enter this factory's bag-making flow, stock replenishment, finished-goods inventory, raw-material inventory, or material-cost allocation. Supplied material is recorded only as lightweight metadata such as processing party, roll count, material color/spec notes, optional photo, and optional estimated printable quantity. Pricing uses a separate external-processing print price table and bills by print unit price times actual printed quantity, with current-order override and price snapshot support. Side, print color, and color count are retained for statistics and reconciliation, but V1 does not build complex automatic pricing. Completion creates a printing-service fulfillment record, with return delivery, self-pickup, and express / less-than-truckload using normal fulfillment evidence. Quality issues route to after-sales / complaints with fixed outcomes such as customer accepts, allowance, reprint, no compensation / no handling, or other. Associated-factory small-plate and manual supplemental printing only needs monthly summary export in V1 and should not become full accounts payable.

## 2026-06-29 - Initial Master Data Uses Staged Imports And High-Risk Gates

V1 initial master data should be imported in stages, not as one risky all-or-nothing launch file. Customers may import with only a name, defaulting to price table 1 and cash / unset settlement. Inventory import requires standard colors first; unknown colors and unknown sizes become pending-confirmation rows. Initial stock must mark counted versus estimated / pending review, and the UI must show this trust level before formal stock promises or outbound. Employee accounts import disabled until roles, machines, and permissions are checked. Machine capacity starts as low-confidence manual estimates until production-summary data calibrates it. Price-table import is high-risk and must full-table pre-check price table number, version, effective time, reviewer, and changed values before an authorized review account approves it; failed rows must not be skipped while the remaining rows go live. Actual V1 template generation should start with customers, colors / aliases, sizes / finished-goods styles, employees, machine capacity, and initial finished-goods inventory; suppliers and raw-material batches follow second.

## 2026-07-04 - Master Data Precheck Accepts Common Re-Saved XLSX Shapes

V1 master-data upload precheck should not depend on the exact ZIP shape produced by the ERP template generator.
