The reader should accept common Excel / WPS / external-tool re-saved `.xlsx` files when the workbook still follows the required sheet and column contract: deflate-compressed ZIP entries, local file headers with zero sizes and central-directory sizes, sharedStrings including rich text runs, formula cells with cached values, XML attributes written with single quotes, value tags with attributes, omitted cell coordinates that can be read in sequential column order, and absolute or relative workbook relationship targets. This compatibility only covers file-shape parsing; business validation, staged import review, confirmation plans, formal import permissions, rollback, and failed-row handling remain mandatory.

## 2026-06-29 - V1 Builds Page Shells Before Hardening Database

V1 development should validate page shells and fake-data workflows before hardening the database and real APIs. ERD and API contracts should still be drafted in parallel, but the first usable work should prove office and shop-floor paths: desktop office shared todo workbench, order entry / recognition, order pool, inventory lookup, outbound / fulfillment, and statements / payments first, then workshop mobile, warehouse / outbound mobile, driver mobile, management mobile, and TV board refinements. V1 is single factory and single operational/accounting tenant; it should not implement multi-company, multi-store, multi-ledger, or complex warehouse organization yet. Warehouse modeling starts with zones and inventory states. Trial imports use a test tenant before formal data. The first realistic sample dataset should contain 20-50 desensitized orders covering stock, shortage, custom print, express/LTL, delivery, pickup, quantity variance, and payment variance. Dot-matrix outbound/pickup and delivery templates are the first print templates, followed by express/LTL labels and statement Excel polish.

## 2026-06-29 - Customer Group Automation Is Manual-Only In V1

V1 must not implement automatic customer-group reading, automatic formal order creation, or automatic customer-group replies. Customer-group support in V1 is business metadata plus manual handling: copied source text, screenshot uploads, manual draft review, manual statement/payment confirmation, and manual customer communication. Customer profiles keep lightweight fields for group name, platform, ordering use, statement use, group owner, notes, and automation candidate level. The candidate levels are disabled, record group only, future pilot candidate, and automation forbidden. Later automation remains split into conversation archive reading and Enterprise WeChat employee-account sending-agent modules. Even in V2, automatic replies use fixed templates only and must not promise accepted orders, scheduled production, or guaranteed stock. Pilots start with 5-10 stable cooperative customers or self-operated store groups after the core ERP flow is stable. Internal reminders stay in the ERP workbench and role task pools, with only severe exceptions pushed to responsible accounts.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Prototype Is Office-Desktop First

P0 is limited to six desktop office pages: shared todo workbench, order entry / recognition, order pool, inventory lookup, outbound / fulfillment, and statements / payments. Mobile role surfaces and the production TV board should be preview / placeholder surfaces with fake data in P0, not complete interaction loops. Order recognition starts with a large pasted-text input, local rule-based parsing, and editable parsed lines, without AI or external APIs. Inventory lookup, reservations, outbound state changes, document / label previews, statement tables, and payment states are simulated first. Acceptance uses 20-50 desensitized sample orders to validate the main workflow and exception handling before real APIs, real printers, final Excel styles, or deeper backend hardening.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Synthetic Data Mirrors Real Business Keys

P0 may start with 30 synthetic business records so the prototype can proceed before real desensitized samples are available. The synthetic data should include 12 customer fixtures covering settlement cycles, debt, pickup, delivery, and express / less-than-truckload behavior; eight order categories covering in-stock stock, out-of-stock stock, custom print, printed stock, external-processing print, pickup, delivery, and express / less-than-truckload; deliberately messy pasted-order strings; inventory records keyed by size / model, color, handle type, finished-goods style, and zone / state; confirmed bag-price and silk-screen tier fixtures; and accounts for office, management, production supervisor, warehouse, driver, silk-screen, bag-making, and packing. Real desensitized orders later replace or calibrate these fixtures, but the P0 prototype should not wait for them.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Uses Dense Office ERP Interaction Defaults

P0 office pages should use a narrow left navigation rail plus a top page title / action bar. The visual density should be a high-density SaaS ERP / operational-table style, not large-card or marketing-style pages. Navigation exposes the six usable office pages first and keeps later modules as gray placeholders. Order entry uses a large free-text input above editable parsed table rows; common fields are edited inline, while complex print information and notes open in a side detail panel. Inventory status colors are available green, reserved / occupied yellow, out-of-stock red, and pending / unavailable gray. Statement and payment pages are customer-first, with customer list on the left and selected-customer period orders and payment state on the right. Status colors are unified across modules: normal blue / green, pending yellow, exception red, completed muted green, and voided gray.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Shared Todo Workbench Uses A Small Public Pool

P0 shared todos start with eight types: order draft pending confirmation, shortage pending handling, quantity difference pending handling, label waiting print, express / less-than-truckload pickup confirmation, statement waiting generation, payment variance pending confirmation, and boss / management watch. Todos are sorted by rush / today-first, then red exceptions, then latest required time and waiting duration. They are not claimed or assigned; all permitted accounts can handle them and the system records the actual handler. The detail panel shows customer, order tail number, source, summary, key quantities, amount impact, notes, and recommended action, with full order details opened separately. Batch actions are limited to low-risk snooze, print preview, mark viewed, and label printing; payment, quantity, price, inventory, and customer-commitment decisions are handled one by one. P0 reminders stay inside ERP with red dots, badges, and pinned list placement, and handled todos default to today with a 7-day filter.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Order Entry Prioritizes Paste Then Edit

P0 order entry / recognition uses a large top input area with only recognize, clear, and fill-sample primary actions. Recognition automatically splits one pasted message into multiple editable order lines when it contains multiple sizes, colors, print contents, or fulfillment methods. The visible table fields are customer, product / print content, size, color, handle type, finished-goods style, print flag, quantity, fulfillment method, latest needed time, inventory status, and estimated amount. Complex print artwork, print side, print color, customer notes, office notes, price snapshot, and source evidence live in a side detail panel. Missing-field blocking depends on order type: stock orders require only core stock fields, while custom printed orders require production-critical print fields. Confidence states are normal, yellow warning, and red required confirmation. Footer actions are save draft, save and confirm, split order, and void draft; complete manual entries can save and confirm directly without duplicate shared-todo review.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Order Pool Is For Search And Traceability

P0 keeps the order pool separate from the shared todo workbench. Shared todos are for immediate actions, while the order pool is for full search, filtering, traceability, and status lookup. The default scope is latest-30-days incomplete orders plus today-completed orders, with an all-orders switch. The main list uses order-line rows because production, inventory, outbound, fulfillment, and statements flow by order line; original-order grouping and filtering remains available above the list. Columns include order / line number, customer, product / print content, size, color, handle type, quantity, order type, current status, fulfillment method, latest needed time, amount, and exception marks. Filters start with customer, status, order type, fulfillment method, exception flag, date range, statement status, and debt / payment variance. Opening an item uses a right-side drawer with status and key actions, then detail, production, inventory, packing, fulfillment, statement, and operation-history sections. List actions stay low-risk: view, copy, open detail, and void draft; key edits use order detail or dedicated flows.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Inventory Lookup Uses Exact Keys And No Direct Total Edits

P0 inventory lookup displays finished-goods inventory by exact inventory key: size / model, color, handle type, finished-goods style, and zone / state. The default view focuses on available, reserved / occupied, and waiting-pickup locked stock, while pending-handling / pending-scrap stock is collapsed. Quantity columns include in-stock, reserved, available, waiting-pickup locked, pending-handling, and estimated / pending-review flags. Shortage handling shows shortage quantity, replenishment / production suggestion, copyable customer text, and reference-only similar-stock hints. Similar colors or nearby sizes never count as available stock, cannot be one-click substitutes, and cannot generate in-stock customer wording. Inventory detail shows source trust summaries such as counted inventory, workshop-reported inventory, packing-counted inventory, waiting-pickup locked stock, and estimated stock. P0 does not allow direct editing of total inventory; it only exposes a start-inventory-correction action, with actual stock changes handled through ledger entries, confirmed corrections, or business actions.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Outbound And Fulfillment Uses Shared Tabs And Simulated Printing

P0 outbound / fulfillment should use one shared fulfillment dataset with tabs for all, self-pickup, driver delivery, and express / less-than-truckload. The default view focuses on records due today, incomplete outbound / fulfillment records, and pending exceptions; completed history is available through filters. Rows show customer, order / line tail number, fulfillment method, goods / spec shorthand, quantity / package count, latest needed time, current status, and customer-note / office-note markers. A right-side drawer shows contact and address details, order-line details, inventory source, package / label / document preview, customer notes, office notes, and operation history. Initial actions are mark prepared, complete outbound / fulfillment, quantity mismatch, unable to outbound, print / label preview, and confirm express / LTL picked up. Quantity mismatch must use a fixed reason flow that records actual quantity, creates an office todo, and logs the action; the list does not directly edit quantities. Printing in P0 is browser-preview and simulated printed / void-reprinted states only; real dot-matrix printers, label printers, and final templates are integrated later.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Statements And Payments Stay Customer-First

P0 statements / payments should use a customer-first work view. The default list shows customers that need current-period statement generation, customers with unpaid variance / debt, and customers with payments waiting confirmation; settled history is available through filters. Customer rows show customer name, settlement cycle type, current receivable amount, debt / overdue marker, payment-confirmation marker, and last statement date. The right-side detail is divided into customer summary, current-period order / line table, totals, adjustments, received amount, variance handling, and operation history. Customer-facing statement details summarize by order line, while internal drilldown keeps delivery batches, package records, self-pickup / delivery / express evidence, and payment records. Initial actions are generate statement preview, mark sent, record received amount, variance pending confirmation, confirm write-off, and export Excel. Less-than-receivable payments default to variance pending confirmation; the system must not automatically treat them as rounding or write-off. P0 export is a browser statement table plus the first system-default Excel template until the factory's real statement template is provided.

## 2026-07-01 - P0 Statement Export Initially Used Two-Sheet SpreadsheetML

This historical decision established the two-sheet statement export structure before true `.xlsx` generation existed. It has been superseded by the 2026-07-02 shared XLSX template decision: P0 statement `导出Excel` now emits a real Open XML `.xlsx` workbook with fixed worksheet names `对账汇总` and `交付明细`. The export keeps the same preview / permission flow as `POST /api/statements/{id}/preview` and uses internal-archive preview data; later template work can replace the styling without changing the user-facing two-sheet structure.

## 2026-07-01 - Statement Export Download Token Is The File Boundary

Statement preview should return a `downloadToken`, and the actual exported workbook should be fetched through `GET /api/statements/{statementId}/exports/{downloadToken}`. In P0 the backend may keep the generated workbook in memory, but the frontend should still treat the token as a backend file reference and fall back to local generation only when the download endpoint is unavailable. Explicit backend permission denials should block fallback. Future object storage or formal template rendering should preserve this preview-token-download boundary.

## 2026-06-29 - P0 Builds Frontend-Local Office Pages First

P0 implementation should now move from requirements refinement to a frontend-local React prototype for the six desktop office pages: shared todo, order entry / recognition, order pool, inventory lookup, outbound / fulfillment, and statements / payments. These six pages are clickable; later modules remain disabled / gray placeholders. React local state and fake data simulate recognition, inventory, reservations, outbound, statements, printing, export, and payment states. P0 does not connect to a database, real API, AI service, payment interface, real printer, or final Excel template. Fake data starts with about 30 orders, 12 customers, and enough inventory, todo, package, and statement records to cover the main business scenarios. Print and export actions use previews or simulated state changes only. Acceptance is scenario-based, with at least 2-3 business cases per core page, and the running local prototype must be checked in the browser for desktop table density, controls, drawers, dialogs, and text overflow before handoff.

## 2026-07-11 - Feature Pages And Create Workflows Use Explicit Ownership Boundaries

Large office pages should move into `src/features/<domain>` while their former barrel modules preserve public re-exports until callers can migrate safely. Cross-page display-only helpers belong to the relevant shared feature instead of remaining hidden at the bottom of a page file. Backend create workflows that combine validation, digesting, deduplication, storage, audit input, and repository transactions belong in `server/services`; HTTP routes should retain authentication, permission, error, and response mapping only. Each extraction must preserve URLs, permissions, API contracts, and business rules and must add a direct behavior check before joining `check:core`.

## 2026-07-11 - Core Navigation Is Stable And Role Tools Are Permission-Scoped

The left navigation always presents the six office core pages first: todos, entry, orders, inventory, fulfillment, and statements. Implemented V1 operational surfaces outside that P0 core belong in a secondary `role tools` group and appear only when the current permission set matches; losing access while a secondary page is active returns the user to the todo page. Stable English API/database enums remain valid internal contracts, but API clients must convert them to Chinese operator labels and reverse-map Chinese filters before requests. Internal enum values must not appear in operational tables, details, labels, or action text.

## 2026-07-11 - Fulfillment Prepared State Is Audited But Non-Financial

Marking a fulfillment as prepared is a real backend state transition with permission, idempotency, and operation-log coverage. It must not deduct finished-goods inventory, release reservations, create a statement candidate, or imply customer handover; only fulfillment completion or pickup confirmation performs those downstream effects. Delivery dispatch is also server-authoritative, and the fulfillment list projection must return the active driver, route date, route number, stop sequence, dispatch status, planned departure, assignment metadata, and remark so a committed dispatch cannot disappear after refresh.

## 2026-07-11 - Production Machine Assignment Changes Only Through Scheduling

An existing production task's machine assignment is server-authoritative and can change only through the permissioned machine-queue move workflow. Daily progress and production-completion reporting may record the assigned machine as evidence, but a stale or default client machine value must not overwrite the current assignment. Machine action/cycle counts remain evidence only and never select a machine, create finished-goods inventory, reserve stock, or create a packing task by themselves.

## 2026-07-11 - Global Interaction State Uses One Controller Boundary

Global toast and overlay state, permission action guards, and cross-domain modal routing belong in an application interaction controller rather than `App.jsx` or individual pages. The controller may coordinate existing domain write hooks and API clients, but it must not duplicate permissions, URLs, transaction rules, or business state machines. Permission denials, version/lock conflicts, and blocked results without feature-specific feedback must produce a visible normalized message; committed business writes remain server-authoritative and feature hooks remain responsible for projection refresh behavior.

## 2026-07-11 - Shared Operational UI Uses a Compatibility Layer

Shared office UI carries structure, accessibility, and visual rules only; feature modules continue to own business fields, permissions, and action eligibility. The legacy `styles.css` remains loaded during staged migration, while tokens, shell, shared components, and feature styles load afterward. Page descriptions belong to navigation metadata, and the workspace header shows a read-only runtime context plus one real refresh action; a no-op “local demo” command is not shown. Public Todo and Order Entry are the first adopters, followed by Order Pool, Inventory, and Fulfillment without changing business state machines or API contracts.

## 2026-07-11 - Mobile Operational Tables Show Key Columns

At the office mobile breakpoint, wide operational tables show a page-specific key-column subset for scanning instead of compressing every desktop column into unreadable cells. Order Pool keeps order, customer, product, status, and exception; Inventory keeps size, color, style, state, available quantity, and trust; Fulfillment keeps method, customer, goods, quantity, and status. The selected record's full fields and actions remain available in the detail pane below, and business filtering is unchanged.

## 2026-07-11 - Print Device Commands Use Stable Identity And Idempotent QA Writes

Print-device maintenance, driver-mode changes, and printer field-QA recording belong in a dedicated command service rather than the HTTP composition root. When an operator omits a device ID, readable ASCII names may form the identifier; names that contain no usable ASCII slug must use a stable digest suffix and must never produce an empty identifier such as `PRN--`. Printer field-QA writes use the request idempotency key plus print-device and field-test-record resource locks. The authenticated operator is authoritative over any operator value supplied in the request body, while device and print-job ownership must be validated before evidence is accepted.

## 2026-07-11 - Print Batch Records Use Authenticated Identity And Replay-Safe Persistence

Print-batch creation belongs in a dedicated command service, and the authenticated operator ID and display name override body-supplied identity. A missing batch ID is derived deterministically from a valid idempotency key; non-production compatibility calls without a key use a collision-resistant generated ID. Invalid relative client timestamps are not persisted as business time and fall back to the server ISO timestamp. PostgreSQL creation uses the shared idempotency executor with a print-batch resource lock, and an idempotent replay must return the original operation log without projecting a newly generated, unsaved log into workspace memory.

## 2026-07-11 - Todo Handling Persists State, Full Events, And Audit Atomically

Todo handling must not be represented only by an in-process workspace projection. In production, one idempotent transaction updates the canonical `todos` row, appends a `todo_events` record containing the complete post-action projection, and writes the operation log under a todo resource lock and stale-snapshot guard. The authenticated operator is authoritative; body-supplied identity is compatibility input only. Startup reads merge the latest event payload with canonical row fields so notification copy, print-result packages, reminders, and other extended details survive restart without weakening the relational status fields. This decision covers handling actions; business workflows that create or automatically resolve todos must still persist them inside their owning transaction.
